# Soulvail — Progress

**The log.** Where we are right now, and what each merged task actually did. Updated **in the same PR** as the implementation — never in a separate "update docs" commit.

The map is [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md). The specs are in [tasks/](tasks/).

**Closed milestones are archived, not deleted:** [M0](archive/PROGRESS-M0.md) · [M1](archive/PROGRESS-M1.md) · [M2](archive/PROGRESS-M2.md). The log below covers the milestone in progress only. This file used to be 381 KB and could not be opened whole by any tool; keeping it to one milestone is what stops that happening again — **and M2 is the first milestone archived on time**, at its own acceptance rather than a milestone later.

---

## Current State

_Updated: 2026-09-18 (M3-14a)_

| | |
|---|---|
| **Milestone** | **M0 — Walking skeleton: complete, tagged `m0`. M1 — Combat feel: complete (21/21). M2 — Stage loop: complete (27/27), acceptance passed on Editor evidence.** `m1` and `m2` are both the owner's to tag. Now **M3 — Levelling and the tree** (30/34): **its specs are complete and the code has started.** Twenty-nine specs were written before a line of the milestone's code; M3-01a was the first line of it, M3-01b was the first thing in the milestone a player would keep, M3-05 was the first thing that could change a number at all, M3-02a was the first shape a designer could author against, M3-02b was the first task after which a designer could sit down and author one, M3-03 was the first thing in the game that can take a node and change a number by it, M3-04 was the first that can decide which nodes a player is asked to choose between — which closed the M3-00a group and the whole of the tree's core side — M3-06 is the first thing in the game that acts without the player, which opened the M3-00b group — **M3-07a is the first player input that reaches a skill**, and the first task in the milestone that gives the player a decision to make about what the game does for them — **M3-07b is the first task in the project to run a migration *chain* rather than a step**, and the first that makes a decision the player made about their own hands survive the app dying — and **M3-08a is the first task that spends a pick, and the first that can stop the game**. The count moved 23 → 31 → 33 → **34** as M3-00c split all four of its titles, M3-00d split two of three and added one the owner asked for, and **M3-11a split itself at build time under its own pre-declared tripwire**; the tables are the authority and the Milestones row agrees with them. **M3-08a is the first task that spends a pick and the first that can stop the game; M3-08b is the first a player chooses *with*, and the two together close the level-up loop.** **M3-09a is the first task in the milestone a player can reach in the build that exists** — every screen before it needs a tree, and a pause icon needs nothing. **M3-09b is the first screen in the game raised *over* a pause it does not own**, and the first that asks the player a question core would otherwise refuse with a throw — and **M3-09c is the first task in the project to write down something the player keeps *about themselves* rather than a setting they chose**, and the first to run a step of the profile migration chain — and **M3-09d is the first screen in the game with two doors into it**, which is the first time a screen has had to know which way it was opened in order to know where Back goes, and the task that finishes the M3-09 group and the pause panel with it — and **M3-10a is the first task in the milestone to put a command back in the player’s hand**, the first since M1-16 to add one at all, and the first to give `CommandPhase` a second poller since M1-09 built the shape — and **M3-10b is the first task in the milestone whose feature every player of the current build can see working**, every screen before it needing a tree, a pick or an Active that no shipped class has: a kill moves the strip and a threshold moves the number, which is the first time the levelling this milestone has been building since M3-01a is visible anywhere but the debug overlay. It is also the task that closes the M3-10 pair and the HUD's progression half with it. — and **M3-11a-i is the first task in the milestone whose whole subject is a data structure, and the first in the project ever split by a spec’s own tripwire rather than by a file count discovered late**: M3-11a wrote down the condition under which it must become two tasks, the condition was met on the first day, and the two halves were named before a line was written. — and **M3-11a-ii is the first active in the game and the first thing in the project whose effect has to be *undone***, which is what makes it the first production caller of `EffectRegistry.Remove` in a live run: the door M3-05 opened, specified and left unused for six tasks. — and **M3-11b is the first effect in the game whose result is a *place* rather than a number on the player**, and the first task to call in the promise `TimedEffects` was built to keep: a new timed primitive costing one file and one `Register` line, with nothing in the clock, the registry or `RunSession.Tick` changing to admit it. — and **M3-11c is the first task in the group whose subject is what the player *sees*** — the first to draw anything either of the M3-11 skills does, and the task that **closes ledger row 5**: `PlayerAnimatorView` has driven the player’s body since M2-art with no test on it, and it now has fourteen, written without changing a line of what it does, which is how the row asked to be closed. — and **M3-12a is the first task that makes the damage-touching half of the tree reachable at all**, and the first in nine whose feature the owner can see without waiting on M3-12c: `PlayerStat` went from six members to **eleven**, and a designer can author `WeaponConeAngle +50 %` from the Inspector today. **It moves no number in any run this build plays**, which is the distinction [ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) turns on. — and **M3-12b is the first task in the milestone whose file count started at the *ceiling* rather than the floor**, so the ruling it owed was not *“is this one task”* but *“does any edit make it six”*; it is also the first effect in the game to address a piece of **content** rather than a number, and the first to find that a rule written for a rare case — a modifier held for a skill the player does not own — is in fact the path **every resumed run** takes. — and **M3-12c is the first task in nine whose feature a player of this build can reach, and the one [ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) has been waiting for since M2-15**: every primitive and every address existed and **not one node was authored**, so `Data/` held no tree at all and six consecutive tasks had shipped features nothing could reach. Twenty-six assets later the row **closes on its test** at 3 / 4 / 5 hits — measured against `DepthScaling` rather than against the spec's table, which it corrects — and `TryGetTreeFor(character.oathbound)` answers **true for the first time in the project's life**. It is also **the first task in the milestone to be told by its own spec that seven of its twelve identifiers could not be constructed**, so the ruling it owed came before the content rather than after it. **M3-13a is the first task in the milestone whose subject is a *rule* rather than a feature, and the first whose whole deliverable is that a number stops existing in more than one place** — no player of any build can see anything it did, which is the point: it is a no-visible-change PR by construction, and what makes the change real is a reflection row that can fail. It is also **the first task in the project to declare its size against the ROADMAP's criterion out loud rather than against its file count**, at sixteen files and size S. **M3-14a is the first task in the milestone whose deliverable is that the game can be *read*** — every screen before it drew its own `LocKey`, and it is also the first to find that a rule in `GameDesign.md` cannot be met by the asset it describes. **M3-14b is next.** |
| **Last merged task** | **M3-14a** — **`ILocalizer`, `TableLocalizer`, and one English table: the port ADR-0012 has been waiting three milestones for, and the first build in this project's life whose screens are readable.** One port, one `ScriptableObject`, one adapter and **63 rows** — registered eagerly in `BootScope` as `ILocalizer` only, because a table is content read at boot and a duplicate key should fail at launch naming the asset rather than on whichever screen asked for a word first. **Six readers stop drawing keys**: the offer card, the Skills row *and the trigger line it composes*, the tree view's twelve cells and three branch headings, the first-active hint, the Overflow toast, and the 60 dp slot button. **The spec's own key spelling is wrong and the failure mode is silent by design** — rule 7 says `.desc` and the twelve assets say `.description`, kebab-case, so a table typed to the spec ships **twenty-four dead rows and an entirely green suite**, because rule 1 makes a miss return the key. The rows were therefore **generated by reading each asset through `SerializedObject`** rather than typed, and probed: 24 of 24 resolve while `skill.oathbound.consecrate.desc` answers `Has=false`. **Coverage is deliberately unasserted — M3-14b owns it**, and asserting it here would be this task grading its own homework. **Rule 8 counts four raw strings and there are five**: `Continue` arrived with M3-07b's resume flow and neither rule 8 nor the ROADMAP's parking-lot line counts it; it is a key too, and the ROADMAP's count is the owner's to correct. **GD §13.1's one-line rule turns out to be unsatisfiable at the shipped card, and that is a finding about the card**: the description label is **176 dp at 18 pt, about twenty characters a line**, so **ten of twelve descriptions wrap to two lines and one to three** — three rows were shortened first and the count did not move. Its box is 176 × 150 dp, room for eight lines, so two lines is not a layout failure; what survives is reading time, and the two worst were both Actives, which must state a condition *and* an outcome. **Five code files against a ruling of four**, and the fifth is named rather than absorbed: a shared `DictionaryLocalizer` fake, forced by **ten** broken fixtures rather than the six predicted — five is size M's ceiling so the pre-declared split did not fire, but the ruling was four. **The finding the spec does not have: eleven static labels on four prefabs draw unresolved keys and nine still do**, so the tree view now shows twelve English nodes under a Close button reading `ui.tree.close`; all eleven have rows, so wiring each is one line. **Ledger row 9 closes for six readers and stays open for the seventh.** |
| **Task before that** | **M3-13b** — **Health that reads at a glance: the damage tint, the Elite's bar, and the shield you were granted.** **All four rows of GD §16.2's table**, and the first task in the milestone whose whole subject is whether a player can *read* the fight rather than whether the fight is correct. A basic enemy darkens toward `Palette.EnemyDying` `#3B2422` as its hit points fall and earns a 28 × 3 dp bar on each hit that fades 2.25 s later; an Elite's is up from the rental and never leaves; and the player's HP bar finally draws what Bulwark granted them, as a segment ahead of the fill in absorption order. **The tint lives inside `EnemyHitFeedback` as a third term rather than a fourth writer** — one scalar composed with the archetype colour, so `SetColour` is still the only thing in the project that writes `_baseColor`, and the pre-declared tripwire did not fire. **Proved by swapping it**: the rejected arrangement reddens exactly one row, and the spec expected two — `Tint_ArchetypeLookRebasesIt` is green under both, because the rental assigns outright either way. **GD §16.2 and GD §16.4 disagree about this and §16.4 won**: a dying Husk drawn in the telegraph colour would be the worst readability bug the project could ship, so the body darkens toward red without ever arriving at `#FF4A1F` — and *that* is measured, not asserted. The 0.15 band had to be read as **not all three channels**, because the **Bloater ships 0.051 from `Danger`'s green and 0.098 from its blue before any tint is applied**; red is the entire margin, worst case 0.361 over three archetypes × 21 steps. **Elite-ness rides `EnemySpawned` as a defaulted field**, which keeps the PR to three assemblies and takes PlayMode out of the blast radius — `EnemyViews` holds a look book and no catalog, and M7-02's Elites are made at spawn rather than authored. **Rule 9's *“oriented once”* rested on a false premise** (`EnemyView.Face` yaws the root every tick) and was kept in intent rather than letter. **And a red row found a real design bug**: clamping the shield segment collapsed it to nothing at full health, which is exactly when Bulwark grants one, so the width is preserved and the start gives way instead. **Ledger row 6 stays closed and the price held at one field and one row.** |
| **And the task before that** | **M3-13a** — **`Palette`: GD §16.4 in one file, and the nine readers that have been waiting for it — the first task in the milestone whose subject is a rule rather than a feature, and a no-visible-change PR by construction.** Fifteen `static readonly Color`s: GD §16.4's five reserved meanings at the hex the document names, CH §4's four kind tints, M3-09d's three node states, four derived readouts, and `IsDanger`, which is how *“for nothing else, ever”* stops being a sentence. **Nine readers lose their copy** — `ThreatArrows` loses both statics **with no forwarder**, `TelegraphRings` stops reaching across the `Views`/`Presentation` seam, and seven views lose **seventeen serialized `Color` fields** between them. **It does not split, and the ruling is on the ROADMAP's own criterion rather than on the sixteen-file review**: two new code files and **zero substantial rewrites**, every production edit one field deleted and one expression repointed — and **every seam is disqualifying**, because any cut ships `Palette` with eleven of fifteen members unread and leaves `Views_CarryNoSerializedColour` unwritable until the second half, closing [ledger row 6](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) on the *second* PR whichever way the files fell. **The finding the spec does not have is the drift row 6 exists to catch, arrived at before the palette: there are two player cyans in the build.** `ReticleView`, `FocusGlowView` and `PlayerView._swingColour` each carry a serialized cyan nothing had counted — so the honest count is **twelve files, not nine** — and theirs is **`#4CE6FF`** against the palette's `#22D3EE`, measured off `Player.prefab` and `Reticle.prefab` rather than off the initialisers. **Out of scope on a ruling**: moving them recolours the reticle, the glow and the swing arc on every frame, which manual step 2 forbids. `Views_CarryNoSerializedColour` is scoped to the nine **by name** and asserts the three are not in it; `Palette_IsNotTheOnlyCyanInTheBuild` pins all three; the question is a [parking-lot](ROADMAP.md#parking-lot) line because no task owns it. **`HeldFocus` is `Player` byte for byte and the spec's *“dimmer”* was about the wrong thing** — what reads dimmer is `ThreatArrows.Place` ramping alpha by distance, so the comment was corrected and the value was not, and **no *“every member is distinct”* row was written** because `Player`, `Heal` and `HeldFocus` are all `#22D3EE` on purpose. **`EnemyLook.BoneGrey` stays content**: it is `M_BoneGrey`'s number written down twice on purpose, so `EnemyLook.cs` was not edited and `Neutral_AgreesWithTheEnemyDefault` pins the agreement instead. **The AR row is §7, not §18.3** — §18.3 is *“Numbers and identity”* and this is a rule about what may be **written**, so it sits beside the ban it is the exception to. **Three predictions were wrong on the facts and were measured rather than repeated. The ripple is nothing**: `Run.unity` holds both components only as **stripped** references carrying no field data, and Unity 6.3 **keeps** a removed field's YAML line — `ForceReserializeAssets` on `VFX_ConsecrateZone.prefab` gave a **zero-byte diff** with `_colour:` still on line 107 — so **no prefab and no scene moved** and **manual step 1 still passes**, checked by a `SerializedObject` walk that reports no colour property on any of the seven. **Two more test files break than anyone counted** (`ThreatArrowsTests`, `TelegraphRingsTests`), and **the rewritten rows are five rather than four**. **The white initialisers were read from the YAML**, which is what rule 5's argument depends on: both were `Color.white` in C# and both prefabs were dressed `#22D3EE`, so `Palette.Heal` and the Bulwark shell are that and not white. **Four values moved and nothing else did**, each under half of one 255th and each onto the byte it already had — the five reserved carry GD §16.4's exact hex, the derived sets keep the floats the build shipped, and `IsDanger` compares at eight bits so **both spellings of `#FF4A1F` answer true**. **Two assemblies, no third.** |
| **And the one before that** | **M3-12c** — **The Oathbound tree v1: twelve nodes, and the number [ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) has been waiting for since M2-15 — the first content a player can actually be offered, and the first task in nine whose feature is reachable in the build that exists.** Twenty-six assets: twelve `SkillDefinition` nodes in three branches of two tiers of two, thirteen `EffectDefinition`s behind them (thirteen, because Unbowed carries two), and `OathboundTree.asset`. **`BootScope.prefab`'s two arrays stop being empty for the first time since M3-02b shipped them that way**, which makes `TryGetTreeFor(character.oathbound)` answer **true for the first time in the project's life** and retires M3-03 rule 10's null-tree branch from every real build. **Four rulings were made before a single asset was authored, and the first one changed the content.** `ContentId`'s grammar is `^[a-z0-9]+(\.[a-z0-9_-]+)+$`, so **seven of the spec's own twelve ids throw** — `temperedVow`, `steadyBreath`, `keenCenser`, `longReach`, `broadCensure`, `crashingCensure`, `lastingGround`. All seven ship **kebab-case**; the other five were always legal. **`LocKey` forbids only whitespace, so the twenty-four keys were legal either way and were deliberately not touched** — the asymmetry is the trap. What it does to CLAUDE.md's asset-naming rule is turn a **match** into a **mapping**: `TemperedVow.asset` ↔ `skill.oathbound.tempered-vow`, hyphen-to-PascalCase, which is the spelling M3-02b predicted M3-14b would need. **`AssetDatabase.CreateAsset` was unprobed and is now probed** — it works from a `RunCommand`, produces a real `MonoScript` link, and the plural `DeleteAssets` recovers a mistake, so nothing was hand-written as YAML and no `m_Script` GUID was computed by hand (Traps §4). **The finding the spec does not have, and it is invisible to every check the project owns: Bulwark shipped with the wrong trigger on the first pass.** `TriggerField.IncomingProjectiles` is **5**, not 3 — 3 is `EnemiesWithin8m` — and the wrong ordinal compiles, converts, and is a **legal Active that fires at the wrong moment for ever**: a shield that arrives because something is near rather than because a bolt is in the air, the exact opposite of CC §6.4. It was caught by **printing the converted spec from a probe**, which is the content check the protocol asks for, and it would have survived a green compile and a green suite. **[Ledger row 1] CLOSES on the test and is gated on the playtest.** **3 / 4 / 5 hits at stages 1 / 15 / 30**, with **8** for the unlevelled stage-30 control — and the arithmetic was computed from `DepthScaling` rather than copied, which **corrects the row's own table**: the Husk is **66.24 and 98.64** hit points, not 66 and 99. The hits land the same, so the table was right for a reason it did not state. **The condition M3-12b attached is met in the test's own text**, not only in the spec's: `Ttk_ExcludesSurvivabilityAndReach` names all six exclusions in code, asserts none of them can address `WeaponDamage`, then applies the four that are applicable and shows neither the damage nor the count moves — discriminating rather than declarative. **It does not invert.** **The warning the row demanded is recorded: it holds at stage 30 because of Overflow, not the tree** — 29 uncapped levels against twelve nodes — so M7-04's twenty-seven owes a re-measure. **[Row 9] becomes concrete and does not close**: twenty-four node keys and three branch keys ship, and the tree view now draws twenty-seven strings on one screen. **One deviation outside the Files table** — the boot count is asserted in `SkillAuthoringTests`, not `InstallerTests`, and it was **inverted rather than deleted**. **Two assemblies, no third. `BootScope.prefab` moved; `Run.unity` and `Hud.prefab` did not.** |
| **And the one before those** | **M3-12b** — **Two primitives that are not a stat: a skill's own cooldown, and a swing that shoves — the first effect in the game that addresses a piece of *content* rather than a number, and the first that switches on a rule the swing did not have.** `ModifySkillCooldown` names a **skill**, which is why it cannot be a `PlayerStat` (one member per stat, and the set of skills is content where the set of stats is code); it resolves live through `SkillRunner.TryIndexOf` → `CooldownOf` onto the runner's **own** `Stat`, so M3-06 rule 12's promised pair arrived as *a file rather than a refactor*, exactly as that task predicted. `KnockbackOnSwing` lifts a new base-0 `PlayerCombat.SwingKnockback` off zero, after which `ResolveConeHits` emits one `EnemyKnockbackIntent` per enemy the swing reached — **the machinery is the Charge's, unchanged since M1-15**, which is `EnemyKnockbackIntent`'s own remark coming true: *“the day something else pushes an enemy it reuses this rather than growing a parallel one.”* **Five new files and no sixth**, which is size M's *ceiling* rather than M3-12a's floor: the Files table is five new code files before a single edit, so the three candidate edits — `PlayerCombat`, `SkillRunner`, `RunSession` — were each ruled additive out loud **before** writing, and the pre-named split into **M3-12b-i / M3-12b-ii was not taken**. **Rule 3 had no mechanism and one was built:** `SkillRunner.Add` publishes nothing, so it gains an `internal` `WatchArrivals(Action<ContentId, Stat>)` invoked **last** — after the fresh `Stat` and after `_count++` — with the pending table on the handler where the spec puts it; the flush-at-the-seam alternative would have dragged `LevelUpFlow` in as a fourth production edit, and a domain event is banned because core does not listen to its own. **The ordering is measured, not argued**: moved above the `new Stat` line it reddens **exactly four** rows and leaves the other sixteen in that fixture green. **The finding the spec does not have: rule 3's pending path is not an edge case — it is the path every resumed run takes.** `RunSession.Start` replays the whole tree through `SkillTree.Restore` and only *then* feeds the runner, so a cooldown node is applied to an **empty** runner on every `Continue`, whatever order it was taken in; the Passive-carrying-one case rule 3 argues from is the rarer one. **Ruled: the shove is the swing's facing, because rule 7 contradicts its own test row** — the prose says *“what `ResolveChargeHits` already does”* (one direction for everything a sweep catches) while `Swing_ShovesAwayFromThePlayer` asks for the radial answer the same rule rejects two sentences later. Renamed `Swing_ShovesTheWayTheSwingFaced` and made discriminating: **as written it would have gone green against either implementation.** The facing is a **field written on the damage frame beside `PendingConeRequestId`**, never a parameter. **`SwingKnockback` is a second base-0 `Stat` and it is pinned rather than left to rule 6's argument** — one modifier reading 1.5 on a base of zero is reachable only with `Flat`, which is behavioural proof that the handler picks it. **It moves no number in any run this build plays**, and [ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) **moves** rather than only gaining reach: both primitives raise effective DPS **without raising damage per hit**, so **M3-12c's TTK test must state the path it measures and exclude these two**, or it passes while the tree does nothing. **Four assemblies, no fifth; no prefab and no scene moved.** Manual steps 1–3 all need hand-authored assets — `Data/` still holds no tree at all — but step 1's *“first time a node has visibly changed a number on a screen”* is a claim about M3-09b's shipped row, and `Session_RegistersBothHandlers` already asserts the 12 s → 9 s that screen would print. |
| **And the one before that again** | **M3-12a** — **Five numbers a node can reach: the stats M3-05 deferred — the first task that makes the damage-touching half of the tree reachable at all, and the first in nine whose feature the owner can actually see.** `PlayerStat` is **eleven members**, not six: `Weapon.Range` and `Weapon.ConeAngleDeg` stopped being forwarded floats, `ChargeSkill.Damage` was promoted off the spec at the line `ResolveChargeHits` has flagged since M1-15, `Health.ShieldRechargeDelay` became the one reachable Aegis number, and **`PlayerCombat.HealPerKill` was added outright at base 0**, paid through `EnemySystem.PendingKills` / `DrainKills()` — `DrainXp`'s shape exactly, banked on the same line and drained one line below it in `RunSession.Tick`. **No new production file**, and four counted: `Weapon`, `PlayerCombat` and the two test files, which is size M's floor and agrees with the spec's own header. **It moves no number in any run this build plays** — every base is what the assets say, every new stat ships with zero modifiers, nothing authors a node — and that is [ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) gaining reach rather than closing: **M3-12c still owes the TTK test at stage 1 / 15 / 30.** **The finding the spec does not have, and it is measured rather than argued:** `Stat` computes `(Base + ΣFlat) × (1 + ΣPercentAdd) × Π(1 + PercentMult)`, so **`HealPerKill`'s base of 0 makes every percentage kind multiply into zero and `ModifierKind.Flat` is the only kind that can ever move it** — a probe read +500 % as **0** and +2 Flat as **2**. **M3-12c's Retribution must be authored Flat or it heals nothing, silently.** The base stays 0 (a non-zero one would make every class in the game heal on kill) and the constraint is written into `HealPerKill`'s own remarks, `PlayerStat.HealPerKill`'s, and two test rows. **The spec's `Cone_ClampsAnAbsurdAngle` gave NaN the *more* permissive answer and now gets the less:** 400 → 360, −10 → **0**, non-finite → 0, because answering NaN with 360 draws the widest wedge in the game for a number nobody can read. Rule 7's `(0, 360]` becomes `[0, 360]`, and **the refusal is a zero-angle intent rather than a suppressed one** — the swing is already announced by then, and suppressing it would strand `PendingConeRequestId` on a report that can never arrive. **Two corrections came from running rather than reading.** `Stat.Base` and `Modifier` both **throw** on a non-finite input, so a NaN cannot reach the swing through either ordinary door and the clamp is a backstop against overflow inside the stack; the row now asserts both refusals and a sibling reaches the same branch by a `PercentMult` below −1, which ADR-0008 permits. And **the spec's `Cone_ClampsANegativeRange` is unobservable as written** — the in-range test and the intent read the same clamped reach, so a negative range stops the swing rather than throwing one that reaches nothing; shipped as two rows, with the intent's 0 reached by driving the reach negative *mid-swing*. **There was no sixth reader**: `Weapon.Range`/`ConeAngleDeg` had exactly the three the owner named, and `ConeOverlapQuery` reads floats off the intent. **Fourteen files, two assemblies, no third; no prefab and no scene moved.** **Manual step 2 is runnable today** — `ModifyStatDefinition` serializes `PlayerStat` as an enum dropdown, so `WeaponConeAngle +50 %` is authorable the moment this compiles, and a node, a tree and a `BootScope._trees` drop are all Inspector work. |
| **The spec group before those** | **M3-00d** — **specs for M3-13…M3-15, and the last spec group in the milestone.** **Five specs where the ROADMAP had three titles:** [M3-13a](tasks/M3-13a-palette.md) `Palette` and the nine readers ledger row 6 had been collecting · [M3-13b](tasks/M3-13b-health-bar-treatment.md) GD §16.2's whole table — the damage tint, the Elite's persistent bar, and Bulwark's shield finally drawn on the player's · [M3-14a](tasks/M3-14a-localizer-and-english-table.md) `ILocalizer`, `TableLocalizer`, one English table · [M3-14b](tasks/M3-14b-content-validation.md) the sweeps over every shipped asset · [M3-15](tasks/M3-15-acceptance-and-tag.md) the checklist, the ledger, the tag. **Build order is ID order**, after M3-12c. **Three owner rulings:** the boss bar stays with **M4-04**, which has owned it since M4's titles were written, behind M4-01's phases; **a minimal English table is pulled forward** rather than accepting M3 on keys, so GD §13.1's two-second rule can be judged in this milestone and M6-10 becomes the *rest* of localisation; and **`m3` is tagged on Editor evidence** like `m0`–`m2`, with **multi-touch named apart** as the one device row that risks a shipped feature rather than a feel verdict. **One ruling M3-00d made itself from a shipped precedent:** `Palette` is a static class of `readonly Color`, because `ThreatArrows.Danger` has been exactly that since M2-12a and AR §7 bans static *mutable* state — recorded as AR §18.3's one sanctioned static. **Two findings that moved specs:** GD §16.2 and §16.4 **contradict each other** about a dying enemy's colour, and **`ILocalizer` has never existed as a file** despite three specs calling it an AR §6 port. **Ledger rows 1, 4, 6, 8 and 9 all moved; every M3 row now names a task whose Files table exists**, for the first time in this ledger's life. |
| **And the spec groups before those** | **M3-00c** — specs for M3-09…M3-12, the screens and the first nodes: **twelve specs where the ROADMAP had four titles, because every one of the four split** — the pause gate, the Skills list, profile v2 and the one-time hint, the tree view, S1–S4, the progression HUD, Bulwark, Consecrate, both views and **ledger row 5**, five addressable stats, two primitives, and the twelve nodes with their TTK test. Five owner rulings; one M3-00c made itself (drag-to-reorder is not in V1). **Rows 1, 2, 5, 6 and 9 all moved and none closed**; row 1 gained the table that closes it on paper at 3 / 4 / 5 hits. **M3-00b** — specs for M3-06…M3-08, casting and choosing: the `SkillRunner` and its 40 % floor, Auto/Manual and the four slots, save format v3, the level-up flow and its screen. Three owner rulings — Auto/Manual survives a kill, pause **gates the tick** rather than clocking it at zero, two pending picks are one screen that re-draws. **Ledger row 9 opened.** **M3-00a** — specs for M3-01…M3-05, the tree as data. Seven specs where the ROADMAP had five titles, build order 01a → 01b → 05 → 02a → 02b → 03 → 04. Two owner rulings the group hangs on: **the tree is layered** (two nodes a tier × four + a keystone, 27 a class — which M3-02a has now built and pinned) and **CH §5.2 ships as authored**, its depth divergence pinned by a test M3-01a wrote. **Ledger row 7 closed with a hook**: a staged `ProjectSettings/` change needs `ALLOW_PROJECT_SETTINGS=1`. Full entries for all three are in the Log. |
| **Next task** | **M3-14b** — **Content validation: the sweeps over every shipped asset.** Size **M**, depending on **M3-14a** and **M3-12c**. **The task that turns M3-14a's table from a file somebody wrote into a guarantee.** Rule 1 makes a missing row invisible — a key with no row draws as itself, which is indistinguishable on screen from a wrong key — so *“every key in every asset has a row”* is a claim only a sweep can make, and M3-14a deliberately did not make it. It also owes [ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)'s last structural half, the tree that cannot **starve**, and the file-name-to-id mapping M3-12c's kebab rename made necessary. **It inherits a live finding**: nine authored keys on four prefabs that nothing resolves, which its sweep will see and which no row in `English.asset` is missing. |
| **What works** | **A run is a descent, and it survives the app closing.** Boot → Menu → `Descend` (or `Continue`) → a run that arrives in an arena, seals it, spends a threat budget on telegraphed waves, opens a door when they are dead, and puts the player one stage deeper with the budget, curves and scaling that go with it. **Core owns every outcome**; Unity reports facts and senses and renders events. Three archetypes fight differently — the Husk closes, the Spitter kites to 14 m and will not shoot through a pillar, the Bloater walks in and detonates — and all three are authored data on one shared body, told apart by tint and scale. Arenas change at a boundary, drawn from the mode's roster by seed and stage. Everything the player cannot see has an indicator: screen-edge arrows for off-frame threats, a dimmer marker for a focus held out of range, rings for spawns and blasts. A run writes itself to disk at its opening frame and at every boundary, and deletes itself on death. **And as of M3-01a a kill is worth something:** experience is banked on the death, drained on the tick between the death check and the director, and crossing a threshold publishes a level and banks a pick — visible on the debug overlay as `lvl 3 0.42 owed 2`. **As of M3-01b the level is in the file**: the boundary write carries level, absolute XP and banked picks, a resumed run comes back at the level it had before `RunStarted` is published, and **a `run.json` written by the M2 build still loads** — it migrates to v2 and means what v1 meant, level 1 and nothing earned. **And as of M3-03 a run has a live tree**: given a class with one, it knows which nodes may be taken, taking one puts its effects on the player's stats through the registry, a resumed run gets its picks back *before* its hit points are clamped against the maximum those picks moved, and the boundary write carries them in take order. **As of M3-04 the game can also say which three of those a player would be shown** — a seeded, weighted, no-replacement draw from the available set, one draw per pick, from the `Offers` stream and no other, so the same seed against the same tree state always gives the same offer in the same order. Nothing calls it yet. **And as of M3-06 the game can act without the player being asked**: an owned Active has a live cooldown with CH §4.1's 40 % floor under it, an authored condition read off the `CombatBlackboard` every tick, and a cast that applies its effects and publishes `SkillCast` — at most one a tick, in take order, allocating nothing. The runner ticks immediately after combat and **above** the projectile step, so a trigger reading `IncomingProjectiles` sees the bolts still in the air rather than the ones that just landed. Nothing owns an Active in any shipped build, so the overlay reads `actives 0` — which is the runner correctly empty and saying so. The Charge obeys the same floor, invisibly, until M3-12 gives it something to obey. **And as of M3-07a the player can take that decision back:** every owned Active carries CC §6.1's switch, Auto by default, and a skill moved to Manual stops auto-casting *and* stops having its condition evaluated at all — it takes one of four thumb slots, keeps whatever cooldown it was in the middle of, and fires only when `CastSkill(slot)` arrives. The fifth is refused loudly so M3-09's screen has to ask CC §6.2's question instead of silently swapping. Nothing owns an Active and no screen sends either command, so the overlay reads `slots 0/4` beside `actives 0` — both correctly empty and saying so. **And as of M3-08a a pick can finally be spent, and the game can stop:** a run that is owed a pick and has a tree to spend it on opens a level-up **on the frame after the tick that earned it**, draws up to three nodes from the `Offers` stream, and stops being ticked at all until something chooses — `Time.timeScale` 0 and 30 fps with it, so animations and telegraphs freeze with the simulation rather than carrying on over it. Choosing takes the node, spends the pick, hands a new Active straight to the runner, and re-draws for the next pick over the **new** tree state. A pick with nothing left to offer is **Overflow** instead: +2 % damage and +2 % max HP per level, pooled additively under one source, granted silently and announced rather than shown. **A gated frame costs zero simulated seconds**, so `RunState.Time` is now play time and a stopwatch is play + screens. Nothing in the build ships a tree, so every run still banks its levels and nothing pauses — which is rule 5 working, not a gap. **And as of M3-09a the run can be stopped on purpose, which is the first thing this milestone has built that a player of the *current* build can actually reach** — every screen before it needs a tree and nothing in `Data/Trees` ships yet. A small icon in the top right, inside the safe area, stops the fight dead: enemies frozen mid-step, no telegraph filling, no animation, the panel up on the same call with no fade because `Time.timeScale` is already 0. Resume puts the fight back exactly where it stood. Quit ends the session and goes to the Menu **without deleting the run** — `Continue` is still offered, and resumes from the last boundary. The icon goes dead whenever anything else holds the pause and disappears for good on death, so a pause menu can never open over a level-up or over a death overlay. **The panel now has a Skills button on it, and it opens CC §6.3's screen over the top** (M3-09b): every active the run owns, one row each, with its name, what its wait actually is in seconds after the 40 % floor, its auto-cast condition written out as a key and a formatted threshold, and an Auto/Manual switch — twelve pooled rows in a `ScrollRect`, because a landscape phone holds about five and the runner's ceiling is twelve. Flipping a switch sends `SetAutoCast` and the row redraws when the event comes back; asking for a fifth manual skill shows CC §6.2's *“which one goes back to auto?”* over the four current occupants and sends **nothing** until the player answers, then sends two ordinary commands in order. **The screen holds no pause of its own** — the pause menu underneath still holds the only one, and closing the Skills screen gives that panel back rather than resuming the run. **And as of M3-09c the game remembers one thing about the player across installs** (CC §6.3): the first time they ever own an Active, the level-up screen closes and a small callout appears under the pause icon telling them skills can be set to Manual. It fades on its own after four seconds or on a tap anywhere, takes no touches from the fight while it is up, and is **spent the moment it is shown** — killed mid-callout, the app does not show it again. A Passive never triggers it, a second Active never triggers it, and a run resumed with actives already taken never triggers it. **The flag lives in `profile.json`, which is now v2**, and a profile written by the M2 build still loads — it migrates to v2, keeps the haptics setting the player chose, and reports the hint unseen, which is true. **Toggling haptics no longer rewrites the whole profile**: there is one holder of it and one writer, and every feature moves one field and hands it back. **And as of M3-09d the pause panel has its fourth button and the tree can be looked at from two places** (CH §5.1): Pause → View Tree, or View Tree on the level-up screen itself, and either way the whole tree comes up — three columns, a row per tier, every node's name and description, a stripe for which of CH §4's four kinds it is, and a frame saying whether it is owned, takeable on the next pick, or neither. **It is read-only by construction**: a cell is not a button, the class cannot reach a command port, and looking at the tree in the middle of a level-up costs nothing — the cards are hidden rather than destroyed, the game stays stopped, and Close gives back the same three, still tappable, with the pick still owed. Close returns to whichever door you came in by. **Both buttons disappear entirely for a class with no tree**, which is every class in this build. **And as of M3-10a a skill set to Manual can finally be fired** (CC §6.2): a button appears beside the Charge for each occupied thumb slot — 60 dp, on a 140 dp arc sweeping from the Charge’s left round to directly above it — with a radial fill that empties on the cast and refills over the cooldown. **An unused slot is not drawn at all**, so a player with no manual skills sees exactly one button and one with two sees three, and a hole in the middle stays a hole rather than the others sliding along. A cooling button is at 40 % opacity **and takes no touches**, which is the one place the Charge deliberately differs — it stays tappable while cooling so CC §5’s 0.15 s buffer keeps working. The press does not become a command where the thumb landed: the button writes a slot into a one-frame buffer and `RunTicker` turns it into `CastSkill` at the same point in the frame every other command lands, above the snapshot the tick is answered against. **And as of M3-10b the levelling this milestone has been building since M3-01a is finally on screen** (GD §16.1): a thin strip along the very top edge creeps right with every kill and resets when a level is crossed, and the level itself sits as a number at the end of the top-left row, beside the Aegis ring. Both are live for every player of the current build, which makes this the first M3 feature that needs no tree, no pick and no Active to be seen — everything the milestone shipped before it is either unreachable or reachable and empty. **Under the health bar there is now a row of small radial fills, one for every skill the game is casting on the player's behalf**, which is the only thing in the game that makes the default build legible: every skill starts on Auto, so without it a player who never opens a menu has every active firing itself with nothing on screen to say so. A skill switched to Manual leaves that row and gains a thumb button; switched back it does the reverse, and the two readouts can never show the same cooldown twice. **And an Overflow level is no longer completely silent**: when a pick has nothing left to buy, a small toast reads *“Overflow ×N”* for two seconds over the running fight — the running total rather than the increment, because by stage 30 that total is more than half the power the player has gained, and it counts in unscaled seconds so a pause or a level-up landing on top of it does not leave it hanging. It takes no touches in any state. **And as of M3-11a-i anything that can be hurt has a third pool of shield** — points a *cast* puts on, spent before the Aegis and before hit points, stacking per source rather than per call, and handed back for exactly what they have left rather than for what they were granted. The Aegis is untouched by it: its 30 points, its 4 s delay, its 15/s refill and the ring that draws them all mean exactly what they meant before. **Nothing in the game can put a grant on a player yet** — the pool ships wired to nothing, which is M3-04’s and M3-05’s bargain again — so every run reads `shield 0.00` on the debug overlay beside `actives 0`, which is the pool being correctly empty and saying so. **And as of M3-11a-ii something can fill it.** A cast effect can grant those points and a clock takes them back: `TimedEffects` holds up to sixteen `(effect, source, deadline)` triples sorted by expiry, ticks one line after the skill runner and above the projectile step, and hands each due pair back to the handler that applied it through `EffectRegistry.Remove` — which is the first time in the project that door has been walked through in a live run. `GrantShield` is the primitive, `GrantShieldDefinition` is its Inspector half, and `ShieldGranted` and `ShieldGrantExpired` say what happened, carrying totals so a view needs no read. The deadline is **simulated** seconds, so a level-up screen at `timeScale` 0 does not drain a shield. A second cast from the same node **refreshes** both the points and the deadline rather than queuing a second of either. **What still cannot happen is a cast**: no tree in this build holds Bulwark, because `Bulwark.asset` is M3-12b’s — so the machinery is complete, tested end to end over a real `RunSession` with a Spitter putting bolts in the air, and reachable by nothing a player can do. **And as of M3-11b a cast can put something on the *floor*.** A skill can place a patch of healing ground where the player is standing — 3.5 m across, six seconds long, three hit points every half-second to whoever is inside it when a pulse lands, which is about a quarter of a 140-point bar for holding still through half a wave. **It stays where it was cast**: walking out stops the healing and the zone keeps burning down where it was left, which is the whole decision the skill is made of. Containment is flat — height is a camera's business — in and out between pulses costs nothing, and a recast puts down a **second** zone rather than refreshing the first, which is the deliberate opposite of what a granted shield does. Up to eight may stand at once, they pulse on absolute times so a 30 fps phone and a 120 fps one land the same twelve, and a pulse due on the second a zone ends **lands before the zone does** — so the authored skill is worth 36 hit points rather than 33. The heal is `Health.Heal`'s own, which has promised this task since M2 that it will not reset the Aegis delay, and each pulse announces **what was actually restored**, which is zero at full health and honest about it. A stage boundary leaves a zone standing; only the end of a run drops one, silently. **What still cannot happen is a cast**: no tree in this build holds Consecrate, because `Consecrate.asset` is M3-12c's. Composition is `BootScope` → `RunScope`/`MenuScope`, **no statics anywhere**. **Task-by-task detail is in the [M0](archive/PROGRESS-M0.md), [M1](archive/PROGRESS-M1.md) and [M2](archive/PROGRESS-M2.md) archives** — this row is the shape, not the history. **As of M3-11c both M3-11 skills have a body.** A zone core places puts a cyan disc on the floor at the position, radius and duration the event carries; it **flashes only on a pulse that actually restored hit points**, so a player standing in their own Consecrate at full health sees steady ground rather than twelve lies; core’s `ZoneExpired` retires it and the decal’s own countdown is the net underneath. A granted shield raises a shell on the player that fades in over 0.15 s and comes down **only when the total reaches zero**, so two overlapping grants never leave the player looking unprotected while one is still running. The character now has a **cast pose** — one clip for every Active there will ever be. All of it is pooled, stepped on the snapshot’s clamped `Dt` beside the bolts and the rings, and **reachable by nothing in this build**, because no tree holds either skill until M3-12c. **And as of M3-12a a node has somewhere to land.** Eleven player numbers are addressable by an authored effect rather than six: a weapon's reach and its cone width, the Charge's damage, the Aegis's recharge delay and a brand-new heal-per-kill, each a live `Stat` sampled at the moment it is used — so a modifier taken mid-stage widens the **very next** swing rather than the one after it. `ModifyStatDefinition` already lists all eleven in its Inspector dropdown, so a hand-authored node moving any of them is three assets and an Inspector drop, with no code. **And as of M3-12b a node can aim at something that is not a number.** A `ModifySkillCooldown` names a **skill** and lands on that skill's own live cooldown — 12 s becomes 9 s and the skill beside it is untouched — with CH §4.1's 40 % floor still owned by `CooldownRules`, so no stack of nodes can drive an 8 s Active below 3.2 s. **It works whichever order the player takes things in**: a node taken before its skill is held and spent the moment the skill arrives, which is not a rare case but the path **every resumed run** takes, because a `Continue` replays the whole tree before the first Active reaches the runner. And a `KnockbackOnSwing` makes the basic attack **shove** — one intent per enemy the swing reached, all swept the way the swing was facing, through the same machinery the Charge has used since M1-15. Both are authorable from the Inspector today, and neither is authored anywhere: `SwingKnockback` is 0 and no tree carries either. **And as of M3-12c the Oathbound has a tree, and everything the milestone built is reachable for the first time.** Twelve nodes in three branches of two tiers of two — Oath, Censure and Judgment — authored as twenty-six assets a designer can open in the Inspector, and dropped into `BootScope`'s two lists, which have been empty since M3-02b. **A run now pauses on a level, shows three real cards drawn from the six tier-1 nodes, and taking one changes a number the player can feel**: +15 maximum hit points, a quarter off the Aegis's recharge delay, +15 % Censer damage, a metre and a half more reach, a cone half again as wide, a swing that shoves, twelve per cent more swings a second, two hit points back per kill. Taking one node in a branch opens both of that branch's tier-2 nodes, so the offer stays a real draw rather than the same three heads every time. **Both of M3-11's skills are finally castable**: Consecrate is a tier-1 Judgment node that drops healing ground when the player falls below 60 % health, and Bulwark is a tier-2 Oath node that raises a 35-point shield for five seconds when a bolt is in the air — and Lasting Ground, the tree's one Upgrade, takes Consecrate's twelve-second cooldown to nine. The tree fills at level thirteen, about stage nine, after which every level is Overflow. **Measured rather than hoped: a Husk dies in three swings at stage 1, four at stage 15 and five at stage 30** — inside GD §12.4's band at all three depths, against the eight it took at stage 30 before any of this existed. What every one of those nodes displays is still its `LocKey`. **And as of M3-13a every colour in the game comes from one file.** GD §16.4's five reserved meanings live in `Palette` at the hex the design document names, and the nine files that each held their own copy now hold a name each: the health bar and its blocked flash, the threat arrows and the held focus, the telegraph rings, the white hit flash, CH §4's four kind tints on all three screens that draw them, the tree's three node frames, the healing ground and the Bulwark shell. **A player sees nothing new, and that is the deliverable** — every value landed on the byte it already had — but a Keystone is now the same colour on the card that offered it, the tree that holds it and the auto-cast row, because there is only one value left to be the same. **And `#FF4A1F` is a rule the suite can fail rather than a sentence**: `IsDanger` is reflected over every member and exactly one answers true. **And as of M3-13b a fight can be *read* rather than only won.** An enemy darkens visibly as it dies — toward a dark maroon, never toward the telegraph's red-orange, which is GD §16.4 winning an argument against GD §16.2 in the one place it mattered — and a thin bar appears over whatever is being hit and is gone two seconds later, so the arena is not twenty-eight permanent readouts. An Elite's bar never leaves, which is the whole of M7-02's treatment shipped three milestones before the content that triggers it. And the player's health bar finally shows the shield Bulwark puts on them: a bright segment on the front of the bar that shrinks as a Spitter's bolt is absorbed and vanishes when the grant lapses, while the Aegis ring beside it does not move — which is M3-11a's *“deliberately not the Aegis”* made visible for the first time, and the distinction M3-11a-ii named and nothing could draw. **And as of M3-14a a `LocKey` finally becomes a word, which is the first time in this project's life that a player could read any screen in it.** One English table of 63 rows is loaded at boot and every screen the milestone built now draws from it: a card reads *“Consecrate — healing ground when your health drops”* rather than `skill.oathbound.consecrate.name`, the tree view draws twelve named nodes under **Oath**, **Censure** and **Judgment**, a Skills row reads *“Player HP below 60 %”*, the first-active callout finally says the thing it exists to say, the toast reads *“Overflow ×14”*, and a thumb button says **Bulwark**. The Menu says **Soulvail**, **Descend** and **Continue**, and the death overlay **You died** / **Tap to return** — none of which is typed into a scene or a prefab any more, which is AR §11.5 becoming a thing a test asserts rather than an aspiration. **A missing row is not a blank but the key itself**, so the one failure this can have diagnoses itself on the screen it happens on. |
| **What does not work yet** | **The player levels up and gets nothing for it — and as of M3-06 every piece of the payout exists except the one that shows it to them and the content to pay it with.** M3-01a made a kill worth experience and a level a pick the player is *owed*; M3-01b made all three survive an app kill; M3-05 built the thing that could pay it out and deliberately wired it to nothing; M3-02a built the shape a reward is authored in; M3-02b built the Inspector half and deliberately authored nothing (rule 7); M3-03 built the object that takes a node; M3-04 built the object that decides which nodes to offer; **M3-06 built the thing that would fire the reward once it was taken, and it owns nothing — every catalog this build ships answers `TryGetTreeFor` with false, so `RunState.Tree` is null in all of them, its three reads answer 0, false and empty, and `OwnedActiveCount` is 0 in every run that has ever been played.** The runner is therefore a live per-frame path that walks zero entries, which is exactly what M3-12 turns on. ~~**M3-07a built the Manual half and there is still no button** (M3-10) and no screen to switch on (M3-09), so everything the runner owns would auto-cast in practice — the switch exists, is tested, and has no way for a player to reach it.~~ **M3-09b built the screen and M3-10a built the buttons, so the whole of CC §6.1’s switch is now reachable end to end — and no run in this build can reach it either**, because a slot can only hold an Active and no class ships a tree until M3-12. What every player of the current build sees is **four empty slots and therefore one button**, the Charge, which is rule 5 working rather than a gap. **What they will see the moment M3-12 authors an Active is `skill.oathbound.consecrate.name` inside a 60 dp circle** — [ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)’s fifth reader and the one whose closure is a device question rather than a table one: M3-14a’s English *plausibly* fits an auto-sizing 8–18 pt label and nobody has looked at it on a phone, which is why rule 9 named an icon as the fallback in advance. **And the cluster itself has never been reached for**: 140 dp of radius is the tightest CC §6.2’s 60 dp buttons and 12 dp gaps permit over a quarter turn, so S4 sits about 236 dp above the safe area’s bottom edge on a screen roughly 432 dp tall — whether that is a thumb’s reach, and **whether Android delivers a thumb on the stick and a thumb on S3 at the same time at all**, are both [ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3), and the second is the one row in the milestone that risks a feature rather than a verdict. **And a loadout set in the Editor does not survive an app kill** (M3-07b): the four slots and the flags live for the run and die with it, which is a known, named gap rather than an omission. **As of M3-08a something finally spends `PendingLevelUps` — and no run in this build can reach it**, because no class ships a tree (M3-12) and `IsLevelUpPending` is false without one, so every level is still banked exactly as it was. The flow, the port, the Overflow arithmetic and the gate are all live, tested and unreachable in play: the first build in which a player sees any of it is M3-12's content plus M3-08b's screen. **The lazy draw shipped and is proved from both ends** — neither `Start` nor any `Tick` draws, only `OpenLevelUp` does, and two whole compositions from one saved file draw the identical three cards, which is the free-reroll-by-killing-the-app hole closed and the guarantee stated as the player would feel it. ~~**There is still no screen** (M3-08b), so a hand-authored tree pauses the game with no way out but Stop Play.~~ **The screen shipped at M3-08b and the way out exists** — three cards, a tap, `ChooseOffer`, and the frame starts again — **but nothing in this build can still reach it**, because `IsLevelUpPending` needs a tree and `Data/Trees` is empty until M3-12. So the honest state is that the whole level-up loop is now built, tested end to end over a real `RunSession`, and **unreachable in play**: M3-12's content is the single remaining thing between a player and the first card they ever choose. What a player would see on that card today is `skill.oathbound.consecrate` rather than "Consecrate" — ADR-0012 working as designed, [ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3), and M3-14a's job. So [ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) is exactly where it was: a basic enemy takes 3 hits at stage 1 and 8 at stage 30, and nothing in the game closes that yet. **M3-09a and M3-09b are the two tasks in the milestone so far whose feature a player can reach today** — a pause icon needs no tree, and neither does the screen behind it — **and what the Skills screen shows every run in the current build is one line saying the player owns no actives**, which is the honest answer until M3-11 and M3-12 author one and is exactly why it is a line rather than an empty panel. The panel is now Resume, Skills, View Tree and Quit, and **M3-09d is the task that closed that sentence** — every button on it now has a screen behind it, and M8-03's Settings is the next thing that would change the count. **What no player of this build will see is the tree itself**: `TryGetTreeFor` answers false for the Oathbound, so both View Tree buttons take themselves off the panel and off the level-up screen, which is rule 2 working rather than a gap. What they will show the moment M3-12 authors a tree is **twenty-seven unresolved `LocKey`s on one screen** — [ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) at its worst by a long way, against a card's three for two seconds, and the reason M3-15 should rule the whole of that row rather than the cards alone. **And whether twenty-seven cells fit a landscape safe area at a readable size is unknown and unknowable here** ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)): rule 9 bets that a fixed grid with no scroll and no zoom is enough at M3-12's twelve and survives to twenty-seven, cells shrink to fit so the honest failure mode is *small* rather than *clipped*, and the spec names this as the row most likely to send the task back. **M3-09c is the third feature a player can reach and the first one no player of this build will ever see fire**: the callout needs a first Active, and a first Active needs a tree, so the whole of it — the arming, the dwell, the tap, the flag — is live, tested over a real `RunSession`, and unreachable until M3-12 authors one. What a player *would* read when it fires is `ui.hint.firstActive`, which is [ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) at its sharpest so far: a callout whose entire job is to tell them something, telling them a key. **And the callout has never been looked at on a phone**, which is the question it actually turns on — whether a corner box is noticed at all in the seconds after a level-up, when the player is re-orienting to a fight that just restarted; if it is not, the honest answer is a longer dwell rather than a modal ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)). **A trigger line is the sharper end of ledger row 9 and it is visible now rather than at M3-15**: a row that should read *“Player HP below 60 %”* reads `trigger.hpFraction.below 60 %` — the key resolves at M6-10 into a format string with one placeholder and the number slots into it, so the *composition* is settled and only the words are missing. The three labels on the pause panel read `ui.pause.resume`, `ui.pause.skills` and `ui.pause.quit` rather than English — ADR-0012 working as designed, [ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3), and M3-14a's job — which now applies to a screen a player of this build will actually open. **And nothing has ever paused on a phone**: whether a 44 dp target in the top-right corner is reachable mid-fight with the left thumb planted on the stick, whether *instantly* survives the 30 fps drop, and whether a 56 dp row with a switch at one end is comfortably tappable, are all [ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3). **And as of M3-10b two of the four progression readouts are built and cannot be reached, while the other two work for everybody.** The strip and the level number are live in every run today. The auto-cast row needs an Active and the Overflow toast needs a full tree, so both need M3-12: **every run in this build draws twelve deactivated cells and never toasts**, which is the row and the toast working rather than a gap. What a player will see the moment a tree ships an Active is **a 24 dp circle with no icon and no text in it** — [ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)'s seventh reader and **the one M3-14a's English table cannot fix**, because the cell holds neither `skill.oathbound.consecrate` nor *“Consecrate”*; there is no space at all, so which skill a cell is showing is not communicated in any form until art exists. That is a different problem from the 60 dp slot button beside it, where a word plausibly fits and the open question is whether it is legible on a phone. **And neither of the two numbers this task ships has ever been looked at on a device** ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)): whether a 4 dp strip on the very top edge is visible at all beside a landscape cutout — it is inside the safe area, so a notch insets it rather than covering it, which is the safer reading and still a guess — and whether a 24 dp radial sweep reads as a cooldown or as a dot, which matters more here than anywhere else because the sweep is the only thing distinguishing one cell from another. Both numbers are serialized fields for exactly this reason. **The toast's key is the one unresolved `LocKey` in the build that barely costs anything**: *“Overflow ×14”* is mostly the number, and the number resolves fine. **As of M3-11a-ii the shield pool has something that can fill it and still nothing that does.** The handler exists, is registered in every run’s `EffectRegistry`, and grants and expires correctly over a live `RunSession` in tests — but **no authored node carries a `GrantShield`**, because `Bulwark.asset` is M3-12b’s, so `PlayerGrantedShield` still answers 0 in every run that has ever been played and the debug line still reads `shield 0.00`. **This is the sixth consecutive task whose feature needs M3-12 to be playable at all**, and it is the largest one yet: an entire active, its clock, its authoring half and its two events, reachable by nothing a player can do. **And there is still no view.** A granted shield is a number on the debug overlay and nothing else — no ring, no countdown, no health-bar segment — so even with a tree authored, the question the design actually asks (does a shield that appears *before* the bolt read as the skill working, or as nothing happening?) cannot be answered until **M3-11c** draws it and a **phone** shows it. **Two gaps are named rather than left to be found.** A hit absorbed *entirely* by granted shield publishes `PlayerDamaged(0, 0, unchanged, unchanged, blocked: false)` — indistinguishable from a hit that did nothing — because `DamageResult.ToShield` means the *Aegis* and CH §3.1’s Martyr keystone sums that field; widening it is **M3-13b**’s call, and until then no view can tell the two apart. And **a resumed run comes back with no granted shield**, which is a decision rather than an omission ([ledger row 2](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)): a grant is derived from a cast that is gone, unlike a cooldown, which M3-07b rebuilt from `TakenNodeIds` because the *node* survives. **As of M3-11b the same sentence is true of Consecrate, and this is the seventh consecutive task whose feature needs M3-12.** The zones, the pulses, the trigger and the three events all work over a live `RunSession` — a run resumed at 70 of 140 casts, stands still and comes out at 106 — and **no authored node carries a `SpawnHealZone`**, so `RunState.ActiveZoneCount` answers 0 in every run that has ever been played. **And there is still no view, which is sharper here than it was for Bulwark.** A zone is a position and a radius in core and nothing on screen: no decal, no ring, no flash on a pulse — so a player would stand in an invisible circle, and the question the design actually asks (**is standing still for six seconds survivable at stage 15 at all?**) cannot be asked until **M3-11c** draws it and a **phone** runs it. If the answer is no, the skill is a trap and the fix is a bigger radius rather than a bigger heal ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)). **Two limits are named rather than left to be found:** a ninth simultaneous zone **throws** rather than being dropped, which is unreachable at Consecrate's 12 s cooldown and 6 s duration but is a run-ending refusal if a later zone skill is ever authored faster than it expires; and **a live zone is not saved** — like a cooldown (M3-07b), it is seconds of a fight the player is not in the middle of any more, so a resumed run comes back with bare floor. There is **no boss** (M4), **no Sanctum** (M6), and **one class**. Stage 5 is not special and no code pretends it is. **M3-11c removes the "and there is still no view" half of both paragraphs above and leaves the other half exactly where it was.** Bulwark now draws a shell and Consecrate now draws ground, so the two feel questions this row has carried since M3-11a-ii are **askable for the first time** — *does a shield that appears before the bolt read as the skill working?* and *is standing still for six seconds survivable at stage 15 at all?* — but neither can be **answered** here: both need a tree that holds the skill (**M3-12c**, the **eighth** consecutive task whose feature waits on it) and then a **phone** ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)). **One thing the views cannot say, and it is inherited rather than new**: a hit absorbed entirely by a granted shield publishes `PlayerDamaged(0, 0, unchanged, unchanged, blocked: false)`, and `PlayerAnimatorView` treats it exactly as it treats a blocked hit — no flinch — which is right for the body and still leaves **M3-13b** owing the distinction to everything else. **And a killing hit flinches**, one frame before the death clip takes the body; that is what the code has always done, it is now pinned by a test, and whether it is what GD §16.3 wants is a question for a playtest rather than a bug. There is **no icon** for either skill (M7), **no readout** of how much granted shield is left (M3-13, GD §16.2), and **no `Palette`** — both new colours are serialized `Color` fields named as placeholders, which is [ledger row 6](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) at four real readers of eight. **And the five numbers M3-12a made reachable are still, in every run this build plays, exactly what the assets say.** Nothing authors a node that moves one: there is no `SkillTreeDefinition` asset in `Data/` at all, so the offer screen has nothing to offer and the tree view nothing to draw. `HealPerKill` in particular ships at **base 0**, which means the kill-drain line in `RunSession.Tick` is dead code until **M3-12c**'s Retribution — and it must be authored **Flat**, because a percentage of zero is zero. **M3-12b adds the last two primitives and changes none of that.** `SwingKnockback` ships at base 0, so **every swing in this build still shoves nobody**; no `ModifySkillCooldown` exists in any asset, so every cooldown on the Skills screen is exactly what a designer typed. **The whole of M3-12a and M3-12b is reachable only through assets nobody has made** — and that is now the single thing standing between this build and a milestone whose feature a player can see. **M3-12c is the only task left before that changes**, and it owes two things beyond the twelve nodes: Retribution authored `Flat`, and a TTK test that **states** which path it measures and excludes the cooldown and the knockback, because both raise effective DPS without raising damage per hit. **M3-12c ends the sentence this row has repeated for nine tasks, and replaces it with a different one.** There is a tree in `Data/`, `BootScope` ships it, and every feature listed above as *built and unreachable* is reachable: the level-up screen, the tree view, both View Tree buttons, the first-active hint, the Skills screen's rows, the thumb buttons, the auto-cast strip and the Overflow toast all have content to work on. **What is wrong now is that every word of it is a key.** A card reads `skill.oathbound.tempered-vow.name`, the tree view draws **twenty-seven unresolved strings on one screen** — twelve names, twelve descriptions and three branch headings — and the first-active hint tells the player `ui.hint.firstActive`. That is [ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) at full size and **M3-14a's job**; **M3-14b** then has twenty-six real assets to sweep, including the file-name-to-id mapping the kebab rename made necessary. **One reader no table can fix is unchanged**: M3-10b's 24 dp auto-cast cell holds neither a key nor a word, so which skill a cell is showing is communicated in no form at all until art exists (M7). **And nothing here has been played.** The 3 / 4 / 5 hits are arithmetic against a stationary Husk; whether stage 15 *feels* like four hits with three Spitters shooting, whether standing still in a Consecrate for six seconds is survivable at all, and whether a shield that appears before the bolt reads as the skill working are all questions a phone answers and a test cannot ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)). **Ledger row 1 closes on its test and not on the playtest** — M3-15 still owes the played counts — and the reason it closes at stage 30 is **Overflow rather than the tree**, twenty-nine uncapped levels against twelve nodes, which M7-04's full twenty-seven will shift back. **There is still no `Palette`** (M3-13a), **no boss** (M4), **no Sanctum** (M6) and **one class**. **M3-13a removes the *“there is still no `Palette`”* clause and puts one finding in its place: there are two player cyans in the build, and only one of them is GD §16.4's.** `ReticleView`, `FocusGlowView` and `PlayerView._swingColour` are `#4CE6FF` where the health bar, the threat arrows, the healing ground and the Bulwark shell are `#22D3EE` — dressed that way on `Player.prefab` and `Reticle.prefab` since M0/M1, and nobody had ever counted them. **They are deliberately unchanged**, because unifying them is a visible recolour of three world-space views rather than the relocation M3-13a was, and which cyan the player's own views should be is a look decision nobody has made. It is a [parking-lot](ROADMAP.md#parking-lot) line, pinned by a test so it cannot drift further in silence, and **M7's art pass replaces all three surfaces anyway**. **M3-13b closes the *“a hit absorbed entirely by a granted shield is indistinguishable from a hit that did nothing”* clause — the bar now says so — and adds two of its own.** **Nothing in the build is an Elite**, so the persistent-bar treatment is tested and unseen until M7-02 authors an affix; the rows are real and the content is three milestones out. **And the damage tint's entire claim is unverified**: GD §16.2 says it *“reads instantly in peripheral vision — which is where most enemies are”*, and peripheral legibility is not something an Editor, a capture or a test can answer — what *is* measured is only that the colour never approaches `#FF4A1F` ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3), which gains it and three more). One finding a playtest should be pointed at: a Husk at full health is drawn in `Palette.Neutral` `#6E6A63`, and that is also the bar's fill colour, so a Husk's bar is its own body colour floating above it — correct by GD §16.4 and possibly wrong to look at. **Nothing else about this row changed**: nothing has been played, there is still **no boss** (M4), **no Sanctum** (M6) and **one class**. **M3-14a removes *“every word of it is a key”* and replaces it with a narrower, stranger sentence: nine of them still are.** Eleven static labels are authored as keys directly on four prefabs — `ui.pause.resume`/`.skills`/`.tree`/`.quit`, `ui.skills.close`/`.cancel`/`.slotsFull`, `ui.levelup.tree`, `ui.tree.close` — and only the two whose presenters already held a label field (`ui.skills.empty`, `ui.tree.none`) were wired, because the other nine each need a new serialized field and a prefab re-dress outside M3-14a's Files table. **They all have rows in `English.asset`**, so each is one line of wiring, but until someone writes those lines **the tree view shows twelve English nodes under a Close button reading `ui.tree.close`** and the pause panel still reads `ui.pause.resume`. **And GD §13.1's one-line rule is now known to be unsatisfiable, which is a finding about the card rather than about the tree**: the description label is 176 dp at 18 pt — about twenty characters a line — so ten of the twelve descriptions wrap to two lines and one to three, and shortening three of them moved the count by nothing. **Whether they *read* in under two seconds on a phone is untouched by any of this** ([ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)), and so is whether twelve tree cells holding English still fit a landscape safe area — M3-09d rule 9 bet that they do and it bet it while the strings were keys. **The 24 dp auto-cast cell is unchanged and unfixable by any table**: it holds neither a key nor a word, so which skill a cell shows is communicated in no form until art exists (M7). **Nothing here has been played**, there is still **no boss** (M4), **no Sanctum** (M6) and **one class**. |
| **Owner retune, after M2-03** | **Five numbers, playtested and changed by the owner in the assets, not by a task.** Oathbound move speed **5.4 → 3**, Charge **10 m / 0.22 s / 5 m knockback → 8 m / 0.5 s / 4 m**, Husk move speed **3.5 → 2**. The whole game got slower and **the speed ratio barely moved** — 5.4/3.5 was 1.543×, 3/2 is 1.5× — so GD §6.1's *"every class must feel faster than almost every enemy"* survives, and escaping a Husk telegraph still works because the Husk plants itself during its windup (1.2 m gained against a 0.6 m cancel band). **Two derived numbers moved a long way and neither is authored anywhere:** the dash is 45 m/s → **16 m/s**, 8.4× move speed → 5.3×, and its invulnerable window is 0.27 s → **0.55 s**, now longer than a Husk's whole 0.4 s windup. CC §2.5, §5 and §7 were updated with the assets. **The class speed band was deliberately not touched** — see Known issues. |
