| **Reference device** | **None, and the emulator is not a fallback.** BlueStacks 5 (Android 9 / SDK 28, x86_64) **installs the APK and crashes on launch** in its own Vulkan driver via `libhoudini.so` — an emulator-bridge fault on a development-build Vulkan path, not game code. The APK is ARM64-only and always will be: Unity 6.3 cannot build x86-64 for Android. **Nothing outside the Editor has ever run.** [M0-20a](tasks/M0-20a-apk-runs-on-bluestacks.md) owns it; a phone removes the question. Unity Device Simulator still serves for layout. |
| **Deferred — device-only** | **Now [M3 ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3), because the debt spans three milestones and needs an owner rather than a list. Ruled at M3-00d: `m3` is accepted on Editor evidence like `m0`–`m2`, with the grain in the tag message — and with *one row named apart from every other.* M3-10a's multi-touch is the first device row that risks a **feature** rather than a verdict:** if Android does not deliver a thumb on the stick and a thumb on S3 at the same time, **manual casting does not work at all**, and no Editor probe can say so. Everything else on this list risks a number or an impression. **M3-10a turned that row from a sentence into four objects on a prefab, and added two questions beside it.** The cluster ships at a **140 dp radius**, which is forced rather than chosen — CC §6.2's 60 dp buttons and 12 dp gaps allow no two centres closer than 72 dp, and four over a quarter turn need at least 139 — so S4 sits about **236 dp above the safe area's bottom edge** on a landscape screen roughly 432 dp tall. **Whether a thumb reaches it without the hand leaving the stick, and whether a 60 dp target is hittable in an arc without looking, are both unanswerable here.** The four offsets and the anchor margin are serialized `Vector2`s so the whole arc moves in one session rather than one build. **M3's own additions:** a 4 dp XP strip beside a notch, a 24 dp radial fill, twelve tree cells holding **English** rather than keys (M3-14a moved that from *does it fit* to *does it read*), a 3 dp enemy bar, whether GD §16.2's damage tint works in peripheral vision — which is its entire claim for existing — and whether `timeScale` 0 at 30 fps recovers any thermal headroom at all. Multi-touch; landscape flip; touch latency; real frame rate; thermal; sustained fps at the concurrency cap; **the Profiler's no-per-frame-`GC.Alloc` check, carried unmet since M1-21**; whether the M0/M1/M2 feel verdicts survive leaving the Editor. **M1-20's haptics are the largest single block** — above all whether the 100 ms coalescing window reads as *late*. **M2's pacing knobs:** whether 2 s of arrival and 1.5 s of clear read as beats or as waiting; whether 0.3 s of fade covers a room swap; whether a pillar *reads* as cover at a phone's size (a Spitter holding fire looks identical to one that has not noticed you, and that difference is the whole mechanic); whether eleven overlapping additive rings read as separate countdowns or one smear. **M2-15a adds a cost rather than a feel question:** what `Physics.SyncTransforms()` costs per frame at the concurrency cap, which is a flush of the frame's dirty transforms and has only ever run on a desktop. If it turns out to matter, the narrower fix is to flush only when a cone or sweep is actually pending. **And the one row that is a *correctness* question rather than a feel one:** kill the app from recents mid-stage, relaunch, tap `Continue`. **Stopping Play is not a kill** — it disposes the scopes in order and lets every `finally` run — so M2-13b's atomic write has never been tested against the thing it exists for. |
| **Known issues** | **The `FrameOrderTests` intermittency is back, and it is the owner's first call this milestone.** M2-15a fixed a *real* bug — `Physics.SyncTransforms()` at the one seam, three consecutive 11/11 as its evidence — and the tell that it was real was that the *ordering* assertions never failed, only the one checking physics agreed with them. **At M3-01a the same row failed on two of four full PlayMode runs** (10 / 11 / 11 / 10), with M2-15a's exact message — *"The cone found nothing where the body had just walked to"* — and **passes when run on its own**. **It is not M3-01a**, and the reason is checkable rather than a hunch: `FrameOrderTests` drives a `RecordingCore` fake `IRunSession`, so the one line this task added to `RunSession.Tick` never executes in it, and `RunTicker` was not touched. So either M2-15a fixed one of two causes, or something about running the suite whole (the fixture order, the shared Editor process) is the remaining variable — the isolation pass is the evidence for the second. **M3-01b saw 11/11 then 10/11; M3-05 and M3-02a each ran it once and saw 11/11. M3-02b did not run PlayMode at all** — the honest record there is a gap rather than a green run. **M3-03 was the first task since M3-01b whose change could plausibly have moved this row, and it did not: 10/11 then 11/11, with the fixture passing 4/4 run on its own.** The failing row is `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` with M2-15a's exact message, and **the reason it cannot be M3-03's is checkable rather than a hunch** — `FrameOrderTests` drives a `RecordingCore : IRunSession` whose `State` property returns `null` outright (`FrameOrderTests.cs:564`), so not one line this task added executes in it, and `RunTicker` was not touched. **M3-04 ran it twice and saw 11/11 both times, and that is recorded as a tally rather than as evidence** — it touches no `Tick`, no view, no scene and nothing in production at all, so it could not have moved this row in either direction, and two green runs on an intermittent failure say nothing. **M3-06 is the first task since M3-03 whose change could plausibly have moved this row, and it moved it — not by causing it, but by killing the only hypothesis anyone had.** The full run was **10/11**, failing `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` with M2-15a's exact message. **It is not M3-06's, and that is checkable rather than a hunch**, by the same route M3-03 used: `FrameOrderTests` drives a `RecordingCore : IRunSession` whose `State` property returns `null` outright (`FrameOrderTests.cs:564`), so `State.Skills.Tick` — the one line this task added to `RunSession.Tick` — would `NullReferenceException` if it ever ran there, and it never does, because that fixture never calls `RunSession.Tick` at all. **What is new is the isolation pass.** Run on its own the fixture came back **3/4, failing a *different* row** — `Ticker_ReportsFactsAfterBodiesMoved`, not the one the full run failed — and then **4/4** on an immediate re-run with nothing changed. **That retires the suite-whole hypothesis**, which was the only one standing: for two tasks the record said *green alone, red inside a full run*, and it is now red alone as well, on a row the full run had passed. So the variable is neither fixture order nor the shared process; it is something inside a single PlayMode run that moves between rows. **Both failing rows are the physics half rather than the ordering half** — M2-15a's tell, which held then and holds now: the assertions about *step order* have never failed, only the ones checking that the physics scene agrees with them. A green run says nothing about an intermittent failure, and both are recorded here so the tally stays honest rather than as evidence of anything — **three consecutive green runs across three tasks is what M2-15a used as proof once, and it would be the wrong reading here**, because these are single runs a task apart on a suite whose failures cluster within a run. **M3-07a ran it once and saw 11/11, and that is a tally rather than evidence** — the row did not fire. It is the first task since M3-06 whose change is *inside* `SkillRunner.Tick`, and it still could not have moved this row in either direction, by the same checkable route: `FrameOrderTests` drives a `RecordingCore : IRunSession` whose `State` returns `null` outright (`FrameOrderTests.cs:564`), so the `IsAuto` skip never executes there. It is also the first task to **edit `FrameOrderTests.cs` itself** since the intermittency was recorded — two `IPlayerCommands` stubs that growing the port made mandatory, and no row in that fixture calls either. **One green run on an intermittent failure says nothing**, which is this row's own standing warning. **M3-08a is the first task to put a number on it, because the owner called for a baseline before a line was written — and the baseline immediately paid for itself.** `FrameOrderTests` was run **alone, 50 times, on untouched `dev` (`0dd95b2`), before any edit**: **5 failing runs — 10 %** — every one of them `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` with M2-15a's exact message, and **`Ticker_ReportsFactsAfterBodiesMoved` never failed once in 50**, though M3-06's single isolation pass had seen it fail. Failures fell on runs 1, 3, 12, 17 and 43, denser in the first twenty (4/20) than the last thirty (1/30) — recorded as an observation, not a conclusion, but a cold-start component is now a lead a diagnosis task could chase. **Before this, every data point in this row's life was n = 1 or n = 2**, which is why three green runs across three tasks once read as proof. **What the baseline then caught is the part that matters.** Post-edit the same 50-run measurement came back at **30 %** — and the new failures were **M3-08a's own `Frame_TheLevellingTickCompletes`** at 7/50, because it asserted `ConeReport`, hanging a second row on the exact intermittent physics sweep. Rewritten to assert that the fact *phase was reached* rather than what the sweep found, the four new rows are **0 failures in 100 isolated runs** across two further passes, and `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` is back inside its own noise (5/50 then 10/50, against the 10 % baseline; pooled 15/100). **Without the "before" number none of that would have been visible** — a fixture that failed one morning in three would have looked like the old intermittency getting worse, and the row would have absorbed a new cause under an old name. **Both limbs of the alibi are now gone, as predicted**: this task edits `RunTicker` structurally, and `RecordingCore` answers the level-up phase through `IProgressionCommands` rather than through the `State` that returns null — so *"the new code cannot execute in that fixture"* is no longer available to any future task, and the baseline is what replaces it. **Nothing in the game is known to be wrong**; what is wrong is that one row fails about one run in ten, and the next step is still instrumenting the flush rather than re-running the suite. **A diagnosis task now has a "before" number to be measured against, and this is the moment it is cheapest to name.** **M3-08b is the first task whose full-suite number actually moved, and it is recorded as a change rather than as noise.** PlayMode came back **14/15 on both runs** — `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder`, M2-15a's exact message, both times — against M3-08a's **15/15**. At the measured 10 % baseline, 2-for-2 is about a 1 % coincidence, so it is not waved through. **Run in isolation the fixture was 8/8 green on three runs**, which is the *"green alone, red inside a full run"* signature the row carried for two tasks before M3-06's isolation pass retired it — so the fixture itself is not what changed. **What is new, and is a lead rather than a diagnosis: this is the first task since M3-02b to change `Scenes/Run.unity`**, and `BootSmokeTests` loads that scene in the same PlayMode process *before* `FrameOrderTests` runs. One more full-screen `Canvas`, a `GraphicRaycaster`, three `Button`s and five TMP labels changing timing or GC in a shared process is exactly the *"something inside a single PlayMode run that moves between rows"* variable this row has never been able to name — and it is now a hypothesis with a cheap experiment attached (run `BootSmokeTests` and `FrameOrderTests` together, against `FrameOrderTests` alone). **`BootSmokeTests`' own three rows passed both times**, so the dressed scene composes and a run starts with the new presenter registered. **The measurement stopped at n = 2 full and n = 3 isolated, and the reason is a new Traps §7 row**: a `TestRunnerApi` left in a local is collected mid-run and takes its callbacks with it, so the suite finishes, every test executes, and `RunFinished` never fires — **three runs were lost to it** before it was understood. `hideFlags = HideAndDontSave` plus a static root fixes it, and the fix is what produced the second consecutive 1 447. **Nothing in the game is known to be wrong; what is known is that the number moved and why it might have.** **M3-09a is the second consecutive task to change `Scenes/Run.unity`, which is the hypothesis M3-08b named, and its number is 14/15 then 15/15** — `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder`, M2-15a's exact message, once out of two. **That is squarely inside the 10 % baseline and it neither confirms nor retires the lead**: one red-then-green pair on an intermittent failure says nothing on its own, which is this row's own standing warning, and it is recorded as a tally. What it does say is that the number did not get *worse* on a task that added a second full-screen `Canvas`, a second `GraphicRaycaster` and three more `Button`s to the scene `BootSmokeTests` loads first — so if one dressed canvas were enough to move it, two should have moved it further, and did not. **The cheap experiment M3-08b attached is still unrun**, and is still the next step rather than another full suite. **The flush's own price is still unmeasured** on a phone and joins the device rows below. **Two environmental failures were seen at M3-09a and neither is game code.** The first is new and is now [Traps §7](../Traps.md#7-tests-nunit-and-measuring-allocation): `SaveWriterTests.Writer_ObservesEveryFault` subscribes to the **process-wide** `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException` and forces two GCs, so *any* unobserved faulted task finalised inside that window fails it — one EditMode run in three came back **1 464 / 1** carrying a `ClientWebSocket.ConnectAsyncCore` fault from **the Unity MCP's own bridge**, with the two runs either side green on unchanged code. It is a real property of the row rather than a flake to wave through: the row cannot tell a `SaveWriter` fault from anyone else's, and the fix if it recurs is to filter by the exception the fixture armed. The second is the **`TimeManager.asset`** re-serialisation, now seen at M3-08a and again here, and it is not a `timeScale` leak (`m_TimeScale` correctly 1) — found and reverted both times, and the standing advice is to check that file before every handover. **It has now been found and reverted on five of the last six tasks** (M3-08a, M3-09a, M3-09b, M3-09c, M3-09d), which is frequent enough that it is no longer worth calling a surprise: it is Unity 6.3 rewriting `Fixed Timestep` into its rational form whenever the Editor touches the settings, the pre-commit hook refuses a staged `ProjectSettings/` change without `ALLOW_PROJECT_SETTINGS=1`, and the check belongs in the handover list rather than in this row. **M3-09d ran no PlayMode**, so it adds no tally to the intermittency above and leaves no `InitTestScene` behind — and it is the third consecutive task to change `Scenes/Run.unity`, which is M3-08b's still-unrun lead, now with a third data point it did not measure. **M3-10a ran it, and it is the first task since M3-09a to do so and the first ever to take a baseline on its own branch before editing the fixture.** `FrameOrderTests` alone on untouched `dev` came back **8 / 8** before a line was written; the full suite afterwards came back **16 / 16**, with `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` green. **Both numbers are tallies rather than evidence**, which is this row's own standing warning: n = 1 either side of a change, on a failure measured at 10 % over 50 isolated runs, says nothing. What it does say is that the number did not get *worse* on a task that **changes `RunTicker.CommandPhase` structurally, changes `Hud.prefab` and changes `Scenes/Run.unity`** — three of the leads this row has been collecting, in one PR, and the fourth consecutive task to touch the scene `BootSmokeTests` loads first. **The new row was written to M3-08a's lesson deliberately**: `Frame_SlotPollSitsBesideTapToFocus` asserts the snapshot had not been built when the command landed — a deterministic read of a transform the fixture placed — and asserts nothing about what a sweep found, which is the mistake that took the flake rate from 10 % to 30 %. **M3-10b ran it and it came back 16 / 16 again, which is two consecutive green full runs and is recorded as a tally rather than as evidence** — this row's own standing warning, and n = 1 a task apart on a failure measured at 10 % over 50 isolated runs says nothing. What it does say is that the number did not get worse on the **fifth** consecutive task to change `Scenes/Run.unity`, which is M3-08b's still-unrun lead now carrying five data points nobody has measured; and `BootSmokeTests` passed 3/3 over a scene that gained three more injected components and a prefab that gained thirty-two objects, which is the closest thing to a test of that lead anybody has run by accident. **The cheap experiment M3-08b attached is still unrun**, and is still the next step. **M3-11b ran it and it came back 15 / 16**, failing `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` with M2-15a's exact message — **inside the measured 10 % baseline, recorded as a tally rather than as evidence**, which is this row's own standing warning. It is the first task since M3-10b to see it fire, and the reason it cannot be this one's is checkable rather than a hunch: `FrameOrderTests` drives a `RecordingCore : IRunSession` whose `State` property returns `null` outright (`FrameOrderTests.cs:564`), so `State.Zones.Tick` — the one line this task added to `RunSession.Tick` — would `NullReferenceException` if it ever ran there, and it never does. It is also the **third** consecutive task to touch neither `Run.unity` nor `Hud.prefab`, which is the other half of M3-08b's lead: three tasks in a row with no scene change at all, two of them green (M3-11a-i, M3-11a-ii) and this one red — so a dressed scene is not what moves it either. **M3-11c ran it and it came back 16 / 16, `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` green — recorded as a tally rather than as evidence**, which is this row's own standing warning: n = 1 on a failure measured at 10 % over 50 isolated runs says nothing. What it does say is that the number did not get worse on a task that **changes `RunTicker` structurally, changes `Player.prefab` and changes `Run.unity`** — the first task in four to touch a prefab or the scene at all, which is the *other* side of M3-08b's lead and now carries six scene data points nobody has measured. **The pre-edit baseline this task was asked for does not exist, and the reason is a new observation worth more than the baseline would have been.** Two PlayMode runs queued before the first edit sat at their sentinel for **26 and 6 minutes** with `isPlaying` false, nothing in the Console, and a `QueuePlayerLoopUpdate` nudge changing nothing; the post-edit run then finished in about **seventeen seconds** on an Editor that was unfocused for all three. **So "PlayMode needs focus" is not the explanation** — the one that fits is [Traps §4](../Traps.md#4-the-unity-mcp)'s two-queued-runs rule, with the second `Execute` inheriting the first's stuck state and the first script reimport's domain reload clearing it. The cheap consequence: **a PlayMode run that has not reported `RunStarted` within a couple of minutes is stuck, not slow**, and the fix is a domain reload rather than another `Execute`. **The cheap experiment M3-08b attached is still unrun**, and is still the next step. ~~**First open item, smaller and the owner's:** whether GD §16.4's palette earns a file of its own~~ — **answered at M3-00d: it does.** Nine readers across nine files, three of them holding the player's cyan and three holding CH §4's four kinds, is the state [ledger row 6](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) predicted; [M3-13a](tasks/M3-13a-palette.md) is the file, and its form was settled by `ThreatArrows.Danger` having been a static `readonly Color` since M2-12a rather than by a preference. **A second doc contradiction, opened by M3-00d and open on purpose:** GD §16.2 wants a dying enemy to *"shift toward red"* and GD §16.4 reserves `#FF4A1F` for danger *"for nothing else, ever"* — [M3-13b](tasks/M3-13b-health-bar-treatment.md) rule 3 ships §16.4's reading (a desaturated maroon, `Palette.IsDanger` as the test) because a Husk at 20 % HP in the telegraph colour is the worst readability bug available, and `EnemyHitFeedback` had already reached the same conclusion in a comment. One line in §16.2; the owner's, and M3-15 rule 9 resolves it or carries it with a reason. **One older doc contradiction, open on purpose.** The owner's retune puts the Oathbound at 3 m/s while **GD §6.1's *"5.4–6.2 m/s"* band and Characters.md §3's `140 / 5.4` row still carry the old numbers**, so the starter class sits below its own stated floor. Moving the band is a statement about the Gravecaller (5.6) and the Emberwright (6.2), neither of which is built; [parking lot](ROADMAP.md#parking-lot) holds it and **M5-02** is the first task that cannot avoid it. **One environmental failure to know about and not to chase:** `BootSmokeTests` can fail on *"Unhandled log message: `[Log] Error reason is 'NoSubscription'`"* — Unity's AI Assistant package logging into a PlayMode test, and only when the Editor is signed out. The APK crash has its own task. Disk sits near full — an IL2CPP Android build wants several GB of headroom. **A `ProjectSettings/` change is now refused by the pre-commit hook unless `ALLOW_PROJECT_SETTINGS=1` is set, and an unstaged one is warned about on every commit** — Unity backfills defaults behind you and holds the rewritten copy in memory until restarted ([Traps.md §5](../Traps.md)), and M2-15 found that the "check the diff before committing" habit only ever reached a session following a spec (ledger row 7, closed at M3-00a). The override is for a change made on purpose, like M2-11a's `Cover` layer. |

That is the half `Chain_IsUnbrokenFromOldestToCurrent` could never test while it looped over a range of one. **It does not close because M3-09c remains**: `MigrateProfile` has never run a step at all, and a row leaves only when its *last* owner's *As built* says it is answered — the same rule that kept it open at M3-01b and M3-03. **The open question M3-06 handed forward is now answered rather than passed on**: the owner ruled cooldowns stay off disk, **and the spec's stated reason was found to be false and was rewritten** — it claimed `RunState.Time` does not survive a process death, when `RunTime` has been a v1 field restored exactly by `RunSession.Start` all along. The real reason is that `_readyAt` is keyed by registration order derived from `TakenNodeIds`, so on disk it is a keyed pair of lists with its own guards rather than one field, and the v4 that would carry it is **cheap** precisely because `_readyAt` is absolute against a clock that is itself restored. **Row 4 gains a reason rather than a measurement**: the unmet Profiler `GC.Alloc` line is unmoved — the boundary write is still zero-allocation for every run this build ships, which took a shared four-empty instance to keep true rather than luck — but its **kill-from-recents** row now has a *third* thing to lose, and this task is why that check is worth running: a loadout is the first thing in the save a player would notice missing, where a stage index and a hit point total are not. Still deferred with the rest, and no Editor probe can stand in for it. Rows 1, 5, 6, 8 and 9 are unmoved; 3 and 7 are closed. **And one durable lesson went to the code rather than here**: two lists of `ContentId` on one struct with opposite rules about `default` are written out at **both** ends, not just the surprising one. **M3-09d opened none, closed none and moved three, checked row by row rather than taken from its spec's header.** **Row 9** gains its **fourth** reader and its densest by a long way: twenty-seven unresolved `LocKey`s on one screen, against a card's three for two seconds — which is the fact the row should be ruled on at M3-15 rather than on the cards alone, and it is now a property of shipped code rather than of a spec. **Row 6** gains its **fifth** reader and the largest single one: `TreeNodeView` carries **seven** serialized `Color`s, three node states of its own plus `OfferCard`'s four kind tints **copied value for value** — and `Tree_TintsByKind` now asserts the two files agree, so M3-13a inherits one answer to CH §4's four kinds rather than two that have quietly drifted. The row's predicted shape held: nine readers, of which two are now real. **Row 4** gains the question the spec names as most likely to send this task back — whether twenty-seven cells, let alone M3-12's twelve, fit a landscape safe area at a readable size, and whether rule 9's no-scroll, no-zoom bet survives a 6-inch screen. ## How to write an entry

Append one entry per merged task, newest at the bottom. **Twelve lines or fewer.** The spec's *As built* footer owns the full account of what deviated and why; this entry is the index — what happened, and where each lesson was filed. (The archived logs average 7 KB an entry against a template that said "one line"; the cap is what keeps this file readable.)

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD · M0-03 · Domain events
**Built:** one or two lines — what exists now that didn't.
**Deviations:** none | *n*, in the spec's As built. Name here only one that changes a decision.
**Learned:** one line each, ending with where it was filed — Traps §n · AR §18.x · ledger row n · spent (stays here).
**Follow-ups:** tasks created, ledger rows added, or "none".
```

The PR number lives in the merge commit — do not write it here. Current State is written **as of the PR's merge**: the task the PR closes is *Last merged*. If a deviation changes a decision in `Architecture.md`, it needs a superseding ADR — say so here and link it.

**A durable lesson does not go in Current State.** A toolchain trap goes in [Traps.md](../Traps.md); a rule the code now depends on goes in [Architecture.md §18](../Architecture.md#18-invariants); a thing a named task must handle goes in the [carry-forward ledger](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3), and one with no owner yet in the ROADMAP parking lot, one line. The entry says *what happened*, and links.

**At the end of a milestone**, move its entries to `archive/PROGRESS-M<n>.md`, promote anything durable out of them first, and leave this Log holding only the milestone in progress. **M2 is the first milestone this was done for on time** — M0's and M1's were archived a milestone late and at 381 KB between them, which is the failure the rule was written against.

## Log

_M0, M1 and M2 are archived: [M0](archive/PROGRESS-M0.md) (20 entries) · [M1](archive/PROGRESS-M1.md) (21 entries) · [M2](archive/PROGRESS-M2.md) (27 entries)._

### 2026-09-13 · M2-15a · `Physics.SyncTransforms()`, at the seam

**Built:** one line in `RunTicker.Tick`, between the bodies step and the fact phase. `Physics.autoSyncTransforms` is 0 project-wide, so moving a transform did not move the collider physics holds — AR §18.1's frame order was honoured by the *call order* and quietly not by the code, and a cone could miss an enemy that stepped into it that frame. Landed **after the `m2` tag**, by M2-15's own rule that a bug found at acceptance becomes `M2-15a` with its own PR.

**Deviations:** none. One decision the ruling did not contain — *which* seam, since the two cosmetic steps sit in the middle. It went below them: a projectile view and a telegraph ring touch no collider and ask physics nothing, and flushing above them would have grouped them with the physical half of the frame they are explicitly not part of.

**Learned:** **an intermittent test failure was a real bug the whole time, and the intermittency is why it took four tasks.** It read as a flaky fixture at M2-12a, M2-13b, M2-14a and M2-15 — different counts every run, always the same two `FrameOrderTests` rows, always the `ConeReport` assertion and never an ordering one. That last detail was the tell, and it was in the notes from the start: the *ordering* assertions never failed, only the one that checked physics agreed with it. Filed as its own [AR §18.1](../Architecture.md#181-ordering) row. **Proof of an intermittent fix is repetition, not a green run** — three consecutive 11/11 against 9/11 immediately before. Spent (stays here).

**Follow-ups:** **[M3 ledger row 3](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) struck** — the owner ruled it rather than carrying it into M3's specs, so M3-00a inherits a settled ordering. **The flush's cost is unmeasured** and joins the device rows. Next is **M3-00a**.

### 2026-09-13 · M3-00a · Specs for M3-01…M3-05, the tree as data

**Built:** seven specs where the ROADMAP had five titles — [M3-01a](tasks/M3-01a-xp-and-levels.md) XP, levels and the kill that pays; [M3-01b](tasks/M3-01b-save-format-v2.md) save format v2, one bump for the whole tree; [M3-05](tasks/M3-05-effect-registry.md) the effect registry and `ModifyStat`; [M3-02a](tasks/M3-02a-skill-specs.md) `SkillSpec`, `TriggerSpec`, `SkillTreeSpec`; [M3-02b](tasks/M3-02b-skill-authoring.md) their authoring and the boot lists; [M3-03](tasks/M3-03-tree-rules.md) `TreeRules` and `SkillTree`; [M3-04](tasks/M3-04-offer-generator.md) the offer. Build order 01a → 01b → 05 → 02a → 02b → 03 → 04, because a node carries an `IEffect` before it can be a spec. **Ledger row 7 closed with a hook, not a habit:** `.githooks/pre-commit` refuses a staged `ProjectSettings/` change without `ALLOW_PROJECT_SETTINGS=1` and warns about an unstaged one.
**Two splits, both before starting:** M3-01 at the format seam (the bump is its own review — ledger row 2's point), M3-02 at the core/authoring seam (eleven files together). **Eleven files in this PR** — seven specs, two plan files, the hook and its README — past the five-file rule, docs and shell only, M2-00c's shape and called out the same way.
**Deviations:** none from the brief. Two questions went to the owner and both came back as recommended: **the tree is layered** — two nodes a tier × four tiers + a keystone, nine a branch, twenty-seven a class (M3-02a rule 7) — and **CH §5.2 ships as authored with the depth divergence flagged** (M3-01a rule 9).
**Learned:** **doing the arithmetic on the design doc found two contradictions** — CH §5's "8 a branch, 27 a class" (3 × 8 is 24), whose gating read with one node a tier makes the level-up offer the same three heads every time; and CH §5.2's curve, which against GD §12.1's budget fills the tree by stage 20, not 30 → both parking lot; the first was the shape decision the whole group hangs on. **XP = 3 × threat cost makes a stage's XP a function of its budget alone**, whatever the seed drew — a property worth more than the number → M3-01a rule 2. **A field's writer and reader may be two tasks apart if the bump is one**, and the trade has to be written where the field is → M3-01b rule 1. **A `[SerializeReference]` list has no type picker in Unity 6's Inspector**, so effect authoring is a ScriptableObject per primitive → M3-02b rule 1 (and Traps §5 if it ever bites someone).
**Follow-ups:** ledger row 7 struck; row 2 has its four fields and its two owners (01b, 03); row 1 has the number M3-12 authors against. Four parking-lot lines. **AR §5's `Progression` row is corrected by M3-01a**, not here — `XpCurve` is content. Next is **M3-00b**.

### 2026-09-13 · M3-00b · Specs for M3-06…M3-08, casting and choosing

**Built:** five specs where the ROADMAP had three titles — [M3-06](tasks/M3-06-skill-runner.md) the `SkillRunner`, CH §4.1's 40 % floor as one function two things obey, and auto-cast over the blackboard; [M3-07a](tasks/M3-07a-auto-manual-and-slots.md) the toggle, four slots that are positions rather than a list, and the two `IPlayerCommands` members; [M3-07b](tasks/M3-07b-save-format-v3.md) save v3; [M3-08a](tasks/M3-08a-level-up-flow-and-pause.md) `IProgressionCommands`, the lazy draw, Overflow and `RunPause`; [M3-08b](tasks/M3-08b-level-up-screen.md) three cards and a tap. Build order is ID order, after M3-04.
**Two splits, both before starting, both at a seam an earlier task already argued:** M3-07 at the **format** seam, because the owner ruled Auto/Manual survives a kill and M3-01b rule 1 said that makes it a v3 with its own review; M3-08 at the **core/screen** seam. **Seven files in this PR** — five specs and two plan files — past the five-file rule, docs only, M3-00a's shape and called out the same way.
**Deviations:** none from the brief. Four questions went to the owner; three came back as recommended (**toggles are saved**; **pause gates the tick**, GD §11.4's *"idle the simulation"* read literally; **one screen that re-draws** for a double level-up) and the fourth — where a manual cast lives — **was already answered in a doc**: AR §6 has carried `CastSkill(slot)` and `SetAutoCast` on `IPlayerCommands` since M0-09, so it was never open. The residual half, CC §6.2's full-slots prompt, is ruled: **core throws and the UI asks**, `SpendLevelUp`'s precedent.
**Learned:** **reading the code the spec claims to know moved a rule.** `IncomingProjectiles` is written by `ProjectileSystem.Tick` and nowhere else — *after* combat — so the runner has to tick **above** the projectile step or CC §6.4's Bulwark becomes a shield raised over a wound that already landed; the staleness is the mechanic → M3-06 rule 7, an AR §18.1 row when it is built. **The laziness rule has a second, sharper reason than M3-04 gave:** the boundary capture happens near the *end* of the tick, so drawing anywhere inside it would record a position the draw had advanced and killing the app would be a free reroll → M3-08a rule 1. **Overflow is derivable and needs no field** — `Level − 1 − TakenNodeCount − PendingLevelUps` — which is the exact mirror of M3-03 rule 6's "pending cannot be derived", and it keeps v3 to one field → M3-08a rule 9. **A gated pause splits GD §7.3's stage-length number in two**, since a paused frame contributes no `Dt` → ledger row 8 rewritten. Spent (stays here).
**Follow-ups:** **ledger row 9 opened** — node text is an unresolved `LocKey` and M3-15 cannot judge GD §13.1's two-second rule against a key; **row 6 gained a fourth reader** (M3-08b's four kind tints) and **row 8 gained the play-time / stopwatch distinction**. **M3-09 grew** by the View Tree toggle, the full-slots prompt, drag-to-reorder and the first-active hint; **M3-13**'s palette scope is now known before it is worked. The M3 task count was 22 here and 21 in the ROADMAP; the tables are the authority and now hold **23**. Next is **M3-00c**.

### 2026-09-13 · M3-00c · Specs for M3-09…M3-12, the screens and the first nodes

### 2026-09-13 · M3-01a · `XpCurve`, `LevelTracker`, and the kill that pays for a level

**Built:** **the first code in M3, and the first time the player gets anything for killing something.** `XpCurve` (CH §5.2's `20 + 12·N^1.4`, on the *mode* beside `ScalingSpec`), `LevelTracker` (the level, the banked picks, and an `XpGain` `Stat` nothing yet modifies), and `LeveledUp` / `XpChanged`. `EnemySpec` gained a required `XpValue` — Husk 12, Spitter 21, Bloater 24, three times threat cost — and `EnemySystem` banks it on the one call that reports `Killed`, so a Bloater's own fuse pays exactly like a cone hit. `RunSession.Tick` drains it **after the death check and before the director**. `RunState` hands out `Level`, `XpFraction` and `PendingLevelUps` and keeps the tracker `internal`; the debug overlay shows all three. **Nothing is saved and nothing is offered** — both are M3-01b's and M3-08's.
**Deviations:** four, all in the spec's *As built*. The one that changes a decision: **`Scalings.Xp()` was added to the test support file**, an eighteenth ripple file the table did not name, because the alternative was thirty-four inline copies of the three numbers rule 9 flags for a retune at M3-15.
**Learned:** **the ripple was a third larger than the spec counted, and the miss has a shape worth remembering** — `grep "new EnemySpec("` finds 21 sites and there are 27, because target-typed `new(` is invisible to a grep for the type name. Counting call sites by constructor name undercounts wherever C# 9 let the name be dropped → Traps §7. **Rule 4's guard spelling admits infinity**, and here that is a hung frame rather than a wrong number: `!(amount > 0f)` passes `+∞` into a `while (Xp >= XpToNext)` that then never exits. The spec's own implied non-finite row covers it; the general lesson is that AR §18.3's NaN spelling is about NaN alone and says nothing about the other end → spent (stays here). **A twelve-line `while` loop is the only place in this project where a bad number hangs instead of misbehaving**, which is why `XpCurve.IsAuthored` is checked at three separate doors.
**Follow-ups:** **ledger row 1's curve half is done and pinned by two tests** rather than by prose — `Pacing_LevelEightByStageFive` asserts where CH §5.2's table and its formula agree, `Pacing_TreeFullByStageTwenty` asserts where they stop (27 against the table's 22), so a retune is a red test. **Row 2 was honoured by writing nothing to disk**; M3-01b is the bump. **One issue found that is not this task's and is not fixed here: the M2-15a PlayMode intermittency is back** — see Known issues. Next is **M3-01b**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-01b · Save format v2, and the first migration that ever ran

**Built:** **a levelled run now survives the app closing, and `RunSnapshot` took the first version bump of its life.** Four fields in one step — `Level`, `Xp`, `PendingLevelUps`, `TakenNodeIds` — with the `if (version < 2)` step, the untouched v1 literal as its input and a hand-typed v2 literal as its output, **all in one PR**, which is the whole of ledger row 2. `RunRecorder.Take` captures the three tracker reads (absolute XP, never the fraction — `PlayerShield`'s argument, sharper, because `XpToNext` moves with the curve); `LevelTracker.Restore` puts them back **silently and settling**, beside `Health.Restore` and before `RunStarted`. `RunState` gained `Xp`. `TakenNodeIds` is written empty and read-and-ignored until M3-03, with a row apiece saying so.
**Deviations:** none. Sixteen files, all in the Files table, and **the ripple was exactly the 13 sites across 10 files the spec counted** — the first time this milestone's ripple arithmetic was right first go. Three implementation choices the spec left open are in the *As built*.
**Learned:** **the MCP's refusals name neither the line nor the reason, and this one cost four dead ends.** `File.Delete` is a blocked call reported as *"User interactions are not supported for MCP tool calls"*, so every probe that tidied up its own results file was refused whole — and the blame fell on `TestRunnerApi.Execute`, which Traps §4 says is refused and which **ran clean directly, both suites, no reflection and no temporary helper**. Bisect the script, never the message; a sentinel written over the file beats deleting it → Traps §4, which is now corrected on both counts. **A field initialiser on a `[Serializable]` mirror is load-bearing across a version bump:** `RunMirror.level = 1` is what stops a v1 document — which has no `level` key — hitting `RunSnapshot`'s "level must be at least 1" guard and being discarded as corrupt on every device that already has one. A guard added at the same time as the field it guards will refuse the *old* documents unless the mirror's default is the old meaning → AR §18.3. **An allocation rule can be kept honestly by keeping the empty case empty:** `Array.Empty<T>()` is what makes a copy-on-construction list free until there is something to copy → spent (stays here).
**Follow-ups:** **ledger row 2 moved and did not close** — the run format is v2 and the chain is real, and three bumps remain (M3-03 fills the fourth field and bumps nothing, M3-07b's v3 is the first two-step chain, M3-09c is the first profile step). **One parking-lot line opened:** a hand-edited `"xp":1e38` is a legal snapshot that hangs the settle loop, and capping it is a ruling rather than an implementation detail. **`Take_AllocatesNothing` is now M3-03's row to retire**, named in the test and in `RunRecorder`'s remarks rather than left to go red. **The PlayMode intermittency is unchanged and still not ours** — 11/11 then 10/11, `FrameOrderTests` on a `RecordingCore` fake. Next is **M3-05**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-02a · `SkillSpec`, `TriggerSpec`, `SkillTreeSpec`: the tree as data

**Built:** **the tree exists as data, and a node can now carry the effect M3-05 built.** `SkillSpec` is CH §4's four kinds with the effects taking it puts into force; `ActiveSpec` is what an Active is when it fires — authored base cooldown, trigger, cast effects — and **rule 1 is checked in both directions, unlike `EnemySpec`**: an Active without its block is a skill that cannot fire, a block on a Passive is a cooldown nothing will run, and `ParentId` is the same rule again. `TriggerSpec` is CC §6.4's condition as one or two ANDed `TriggerClause`es over the nine `CombatBlackboard` fields — **ADR-0005's "pure predicates, carried by the `SkillSpec` as data" made authorable**, because a lambda cannot come out of an asset and an enum-and-a-threshold can. `SkillTreeSpec` is three branches of tiers of ids with `TryLocate` as a dictionary built once. `ContentCatalog` gained the fourth and fifth kinds through the same `Index`, plus **one second index nothing else has** — `TryGetTreeFor(characterId)`, the door a run actually uses.
**Deviations:** three, all in the spec's *As built*. The one that changes a decision is **rule 6's own parenthetical, which is wrong and had to be corrected to be honoured.**
**Learned:** **AR §18.3's NaN spelling is about which side of a predicate NaN lands on, and the `!` is not part of the rule.** Rule 6 prescribed `!(value < threshold)`; applied to `AtLeast` — the only comparison a `!` could belong to — `!(NaN < 0.6)` is **true**, so a broken blackboard would have satisfied every `AtLeast` clause the player owns and fired every active at once, which is the exact failure the rule's own sentence forbids. The `!` in AR §18.3's `!(value > 0f)` is there because a **guard** wants NaN on the *throw* side; a predicate wants it on the *false* side and needs no `!` at all. Both comparisons are written out rather than derived from each other, and `Trigger_NaNFieldFails` — which demanded `false` all along — is what proved the parenthetical rather than the code was the bug → **AR §18.3**, as a second sentence on the existing row. **`Core/Content` stopped being a leaf**: it held zero `using Soulvail.*` before this task and now names `Core.Combat` and `Core.Effects`, which makes `Content ↔ Combat` a namespace cycle — harmless today, and it would block AR §5's own *"split into separate assemblies only if compile times demand it"* → parking lot, and **AR §5 needs no row edited**, which is the answer the `XpCurve` precedent was checked against rather than assumed from. **A test written against reflection cannot drift from the thing it checks:** `Trigger_EveryFieldReads` walks `Enum.GetValues` and looks each member up on `CombatBlackboard` *by name*, so a field wired to the wrong one reads 0 and fails, a field wired to nothing throws out of the loud `default`, and a member naming no field fails the first assertion — three failure modes, no hand-kept table → spent (stays here).
**Follow-ups:** **no ledger row opened, none closed, and none moved** — correct for a task that writes nothing to disk and offers nothing to anybody. **One parking-lot line opened** (the `Content ↔ Combat` cycle). **CH §5's *"8 (7 + 1 Keystone)"* re-flagged and not edited** — rule 7's instruction and the GD §12.1 precedent; it has been a parking-lot line since M3-00a and `Tree_RecordsShape` now asserts 2/2/2/2/1 against it. **M3-05's open seam is untouched and now documented where a reader would ask** — `SkillSpec.Effects`' remarks say that neither this constructor nor `CanApply` checks an effect's *address*. Next is **M3-02b**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-05 · The effect registry, and `ModifyStat` as the first primitive

**Built:** **the first thing in the milestone that can change a number.** `Core/Effects/` is a new folder holding three files: `IEffect` / `IEffectHandler<T>` / `EffectRegistry` (ADR-0009's open set, dispatching on the effect's CLR type through a private binding, so there is no `switch (effect.Type)` and adding the eleventh primitive edits nothing that exists); `PlayerStat` and `PlayerStats`, the six-member address table that turns a name an asset can carry into this run's live `Stat`; and `ModifyStat` with its handler, one `Modifier` on one stat. `RunState` gained an `internal EffectRegistry Effects`, and `RunSession.Start` builds the address table and the registry over the motor, the combat and the tracker — after the objects exist, before `RunStarted`. **Nothing in a live run calls `Apply` until M3-03** (rule 10), deliberately.
**Deviations:** two, both additive and both in the spec's *As built*. `Effects` goes in through `RunState`'s `internal` constructor rather than as a settable property, because every other live object on that type does. Five test rows beyond the table's twenty-two — four are the guard rows the spec says are implied; the fifth, **`Registries_DoNotShareHandlers`**, is the half of `Registry_HoldsNoStatics` that reflection cannot see and is the one worth naming.
**Learned:** **"no static fields" and "not a singleton" are two different claims, and only the first is a reflection row.** A registry is the classic static temptation, and the failure it would cause is specific — its handlers hold *this run's* live player, so a shared one applies the next run's nodes to the last run's body. Reflection proves there is no static field; it cannot prove two instances do not share a table, which takes an instance test. Spent (stays here). **`CanApply` answers a narrower question than "is this effect valid".** It asks whether a *handler* is registered for the type, and says nothing about whether the effect's `PlayerStat` resolves — so an authored `(PlayerStat)99` would pass M3-03's validation sweep and throw at the pick. The spec's Public API deliberately leaves the address unguarded at the constructor (`MovementSkillKind`'s argument: the loud place is the one site that knows the full set), so this is a known seam rather than a gap → named in M3-05's *As built* for M3-02b and M3-14b. **A `RunSession.Start` line can be correct and untestable at once:** `Effects` is `internal` and `Soulvail.Tests.Core` has no `InternalsVisibleTo` (AR §18.2, on purpose), so no row can see the registration — M3-03 is the first task that could fail without it. Spent (stays here).
**Follow-ups:** **no ledger row opened and none closed.** Row 1 moves only in the sense that M3-12's nodes now have a mechanism to be made of; the five stats rule 5 defers are M3-12a's and are listed in `PlayerStat`'s own remarks rather than in the ledger, because they already have an owning task. **The PlayMode intermittency did not fire this time** — 11/11, and one green run is not evidence about an intermittent failure either way. Next is **M3-02a**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-02b · Skill authoring: the four definitions and the boot lists

**Built:** **a designer can now author a node, an effect and a class's tree as assets, and boot turns them into the catalog.** `Game/Authoring/` gained four ScriptableObjects: `EffectDefinition`, the abstract base a primitive's authoring half derives from; `ModifyStatDefinition`, the first concrete one; `SkillDefinition` with `TriggerClauseField`; and `SkillTreeDefinition` with `BranchField` and `TierField`. **Polymorphism is by asset type and there is no switch anywhere** — a node references effect *assets*, so a new primitive is a new subclass and no edit to any existing file, which is ADR-0009 arriving on the authoring side of the seam. `BootInstaller.Install` takes `skills` and `trees` as required lists (`enemies`' argument), `BootScope` carries two more serialized arrays, and `BootScope.prefab` gained exactly two lines. **Nothing ships in `Data/`** (rule 7) and `Boot_EmptyListsAreLegal` is the row that says that is a boot before M3-12 rather than a broken one.
**Deviations:** three, all additive and all in the tests, in the spec's *As built*. The one worth naming here is not a deviation but **an owner ruling taken before a line was written**: the address seam M3-05 left open is closed here rather than at M3-14b.
**Learned:** **the seam was real but narrower than it read, and the cheap door was the right size for it.** `CanApply` answers "is there a handler for this type" and `ModifyStat` deliberately does not validate `PlayerStat`, but a member with no resolver line is already caught by `Stats_ResolveEveryMember` and the enum is a dropdown — so the only live vector is a serialized `int` left behind by a removed or reordered member, and `Enum.IsDefined` at conversion is five lines in a file this task was writing anyway. **The general version was deliberately not built and has a different owner:** widening `IEffectHandler<T>` with `CanApply(TEffect)`, so the registry's door means what its name says for every primitive, is M3-03's — the task that actually calls that door at `Start` → recorded in the *As built*, not the ledger, because it has an owning task. **`ContentId` refuses camelCase**, which two of this task's rows found the honest way: the grammar is `[a-z0-9]` then `.[a-z0-9_-]+`, so **M3-14b rule 3's own worked example, `skill.oathbound.temperedVow`, cannot be constructed** — the convention M3-12 must author against is `skill.oathbound.tempered-vow`, and that rule's file-name comparison needs to be hyphen-to-PascalCase → filed in this spec's *As built* for M3-14b. **Traps §5 was checked rather than trusted**: three public top-level `[Serializable]` classes sit beside ScriptableObjects in two files, the SO leads each file, and `MonoScript.FromScriptableObject` answers non-null for all three concrete types → spent (stays here). **A default that equals the value a test authors makes the test vacuous, and it bit twice** — `_cooldown` initialises to 8 and `_kind` to `PercentAdd`, which is exactly what the Tests table asked both rows to assert; both were rewritten to author something else or to flip the field and convert again → Traps §7, already a row.
**Follow-ups:** **no ledger row opened, none closed, none moved** — correct for a task that ships no content, writes nothing to disk and draws nothing; row 9's `LocKey` readers gain two more kinds counted by kind rather than by file. **One finding handed forward to M3-14b** (the camelCase id), and **one question handed forward to M3-03** (the general `CanApply`). Next is **M3-03**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-03 · `TreeRules` and `SkillTree`: the first thing that takes a node

**Built:** **a run has a live tree, and taking a node changes the player's numbers.** `Core/Progression/` gained two files: `TreeRules`, which resolves every id in a class's tree and checks the three rules no single spec could — every node authored, a Keystone as the sole node of its branch's last tier, an Upgrade's parent below it *in the same branch* — and `SkillTree`, which holds the taken flags, answers CH §5's gating, applies a node's effects through the registry with the `SkillSpec` as the source, and publishes `NodeTaken` last. `RunState` gained an `internal Tree` and the three reads that stand in for it; `RunSession.Start` builds both before `RunStarted`; `RunRecorder` writes `state.TakenNodeIds` where it wrote an empty array. **M3-01b's two placeholder rows flipped rather than appeared** (rule 9), and no version bumped.
**Deviations:** five, all additive or forced, all in the spec's *As built*. The one worth naming here is **the contradiction in the spec's own Public API**: it drafted `internal void Restore` and assigned restore to `SkillTreeTests.cs`, which no `InternalsVisibleTo` makes possible — so `Restore` is **public**, on M3-05's precedent, and the seal is one layer out at `RunState.Tree`.
**Learned:** **an `internal` member beside a public mutator on the same object protects nothing.** `Take` is public by design (rule 8 argues it), so anything holding a `SkillTree` could grant every node in a legal order by hand; `internal Restore` would have been a lock on the back door of an open front one. The seal that does the work is the *handle* — AR §18.2 for the seventh time, and the first time the answer settled a question about a second member rather than about the field → AR §18.2, already the standing rule. **The restore ordering is the whole of this task's risk and it is one line:** `SkillTree.Restore` before `Health.Restore`, or a `+20 max HP` node lands after the absolute hit points are clamped and a run saved at 150/160 resumes at 140 → **AR §18.1**, new row, with the test that fails on a swap. **A remark that predicts a future task's work can be wrong, and this one was:** `RunRecorder` and `Take_AllocatesNothing` both said M3-03 would *retire* that row, and it does not — a copy of an empty list is `Array.Empty`, and this build's runs have no tree; the first *node taken* is the first boundary write to allocate. Both comments now say so → spent (stays here). **Three rows were green while proving nothing, in two different ways**: two passed an *empty* registry to a sweep meant to trip on one planted node, so they tripped on the fixture's first node instead; one expected 14.95 where the fixture's own default effects make it 16.25. The general lesson is Traps §7's, one step along — **a fixture whose defaults also satisfy the assertion is as vacuous as a field initialiser that does** → Traps §7, already a row. **And one new trap, which cost this task real time:** a `TestRunnerApi.RegisterCallbacks` registration outlives its run, so running EditMode and then PlayMode fires the EditMode collector again with the PlayMode result and overwrites its own results file — four files all reading `passed=11`, which looks like a suite that dropped from 1266 tests to 11 → **Traps §7**, new row (one file outside the Files table, named as a deviation in the spec's *As built*).
**Follow-ups:** **no ledger row opened; row 2 moved and did not close** — the fourth field is filled and nothing bumped, which was the prediction half of that row, and two bumps remain. **One parking-lot line opened, which is the owner's ruling on the question M3-02b handed here:** `IEffectHandler<T>` does *not* gain `CanApply(TEffect)`, rule 4 stands as written, and the trigger for building it is named — the first primitive whose applicability is a *run-scoped* question. **PlayMode 10/11 then 11/11**, the Known-issues row firing on the first and the fixture passing 4/4 on its own; **it is not this task's, checked rather than inherited** — `RecordingCore.State` returns `null` outright, so no line added here executes in that fixture. Next is **M3-04**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-04 · `OfferGenerator`

**Built:** two files in `Core/Progression/` and `Tests/Core/Progression/` and nothing else — the game can now pick **which three** nodes a level-up shows. Up to `count` distinct nodes from `SkillTree.Available` and never from the tree, weighted for variety (base 1, × 0.5 per offer already drawn this call from the same branch, × 2 for an Active while fewer than two are owned), **one `NextFloat()` per pick whatever the walk then finds**, from `Offers` and no other stream, allocating nothing after construction. Nothing calls it: M3-08 builds one in `Start` beside the tree.
**Deviations:** 4, in the spec's [As built](tasks/M3-04-offer-generator.md#as-built). The one that changes a decision is the owner's, asked before a line was written: **`Draw` refuses a tree built over different `TreeRules`**, against M0-10's trust-the-composition-root argument, because the failure is asymmetric and *silent* — only a bigger wrong tree trips `Available`'s short-buffer refusal, so a same-sized one fills a correct buffer with another class's ids and the first symptom is `Take` throwing in M3-08 at the moment the player taps a card. `Draw` re-asserts the pairing on every call, which is `Restore`'s shape rather than a constructor's.
**Learned:** **A spec row can test that something happened rather than that the rule holds, and the tell is arithmetic nobody did** — `Draw_UsesTheWeights`' scripted 0.99 lands in C under the weights (2.97 of 3.0) *and* under uniform weights (3.96 of 4.0), so it would have gone green against a generator with no table at all; 0.7 separates them (2.1 → C, 2.8 → B). Second time this milestone, after M3-02a's NaN spelling → spent (stays here). **A statistical row asserted only as a ceiling is half a test** — *"under 3 %"* is also met by a generator that never offers three from one branch, which is the hard rule GD §13.1 does not want; it asserts 1.52 % ± 0.5 pp with the ceiling beside it → spent. **A rule split across a table and its caller needs a row per term**: `Draw_UsesTheWeights` pins the branch penalty and nothing pinned the Active boost, so half the table could have been inlined and dropped — `Draw_BoostsAnActiveThroughTheTable` is the second row → spent. **And one new trap: a PlayMode run clears the Console on entering Play**, so a sweep taken after one describes eleven tests and reads as a clean 1 286-test suite → **Traps §7**, new row (one file outside the Files table, named as a deviation).
**Follow-ups:** **no ledger row opened, closed or moved, checked row by row rather than taken from the spec's "Ledger rows: none"** — no content (row 1), nothing written to disk and no version needed since `Offers` has been in `RandomState` since M0-04 (row 2), nothing drawn (rows 5, 6), and what it hands out is `ContentId`s rather than `LocKey`s (row 9). **One parking-lot line moved from a prediction to a fact:** the counting `IRandom` fake is now on its second private copy, so the promote-on-the-third rule has a count behind it — and the private LCG beside it is recorded as a *first* copy, promoted on its own. **EditMode 1 286 / 0 / 0 three times, PlayMode 11/11 twice**, the Known-issues row not firing and unable to be moved either way by a task that touches no `Tick`, no view and no scene. Next is **M3-06**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-06 · `SkillRunner`: the 40 % floor, and auto-cast over the blackboard

**Built:** **the first thing in the game that acts without the player.** `Core/Combat/CooldownRules.cs` is CH §4.1's floor in one place because two things obey it — `max(0.4 × authoredBase, live)`, taken from the **authored** base and never from `Stat.Base`, with a non-finite or non-positive base refused and an infinite *live* deliberately not floored. `Core/Combat/SkillRunner.cs` owns up to twelve actives in take order, each with its own `Stat` seeded from `ActiveSpec.Cooldown` and an absolute `_readyAt`; `Tick` walks them, casts **at most one**, applies `OnCast` through the registry **with the `ActiveSpec` as the source** — not the `SkillSpec` the take used — and publishes `SkillCast` **last**, carrying the effective cooldown. `Core/Events/SkillEvents.cs` is that event. `ChargeSkill` now floors its own `_readyAt` through the same function, which is what its line-241 comment has promised since M1-14. `RunSession.Tick` ticks the runner **between combat and the enemy behaviours**, which puts it above the projectile step; `RunSession.Start` builds it after the tree and adds a resumed run's Actives in take order. `RunState` gained an `internal Skills` and four reads; the overlay says `actives 0`, which is the runner correctly empty rather than invisible.
**Deviations:** 3, in the spec's [As built](tasks/M3-06-skill-runner.md#as-built). The one that changes a decision is the owner's, asked before a line was written: **a tree holding more Actives than the runner can own refuses the run at `Start`** — `TreeRules` gains an `ActiveCount` fact and `RunSession.Start` does the comparison — rather than the thirteenth `Add` throwing. What settled it was not M3-03's keystone precedent but `SkillTree`'s own `CanApply` sweep, which answers this exact seam already: sweep the authored content at `Start`, keep the throw as the backstop. `Add` is not merely late but **dirty** — M3-08a orders `ChooseOffer` as `Take` → `SpendLevelUp` → `Add`, so a throw there leaves the node owned, its effects applied, `NodeTaken` published and the pick spent.
**Learned:** **An ordering row that says "both happened" is not an ordering row, and the only way to know is to break the ordering.** Both halves of rule 7 were proved red under their own swap and green under the other's: below the projectile step, the cast still happens but lands *after* the `PlayerDamaged` (index 2 against 0) — a shield put up over the wound, which a presence assertion would have called a pass; above `PlayerCombat.Tick`, the cast arrives a tick late. Neither swap reddens the other's row → **AR §18.1**, one new row plus an edit to the sequence row. **A sense that no core fixture fills makes an integration row fail for a reason that looks like the code**: `EnemySense.HasLineOfSight` is false by default, so a Spitter never begins a wind-up and the first version of both rows died at *"the fixture failed to land a bolt"* after 3 000 ticks — a core-side session fixture has to play `SnapshotBuilder` → Traps §7's "a fixture that hand-writes perception" family, already a row. **Real arithmetic and float arithmetic disagree at the floor, and the spec quoted the real one**: four additive −15 % nodes give 0.99999994 where the floor is exactly 1.0f, so the row is green because the floor *catches* it and must assert `Effective(...)` rather than the stat → spent (stays here). **And `!(now >= readyAt)` is not a style choice**: the natural spelling reads an unreadable clock as off-cooldown, schedules a NaN `_readyAt` nothing can ever compare true against, and fires that skill every tick for the rest of the run → AR §18.3, already the standing rule.
**Follow-ups:** **rows 1 and 4 both move, neither closes, and the other seven were checked row by row.** Row 1 gains the floor as a measured fact — 1.0 s at 2.5, 3.2 s at 8, six multiplicative nodes or four additive — which is the ceiling M3-12 authors cooldown nodes against; row 4 gains a new per-frame path on the unmet Profiler line. Row 2 honoured by writing nothing for the third time, **with one question handed to M3-07b**, which already owns a v3: a resumed run's cooldowns come back at zero, and that is a decision rather than an oversight. **One finding with no owner, for the owner to rule on:** `TriggerField.Veilrot` reads a field nothing writes, so `Veilrot AtLeast 50` never fires and `Veilrot Below 50` always does — silently, and CH §4.2's Rot Nova is exactly the first — with **M3-14b** named as the right owner rather than M6-04, because the general check is an authoring one. Next is **M3-07a**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-07a · Auto/Manual, the four manual slots, and the two commands

**Built:** **the switch CC §6.1 opens with, and the first player input that reaches a skill.** No new production files. `SkillRunner` gained `MaxManualSlots = 4`, an `IsAuto` flag as a fourth `bool[MaxActives]` beside the three, a `ContentId[4]` slot table with a `ReadOnlyCollection` wrapped **once** at construction, and `ManualSlotCount` / `IsAuto(id)` / `SlotAt(slot)` / `Slots` / `SetAutoCast` / `CastSlot`. **The skip is the first statement in `Tick`'s loop**, above both existing `continue`s, so a Manual skill's authored condition is never evaluated — CC §6.5's *"stop paying for a condition you have decided to judge yourself"* met by construction rather than by a comment. Switching to Manual takes the **lowest free** slot; switching back empties it and **nothing else moves**, because CC §6.2 draws four fixed thumb positions and compacting would slide S3's skill under the thumb that learned S2. `IPlayerCommands` grew `CastSkill(int slot)` and `SetAutoCast(ContentId, bool)` — the two members AR §6 has listed since M0-09 — implemented on `RunSession` through `RequireRunning`; `SkillEvents.cs` gained `SkillAutoCastChanged`; `RunState` gained three reads and no handle; the overlay says `slots 0/4`. **Nothing is written to disk** (rule 11) and **`RunSnapshot.CurrentVersion` is still 2** — M3-07b is the bump, deliberately its own review.
**Deviations:** 4, in the spec's [As built](tasks/M3-07a-auto-manual-and-slots.md#as-built). **The one worth naming is forced rather than chosen: growing a port breaks every implementer**, so two test doubles outside the Files table — `FrameOrderTests.RecordingCore` and `ResumeFlowTests.RecordingSession` — had to gain both members. The compiler found exactly those two and neither fixture sends either command. The other three: AR §6's row gained a word (M3-06's precedent, which edited AR §5's the same way); Traps §7 gained the throwing-clause technique on the owner's ruling; `StartSession` gained an `actives` count.
**Ruled:** **M3-06's ruling against a throw does not carry, and the distinction is the reachability of the state.** There, an over-capacity tree was an *authoring* mistake surfacing mid-`ChooseOffer` — after the node was owned, its effects applied and the pick spent — so the refusal moved to `Start`. Here a fifth manual skill is a **player action** with a reachable state, a legitimate refusal and a designed prompt (CC §6.2's *"Manual slots full — which skill goes back to auto?"*, M3-09's to show). The throw is what makes that prompt mandatory, and it has nothing upstream of it: it refuses before it writes, and `SetManual_FifthThrowsNamingTheCeiling` asserts that nothing moved and nothing was published. `LevelTracker.SpendLevelUp`'s precedent, not `Add`'s.
**Learned:** **A behavioural row cannot see an ordering rule, and this one hid inside a passing test.** `Manual_NeverAutoCasts` stays green with the skip placed *below* the trigger test — the skill still never fires, and the condition is still paid for on every tick, which is the entire thing rule 5 buys. **So the claim needed a row that could tell "did not fire" from "was not asked", and no counting fake can exist**: `TriggerSpec` is `public sealed` with a non-virtual `IsMet` and no interface, `TriggerClause` is a `readonly struct`, `CombatBlackboard` is sealed. The answer, on the owner's ruling, was to make the clause **unreadable** rather than countable — `TriggerClause`'s constructor validates only `threshold` and never `field`, so `(TriggerField)99` constructs, survives `TriggerSpec`'s count-only constructor, and throws out of `IsMet`'s loud `default` on first read. **And it needs its control or it proves nothing:** the same spec left Auto must throw on tick 1, or a `DoesNotThrow` over 100 ticks is green against a runner that skipped the trigger for any reason at all → **Traps §7**, generalised past triggers. **The two Traps rows about the test runner both fired in this session and both were survivable only because they were read first:** the EditMode collector outlived its run and overwrote its own results file with the PlayMode tally (the file now reads `passed=11`), and the PlayMode run cleared the Console — so the sweep was taken *between* the two runs, describing the 1 356-test suite rather than eleven tests' worth of nothing → spent, both already rows.
**Follow-ups:** **none opened, none closed, and all nine checked row by row rather than taken from the spec's *"Ledger rows: none"*.** **Row 2 was honoured by writing nothing for the fourth time** — no field, no version, no step, no fixture — and `Snapshot_CarriesNoLoadout` is what makes that a decision rather than an omission: it walks `RunSnapshot`'s members for anything that looks like a slot and asserts `CurrentVersion` is still 2. **The open question M3-06 handed forward is still M3-07b's and was deliberately not answered here:** a resumed run's cooldowns come back at zero. Row 4 gains nothing measurable — the skip makes a Manual skill *cheaper* per frame, not dearer — but **M3-10a's multi-touch row now has its core half built**, which is the one device row that risks a feature rather than a verdict. Rows 3 and 7 closed; 1, 5, 6, 8 and 9 unmoved, and what this hands out is `ContentId`s, never a `LocKey`. Next is **M3-07b**.

### 2026-09-14 · M3-07b · Save format v3, and the first chain with two steps in it

**Built:** **a loadout survives a kill-from-recents, and `MigrateRun` ran two steps in sequence for the first time.** No new files. `RunSnapshot` is v3 with `ManualSkillIds` — CC §6.2's four thumb positions, fixed-length so a *hole* is expressible, `default(ContentId)` meaning an empty slot; the `if (version < 3)` step ships below the `< 2` one, so a v1 document now walks v1 → v2 → v3 in one decode through the real adapter. `SkillRunner.Restore` is `internal` and silent, dropping a slot the run does not own; the restore line sits below the runner's actives and above `Health.Restore`. **17 `new RunSnapshot(` sites across 13 files** took the new argument. **1373 EditMode / 0 / 0 twice, PlayMode 11/11.**
**Deviations:** 1, in the spec's [As built](tasks/M3-07b-save-format-v3.md#as-built). **`SkillRunnerTests` changed and is not in the Files table**: M3-07a's pin `Snapshot_CarriesNoLoadout` asserted `CurrentVersion == 2` and refused any member named like a slot, so it was the red row this task had to change. **Inverted in place as `Snapshot_CarriesTheLoadout`** rather than deleted — the claim worth keeping is the same one from the other side.
**Ruled:** **the spec's reason for keeping cooldowns off disk was false and was rewritten rather than inherited.** It said saving them would need `RunState.Time` to mean something across a process death *"which it deliberately does not"* — it does, and always has: `RunTime` is a **v1** field and `RunSession.Start:553` restores `State.Time` from it exactly. The owner ruled the **ruling stands and the reason changes**: `_readyAt` is keyed by registration order derived from `TakenNodeIds`, so on disk it is a keyed pair of lists with its own guards, not one field, and it does not belong in the PR shipping the first two-step chain. **The later bump is cheap and that is a fact about the code** — `_readyAt` is absolute against a clock that is itself restored, so v4 is add-only. M3-06's open question is **answered and closed**, not carried.
**Learned:** **a fixed-length list has no empty case to shortcut on *length*, so it would have made every stage boundary allocate** — `Take_AllocatesNothing` would have gone red for a reason that read as a guard bug. Four empty slots are indistinguishable from any other four, so one shared immutable instance answers them all, and the occupancy scan runs *before* the array is allocated → filed in `RunSnapshot`'s own remarks and AR §18.3's sanctioned-static company. **And a class tree has exactly three branches** (CH §5), so the fixture's new Actives joined an existing tier rather than a fourth branch → spent, found by two red rows. **The MCP cost nothing this time: `AssetDatabase.Refresh(ImportAssetOptions.ForceUpdate)` plus a ~45 s wait picked the files up first try** — already Traps §4, and it held.
**Follow-ups:** **none opened, none closed, and all nine checked row by row rather than taken from the spec's header.** **Row 2 moved and did not close** — the chain has run two steps on one document at last, which is the half a range of one could never test, but **M3-09c's profile v2 remains**, and `MigrateProfile` has never run a step at all; a row leaves only when its *last* owner says so. Row 4 gains nothing measurable — the boundary write is still zero-alloc for every run this build ships. Rows 1 (no content, no damage), 5 (no Animator), 6 (nothing drawn), 8 (nothing paused) and 9 (`ContentId`s, never a `LocKey`) are unmoved; 3 and 7 are closed. **One parking-lot line gains a fact rather than a prediction:** the cooldown-reset-by-`Continue` gap is now named in `RunSnapshot`'s remarks and the spec's Out of scope, with the v4 that would close it priced.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-08a · `IProgressionCommands`, the lazy offer, Overflow, and the gate that idles the simulation

**Built:** **the first thing that spends a pick, and the first that can stop the game.** Three new production files — AR §6's third inbound port, `LevelUpFlow`, `RunPause`. An owed pick draws up to three nodes from the `Offers` stream **on the frame after the tick that earned it**; choosing takes the node, spends the pick, hands a new Active straight to the runner and re-draws over the new tree state; a pick with nothing to offer becomes CH §5.2's Overflow, +2 %/+2 % pooled additively under one source, silent and announced. `RunTicker` gains `LevelUpPhase()` above `CommandPhase` and returns on a held pause, so a paused run is **idled rather than clocked at zero**. **`CurrentVersion` stays 3** — Overflow is derived — and AR §18.1's restore block is now **six lines**. 1 423 EditMode / 0 / 0 twice; PlayMode **15/15**, the count moving for the first time this milestone.
**Deviations:** 11, in the spec's [As built](tasks/M3-08a-level-up-flow-and-pause.md#as-built). The two that matter: **the port ships four members, not two**, because rule 4's `State.IsLevelUpPending` is not implementable — `RunTicker` reads `_session.State` nowhere and the PlayMode fixture cannot build a `RunState` (AR §18.2), so all four `Frame_*` rows would have been unwritable; and **the ripple is two fixtures**, `ResumeFlowTests` as well as `FrameOrderTests`, which the Files table does not imply and the compiler found.
**Ruled:** **take the flake baseline before building, and it changed the code.** 50 isolated runs on untouched `dev` gave 10 %, all one row. The first post-edit measurement was 30 % — a new row had asserted `ConeReport` and hung itself on the same intermittent sweep. Rewritten to assert the fact *phase* rather than what the sweep found: **0 failures in 100 runs** for the four new rows. Without the "before" number that would have read as the old intermittency worsening, and a new cause would have been absorbed under an old name.
**Learned:** **two spec rows could not be written, and both absences are findings.** *Blocked but not full* is **unreachable** over any tree `TreeRules` accepts — first tiers are always offered, a Keystone needs exactly "the rest of its branch", an Upgrade's parent is always reachable first — so M3-14b's starvation check has nothing to catch until M6-02's Banish removes nodes from the pool. And a 10 000-cycle open-and-choose cannot be allocation-free: every choose grows a `Stat`'s `List<Modifier>`, so the row would have measured M3-03. **Also: "+2 % damage" means +2 % of the *base*** — on a tree already pooling +180 % it is a ×1.007 change, not ×1.02, and three rows asserted the wrong one first. **And Unity wipes `Temp/` mid-session** (new Traps §7 row): a finished 50-run measurement vanished, and cost nothing only because each file had been read before the next run started.
**Follow-ups:** **none opened, none closed, and all nine checked row by row rather than taken from the spec's header.** **Row 1 moves and does not close** — Overflow is now a *built and tested* second source of power (×1.20 at ten levels, ×1.28 at fourteen), but no run in this build can reach it, M3-12 still owes the nodes and M3-15 still measures. **Row 4 moves and cannot close** — rule 13 adds a device question (does `timeScale` 0 at 30 fps recover thermal headroom, and does the drop read as a hitch entering and leaving the screen) to a list that needs a phone. **Row 8 moves and does not close** — rule 11 is now implemented and pinned by `Frame_PausedTicksCostNoSimulatedTime`, so `RunState.Time` and a stopwatch are genuinely different numbers from here on and M3-15 must quote both, labelled; nothing has been timed yet. **Rows 2, 5, 6 and 9 are untouched and that is correct rather than an omission**: nothing was written to disk (2), no animator work (5), no colour reader added (6) and no new unresolved `LocKey` — `OfferPresented` deliberately carries a count rather than names, and M3-08b's cards are the readers (9). Rows 3 and 7 remain closed.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-08b · The level-up screen: three cards, a tap, and the frame that starts again

**Built:** **the other half of M3-08a, and the first thing in the game a player chooses *with*.** Two new production files — `LevelUpPresenter` and `OfferCard`, both **block namespaces** (Traps §5) — plus `Prefabs/UI/LevelUp.prefab` on its own canvas above the HUD's, `Scenes/Run.unity` dressed with it, and one optional `RegisterComponent` on `RunScope`. `OfferPresented` becomes up to three cards drawn from `RunState.Offer` and resolved through `ContentCatalog.Skill`; a tap becomes `ChooseOffer(index)`; `LevelUpClosed` hides it. The header reads *"Level N"* and *"Pick i of n"*, with **i counting up while `PicksOwed` counts down** — the payload written straight through would read "Pick 2 of 2" then "Pick 1 of 1", which is exactly the double level-up the line exists to make legible. **1 447 EditMode / 0 / 0, twice consecutively** against M3-08a's 1 423 — **24 new rows, landing to the row** — and **PlayMode stayed at 15**, which is where the good news stops; see *Learned*.

**Ruled:** **the owner settled M3-08a deviation 12 before a line was written: the gate stays in `RunTicker`, and M3-08b rule 3 is rewritten rather than implemented.** The screen renders a gate it does not own. Rule 3's own reason — *"a pause raised in one class and lowered in another is how a screen ends up closed over a frozen game"* — argues against **split** ownership, and the ticker holds both halves eight lines apart, so the reason survives and only the nomination of which file changed. **What it buys:** the run being stopped is a once-a-frame pure function of `HasOffer` rather than a consequence of a view having received an event, so a presenter that is absent, destroyed or never dressed costs a *missing screen*, loudly, rather than a run that ticks on with an offer nobody can spend — and it makes rule 12 true instead of a caveat. **The cost was measured rather than argued:** the presenter's version would have turned **four existing rows red**, all four `Frame_*` rows M3-08a added, each asserting `_pause.IsPaused` against a fixture with no presenter in it — and would have edited the fixture whose 0/100 baseline had just cost 150 isolated runs. The ticker's version turned **zero** red. `RunPause` is not a dependency of the presenter and **`Construct_TakesNoRunPause` pins that at the constructor and over the private fields**, so the two-owners-with-an-`if` state deviation 12 exists to refuse cannot grow back without deleting a red row.

**Deviations:** 11, in the spec's [As built](tasks/M3-08b-level-up-screen.md#as-built).
