Beyond the ruling: **three Tests-table rows were inverted rather than deleted** — `Offer_PausesTheRun` → `Offer_TouchesNoPause` and two siblings — each holding a real `RunPause` the presenter is never handed and asserting it stays untouched, so the ruling is enforced rather than merely recorded. **`LocKey` has no `.Value`; it is `.Key`**, and the spec said `.Value` in four places, the ledger row in one, and none would have compiled — a known correction from M3-00c, now fixed in the spec, the ledger and the code together, with a row that fails if `LocKey` ever grows both. **`Tests.Game`'s asmdef gained `Unity.TextMeshPro`**: `UnityEngine.UI` resolves there unlisted because it is a built-in module and TMP is a package, and the alternative was reading every label through reflection on an untyped `Component`. **Rule 5's latch is cleared in `Update`, not in the event handler** — `ChooseOffer` repaints all three cards *inside* the call the first tap started, so a handler that cleared it would re-arm mid-command and a double tap would spend the second pick on a node the player never saw; uGUI dispatches both taps of one double tap from a single `EventSystem` pass, so only a new frame lifts it.

**Learned:** **the PlayMode number moved and is not being filed under the old alibi.** M3-08a's full run was 15/15; this task's is **14/15 on both runs**, failing `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` with M2-15a's exact message — the known intermittency, baselined at 10 % over 50 isolated runs. **2-for-2 at 10 % is about a 1 % coincidence**, so it is recorded as a change rather than as noise. Isolated, `FrameOrderTests` came back **8/8 green on three runs** — the *"green alone, red inside a full run"* signature the row carried for two tasks before M3-06 retired it. **What is new and worth naming: this is the first task since M3-02b to change `Scenes/Run.unity`**, and `BootSmokeTests` loads that scene in the same PlayMode process *before* `FrameOrderTests` runs — so one more full-screen `Canvas`, a `GraphicRaycaster`, three `Button`s and five TMP labels changing timing or GC in the shared process is exactly the *"something inside a single PlayMode run that moves between rows"* variable this row has never been able to name. **A lead for the diagnosis task, not a diagnosis**, and the isolated 8/8 is the evidence that the fixture itself is unchanged. **Two other things the tests corrected.** `Destroy_DropsSubscriptions` went red on the first run because **Unity does not call `OnDestroy` on an object whose `Awake` never ran**, and `Awake` does not run in EditMode — as first written it would have passed against a presenter with no `OnDestroy` at all, so the row now invokes the method and says why. And the fixture's tree holds **no Keystone and no Upgrade**, because `TreeRules` refuses a Keystone sharing a tier and `SkillSpec` refuses a parentless Upgrade — both found by running rather than by reading. **[Traps.md](../Traps.md) §7 gained a bullet**, on M3-08a's precedent: a `TestRunnerApi` left in a local is **collected mid-run** and takes its callbacks with it, so the suite finishes, every test executes, and `RunFinished` never fires — the results file is never written and the Editor afterwards reports idle, which reads as a hung run. It is length-dependent; five short runs worked before the first full one was lost, and **three runs were lost before it was understood**, which is what stopped the PlayMode measurement at n = 2.

**Follow-ups:** **none opened, none closed, and all nine checked row by row rather than taken from the spec's header — which was wrong again.** **Row 9 was not opened by this task**: `git log -S` puts its text in `ae7b853` (**M3-00b**) and M3-00d *recounted* it, which a row opened here could not have been. **Second consecutive task whose header mis-states a ledger fact** — M3-08a's said its ripple was one fixture and it was two. What this task actually did to row 9 is make it **materially worse in the sense that matters**: it is the first thing in the game that **draws an unresolved `LocKey` on a screen**, so the gap stopped being a sentence and became `skill.test.one.name` on a card, and `Card_DrawsTheKeyNotEnglish` pins it so M3-14a inverting it is a red row somebody argues with. **Row 6 does not close and does not change shape** — it goes from nine predicted readers to eight predicted and one real, `OfferCard`'s four `[SerializeField] Color` fields, exactly as M3-00b predicted; M3-13a's reflection row now has a genuine target. **Row 4 gains the largest single device question in the milestone** and cannot close: whether a hard stop mid-fight reads as a beat or a stutter, and whether a 200 × 260 dp card is tappable by the thumb that was on the stick — every number on that screen is a guess, with a test proving only that a non-finite one cannot reach a rect. **Rows 1, 2, 5, 8 unmoved; 3 and 7 remain closed.** Row 8 is worth one line: this task built the screen whose deliberation time is the gap between the two clocks M3-00d ruled on, so the divergence is now reachable in a playtest rather than only in principle.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-09a · The pause screen: the gate's second holder, and "pause anywhere, instantly"

**Built:** **`PauseReason.Menu` finally has a caller, and it is the second screen to stop the run — by a different route from the first, on purpose.** One new production file, `PausePresenter` (**block namespace**, Traps §5), plus `Prefabs/UI/Pause.prefab` on its own canvas at sorting order **50** — above the HUD's 0 so the panel covers the stick, below the level-up's 100 so rule 4's belt is a sorting order rather than a promise — `Scenes/Run.unity` dressed with it, and one optional `RegisterComponent` on `RunScope`. A top-right 44 dp `Button` inside a `SafeAreaFitter` raises `Pause(PauseReason.Menu)` and shows a panel; Resume lowers it; Quit lowers it **and then** asks `SceneLoader` for the Menu, leaving the run on disk. **Both halves live in this one file and M3-08b's live in neither of its own**, which is the same rule applied twice rather than two rules: the level-up gate is a once-a-frame pure function of `HasOffer` and belongs to `RunTicker`, and a pause *menu* has no core state behind it for a ticker to read. **1 465 EditMode / 0 / 0, twice consecutively** against M3-08b's 1 447 — **18 new rows, landing to the row**: the spec's Tests table is 16 named, plus two implied guards (a null row, and the non-finite row the two dp Inspector doors owe). No new spec type, so no validation row.

**Deviations:** 4, in the spec's [As built](tasks/M3-09a-pause-screen.md#as-built). The one that changes a decision: **`SceneLoader` is no longer `sealed` and `LoadAsync` is `virtual`**, so a recording loader can capture `timeScale` and the pause holder *inside* the call — which is the only place rule 7's *"lowered **before** the load is asked for"* is observable. Without it all three `Quit_*` rows were unwritable, because `SceneManager.LoadSceneAsync` takes the Editor's open scene with it (`ResumeFlowTests`' finding, and why its own two taps are still a manual step). An `ISceneLoader` port for one method with one adapter was rejected as a port that exists to be mocked. `ResumeFlowTests`' now-stale *"which is `sealed`"* clause was corrected with it. **`InstallerTests` needed no change** and the spec's *"if the field is required"* is answered *no*: it builds `RunInstaller`, not the `RunScope` MonoBehaviour, and the field is optional.

**Learned:** **`File.Delete` was refused and `TestRunnerApi.Execute` got the blame — for the third recorded time, and this session fell for it anyway.** [Traps §4](../Traps.md#4-the-unity-mcp) says the check is pre-execution, the message identifies nothing, and *"the innocent-looking call nearest the bottom gets the blame"*; M3-01b's bullet names `File.Delete` specifically. Three refused snippets all carried one, both "isolating" probes dropped it **and** the test call together, so the bisect was confounded and the wrong conclusion was filed. Re-run with `File.WriteAllText(path, "RUNNING")` as Traps §4 advises, the direct `Execute` ran clean. **The cost was a temporary Editor helper that should never have been written; the fix is to bisect one variable at a time.** Traps §4 already says all of it, so it is **spent** rather than filed again · **one EditMode run came back 1 464/1 and the failure was the MCP's own websocket**: `SaveWriterTests.Writer_ObservesEveryFault` subscribes to the process-wide `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException` and forces two GCs, so *any* unobserved faulted task finalised in that window trips it — the captured exception was `ClientWebSocket.ConnectAsyncCore`. Green on the two runs either side, unchanged code. **Traps §7**, beside the `BootSmokeTests` AI-Assistant row · **the `TimeManager.asset` diff is back and is still not a `timeScale` leak** — Unity 6.3 re-serialising `Fixed Timestep` into its rational form, `m_TimeScale` correctly 1; found and reverted, exactly as at M3-08a. **spent** · **PlayMode 14/15 then 15/15** — `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder`, M2-15a's exact message, inside the 10 % baseline. **This is the second consecutive task to change `Run.unity`, which is M3-08b's own named hypothesis**, and one red-then-green pair neither confirms nor retires it. **Known issues**.

**Follow-ups:** none opened, none closed. **Ledger row 4** gains the pause icon: whether a 44 dp target in the top-right corner is reachable mid-fight with the left thumb planted on the stick, and whether *instantly* survives the 30 fps drop. **Ledger row 9** gains two readers rather than none — the panel's two buttons are dressed with `ui.pause.resume` and `ui.pause.quit`, not English, because `HudPresenter`'s remarks call the raw-English exception closed at two places and `OfferCard` set the precedent one task ago; a row fails if either label stops being a key. **M3-09b** adds Skills to this panel and **M3-09c** adds View Tree, one button each, and `Panel_HasResumeAndQuitOnly` is the row they each move by one.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-09b · The Skills screen: every active you own, its trigger in words, and the switch

**Built:** three production files — `TriggerText` in `Core` (`TriggerUnit`, `KeyFor`, `UnitOf`), `SkillsPresenter` and `SkillRow` (both **block namespaces**) — plus `Skills.prefab` on its own canvas at sorting order **60**, between the pause panel's 50 and the level-up's 100, and every named small edit: `SkillRunner.EffectiveCooldownOf`, `RunState.SkillCooldownSeconds` (the eighth scalar read, the seal unmoved), `PausePresenter` + a Skills button and its handler, `Pause.prefab`'s third panel button, `RunScope._skillsPresenter`, `Run.unity` dressed. **The first screen in the game raised *over* a pause it does not own** — rule 10, and the reason M3-09a made `Close` and `IsOpen` public a task early. CC §6.2's full-slots prompt is asked by this screen instead of being refused by core, so `SkillRunner`'s fifth-slot throw is now unreachable in a live build and still loud for the caller that forgets to ask.

**Deviations:** 4, in the spec's *As built*. The one that changes a decision: **`UnitOf(FocusRampLevel)` answers `Count` because rule 2 says so, and `CombatBlackboard` declares that field a `float` in `[0, 1]`** — a 0.6 threshold would draw as *"1"*. Unreachable until M3-12 authors a trigger, one arm to move, pinned by a named assertion rather than quietly corrected. **The owner's call.**

**Learned:** **the wiring between two root prefabs is a decision, not a discovery** — a scene-dressed `_skillsScreen` on `PausePresenter`, with an optional `[Inject]` rejected because VContainer resolves every parameter or fails to compose the whole scope, and a back-reference rejected because the panel's interactability is the panel's. **spent (the As built carries it)** · **`Toggle.isOn` raises `onValueChanged` when it is *assigned*** — so a row repainted from `SkillAutoCastChanged` re-sends the command it is redrawing from, and a cancel that restores a switch sends the very command it refused. A paint latch is the fix; the shape generalises to every uGUI control with a settable value. **[Traps §7](../Traps.md#7-tests-nunit-and-measuring-allocation)** · **a `Canvas` instantiated in EditMode has never been driven by its own `CanvasScaler`**, so a stretched viewport's rect is whatever the last layout pass left — `Screen_ScrollsPastFive` pins it to rule 7's five rows instead of measuring it. **Traps §5's neighbourhood; spent here** · **`TestRunnerApi.Execute` ran clean directly**, with the results file overwritten by a `"RUNNING"` sentinel rather than deleted — M3-09a's trap, not repeated. **spent** · **`TimeManager.asset` was dirty again** (Unity re-serialising `Fixed Timestep`, `m_TimeScale` correctly 1), found and reverted: **three of the last four tasks**. **spent**

**Follow-ups:** none opened, none closed. **Ledger row 9** gains its sharper reader — a trigger line is a key *and* a formatted number, and `Card_DrawsTheKeyNotEnglish` pins the composition (`trigger.hpFraction.below 60 %`) so M6-10 has something to resolve rather than something to invent. **Ledger row 4** gains the 56 dp row and its switch. **M3-09d** adds View Tree to this panel and moves `Panel_HasResumeSkillsAndQuit`'s two counts from 3 and 4 to 4 and 5 — **M3-09a's comment named M3-09c and the spec files say otherwise**, so the row now names the task that actually holds the edit. M3-09c (profile v2) touches neither the panel nor this file.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-09c · Profile v2: the first thing the player keeps, and the hint that needs it

**Built:** two production files — `ProfileStore` in `Adapters` (file-scoped) and `FirstActiveHint` in `Presentation` (**block namespace**) — plus `FirstActiveHint.prefab` on its own canvas at sorting order **40**, above the HUD's 0 and below the pause panel's 50. `PlayerProfile` is **v2** with `SeenFirstActiveHint` and two `With` helpers, `SaveMigrations` grew the `if (version < 2)` step, `ProfileMirror` grew the key, and `HapticsSettings`, `BootFlow`, `BootInstaller` and `RunScope` took the named edits. **`RunSnapshot.CurrentVersion` stayed 3** — the two formats version independently (M2-13b), and this is the first time that has been more than a claim. The callout arms on the first `NodeTaken` of kind Active, shows on the next `LevelUpClosed`, spends the flag **on show** and saves it on that frame, takes no touches, and dismisses on a tap through `InputAdapter` or after four **unscaled** seconds.

**Deviations:** 5, in the spec's *As built*. Two that change a decision. **`HapticsSettings.Apply` is deleted and `BootFlow` takes a `ProfileStore` in its place** — once boot hands the profile to the store, a feature keeping its own copy of one field is the second holder rule 3 exists to remove, so the toggle now reads and writes straight through `ProfileStore.Current`. And **the arming condition compares `OwnedActiveCount` against 0, not the spec's 1**: that count is the *runner's*, and `ChooseOffer` feeds the runner **after** `SkillTree.Take` publishes `NodeTaken`, so a handler reads the count from before the node. Written as specced the callout never fired for a first Active and did fire for a second — nine rows red in one shape, which is how it was found. `Hint_SeesTheCountBeforeTheRunnerHasIt` now reads the count from inside a `NodeTaken` subscription and asserts 0, so that order moving is a red row rather than a missing callout.

**Learned:** **the bug a task is written to prevent is worth reproducing before you fix it.** `HapticsSettings`' setter was put back to M1-20's whole-struct write for one run, and `Haptics_ToggleKeepsTheHintFlag` came back red on the assertion that names the bug — *"toggling haptics erased the hint flag"* — with the other ten rows in the fixture green. Without that the row would have been a green assertion about code that was never wrong. The general rule it pins, written where the next writer of a profile field will read it: **a writer that knows one field must never author the whole DTO**, and the `With` helpers are what make the right thing the easy thing. Two reflection rows hold it at the two doors that could reopen it — a constructor overload that omits a field, and this class ever holding an `ISaveStore` again.

**Follow-ups:** **[Ledger row 2](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) closed** — `MigrateProfile` ran a step for the first time since M2-13b wrote it, the v1 profile literal is untouched and now decodes at v2 through the real adapter, and the harness has now been exercised on both formats. **Ledger row 9** gains its third and sharpest reader: a callout whose whole job is to tell the player something, currently telling them `ui.hint.firstActive`. **Three toolchain findings were written into [Traps](../Traps.md)**, which is what M3-09b did not do — §3's false negative on DLL timestamps (an unfocused Editor drained a six-assembly compile while every source still read newer than every assembly; the probe that answers is calling the new API from a command), and §4's `Type.GetType("…, Soulvail.Game")` route for dressing a `LifetimeScope`'s fields, the rule that a reimport and a `TestRunnerApi.Execute` must never share a command, and `groupNames` vs `testNames`.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-09d · The tree view: three branches, your path, and the two doors into it

**Built:** two production files — `TreeViewPresenter` in `Presentation` and `TreeNodeView` in `Controls`, both **block namespaces** — plus `TreeView.prefab` on its own canvas at sorting order **110**, above the level-up's 100. Twenty-seven cells in three columns, a tier to a row, the branch's `NameKey` over each column, read-only: this class takes no `IProgressionCommands` and no `IPlayerCommands`, holds no `RunPause`, subscribes to nothing and declares no `Update`, and a cell carries no `Selectable` at all. `RunState.IsNodeAvailable(ContentId)` is the **ninth** scalar read with the `internal` seal unmoved (AR §18.2); `PausePresenter` gained a fourth panel button and `LevelUpPresenter` the toggle M3-08b's *Out of scope* reserved; `RunScope` gained an optional `_treeViewPresenter`; `Run.unity` was dressed with the instance and **three** references. A class with no tree shows one line and **neither door offers its button**, which is every run in the build until M3-12.

**Deviations:** 4, in the spec's *As built*. The one that matters is **the ruling on `Open(Action onClosed)`, which M3-09b had rejected for the Skills screen.** It ships **as the spec wrote it, with M3-09b's poll kept underneath as the net** — the callback answers *which* door, which a poll cannot, and the poll guarantees *a* door, which a callback cannot. For the pause panel the two converge on `RefreshPanel`, so the callback only moves the restore a frame earlier; from the level-up screen it is the whole mechanism, and `Close_GivesTheDoorBackEvenWithNoCallback` is the row that would go red if either half were dropped. Every shipped comment that claimed the other was rewritten. Three named edits sit outside the Files table: `SkillTreeTests` took the three `State_*` rows (M3-09b's precedent — beside the object the read delegates to), `PausePresenterTests.Panel_HasResumeSkillsAndQuit` was **renamed in place** to `…SkillsTreeAndQuit` with its counts moved to **4 and 5**, and `Scenes/Run.unity` was dressed.

**Learned:** **"hidden, not destroyed" is a split in one method, and the sorting order is what stops it being load-bearing.** `LevelUpPresenter.HideScreen` dropped the root's alpha *and* hid every card; the tree needs the first half without the second, so it is now `Veil()` plus a card loop, and coming back is `ShowScreen()` with **no redraw** — repainting would re-arm cards a tap in the same frame had deliberately killed, which is M3-08b rule 5's failure arriving through a new door. That veil also means an alpha-0 canvas at 100 draws nothing and takes no touches, so the tree *could* have sorted at 70. It sorts at **110** anyway: that would have been a promise made in another file about its runtime state, where a sorting order is a fact about this asset, and the rule it keeps is that a screen sits above every screen it can be opened from.

**Follow-ups:** **Ledger rows 9, 6 and 4 all move and none closes.** Row **9** gains its **fourth and densest** reader — twenty-seven unresolved `LocKey`s on one screen against a card's three, which is the fact M3-15 should rule the whole row on rather than on the cards alone (M3-14a resolves them). Row **6** gains its **fifth** reader and the largest single one: `TreeNodeView` carries **seven** serialized `Color`s, three node states of its own plus OfferCard's four kind tints copied value for value — and a row now asserts the two files agree, so M3-13a inherits one answer rather than two. Row **4** gains the question the spec says is most likely to send this task back: whether twenty-seven cells — let alone twelve — fit a landscape safe area at a readable size, and whether rule 9's no-scroll bet survives a 6-inch screen. **`ProjectSettings/TimeManager.asset` turned up dirty again and was reverted — five of the last six tasks.** Next is **M3-10a**.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-10a · S1–S4: four buttons that are not the Charge button, and the tap that becomes a command

**Built:** three production files — **`ManualSkillButton`** and **`SkillBarPresenter`** (both **block namespaces**) and **`SkillSlotInput`** (file-scoped, like every other adapter) — plus four buttons on `Hud.prefab`, which had not been touched since **M2-12a**, a whole milestone. A slot with a skill in it draws a 60 dp button with a radial fill; an empty one is **not drawn at all** (CC §6.2), which is all four in every run this build can play. Small edits as specced: **`RunState.SlotCooldownFraction` and `IsSlotReady`**, the tenth and eleventh scalar reads with the `internal` seal unmoved (AR §18.2) and the slot → index mapping kept in core where `SkillRunner.TryIndexOf` already lives; `_skillSlots.Poll()` in `RunTicker.CommandPhase`; the registration; `RunScope._skillBar`; **AR §18.1's command-phase row**, which gains a row of its own rather than a line. **This is the other half of a feature that has been built and unreachable since M3-07a** — every owned Active has carried CC §6.1's switch and M3-09b's screen could flip it, and until now a skill moved to Manual had no button to fire it with.

**Deviations:** 1, in the spec's *As built*, plus five files the table does not list. **Rule 4's `Reset` is not built**, because the **Public API** block above it lists two members and nothing in the Files table could call a third: `Poll` clears unconditionally, and a press made after the run ended is never polled at all — `CommandPhase` sits below `RunTicker`'s `IsRunning` guard. The five files: `FrameOrderTests` and `ResumeFlowTests` (the twenty-first constructor argument, compiler-forced), `SkillRunnerTests` (the three `State_*` rows, beside the object the read delegates to), `Scenes/Run.unity` (**12 insertions, zero deletions** — one reference, against M3-09d's 170 at three), and a **carried doc fix from `dev`** correcting the Next-task row's *"first task since M3-08b"* to M2-12a. That last one is the owner's, not this task's.

**Learned:** **the tightest cluster CC §6.2 permits is bigger than it sounds, and the number is forced rather than chosen.** 60 dp buttons with 12 dp of clearance means no two centres closer than 72 dp, and four of those over the quarter turn from *left of the Charge* to *directly above it* need a radius of at least `72 / (2 · sin 15°) ≈ 139` — so the arc ships at **140 dp** and its top button sits ~236 dp above the safe area's bottom edge, on a landscape phone about 432 dp tall. Nothing in the spec predicted that, and it makes the reach question sharper than *"is 60 dp hittable"* → **[ledger row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)**. Second: **`GetComponentInParent<T>()` answers null on a correctly dressed prefab asset**, because every object on an asset reports `activeInHierarchy` false and the default overload filters on it — one red row, and the fix is `includeInactive: true` → **[Traps §5](../Traps.md)**. Third, and the one worth the most: **`FrameOrderTests`' standing exemption is gone.** Its class remarks had said since M2-11b that the commands phase is *"the one step not observed here"*, because its members reach core only through the Input System and that assembly does not reference it — `SkillSlotInput` reaches it through an `int` a button wrote, so AR §18.1's first row is now asserted end to end → **AR §18.1**.

**Follow-ups:** **Ledger rows 4 and 9 both move, row 8 is untouched, and none of the three closes.** Row **4** gains the milestone's sharpest device row — a thumb on the stick and a thumb on S3 at the same time, deferred since M1 and **the one row that risks a feature rather than a verdict** — plus the reach of a forced 140 dp arc. Row **9** gains its **fifth real reader and a correction to how the row described it**: the ledger says M3-14a closes the slot buttons because *"the key never fitted and a word does"*, and now that the button exists — a 60 dp circle with an auto-sizing 8–18 pt label — *"a word does"* is a **device** claim rather than a table one, so M3-15 has to look at a phone before ticking it and rule 9's icon is the named fallback. Row **8** is named as untouched: nothing here changes a clock or what a stage is measured with. **PlayMode was run for the first time since M3-09a and came back 16/16**, `Ticker_RunsTheStepsInOrder` green, against a pre-edit baseline of 8/8 taken on this branch before a line was written. Next is **M3-10b**.

### 2026-09-15 · M3-10b · The progression HUD: the XP strip, the level, the auto-cast row, and Overflow

**Built:** three production files, all **block namespaces** because all three are `MonoBehaviour`s (Traps §5) — **`XpBarView`**, a 4 dp strip across the whole top edge driven by `XpChanged` and nothing else; **`AutoCastRow`**, twelve 24 dp radial fills under the health bar, one for every Active the player is *not* casting themselves; and **`OverflowToast`**, two unscaled seconds of *"Overflow ×N"* and the only thing that ever says CH §5.2's Overflow happened. Small edits as specced: `HudPresenter` gained `_levelText`, a `LeveledUp` subscription and **a fourth rect in `Place()`** (rule 3 — the label is part of the row that method owns, not a fourth component); `Hud.prefab` gained the strip, the label, twelve cells and the toast, **the second consecutive task to change the one asset every screen sorts against**; `RunScope` gained three optional fields. **Rule 5's walk needed no twelfth `RunState` read** — `OwnedActiveCount` → `SkillIdAt` → `IsAutoCast` → `SkillCooldownFraction` all existed, and the `internal` seal on `Skills` did not move (AR §18.2). This class is the one *allowed* to ask about Auto; `Bar_KnowsNothingAboutAuto` keeps M3-10a's two classes out of it, and the two readouts are **disjoint by construction** so no cooldown is ever shown twice.

**Deviations:** 3, in the spec's *As built*, plus one file the table does not list. **The level label is optional rather than one of `HudPresenter.Start`'s named pieces** — `_fade`'s terms, because a HUD without one plays the same fight where a missing HP bar looks like a stopped game. **Its width is a `private const`, not a serialized field**, which is why no new non-finite row is owed on a `Place()` that has had no answer to one since M1-17 (`OverflowDamage`'s argument, applied to a layout). **Rule 9's reader count in the spec is stale** — *"six readers across three files"* predates M3-00d's recount — so the ledger was updated from the recount to six predicted and three real. The unlisted file is `Scenes/Run.unity`: **36 insertions, zero deletions**, three cross-prefab references onto the existing HUD instance, and mandatory rather than tidy — without it three `Start` guards throw in every run.

**Learned:** **a handler of `NodeTaken` sees the owned-active count from *before* the node, so membership cannot be rebuilt inside the event.** `LevelUpFlow.ChooseOffer` publishes through `SkillTree.Take` at `:239` and hands the spec to `SkillRunner.Add` at `:246`, so a row that rebuilt in the handler would miss the very cell it had just been told about — M3-09c's finding arriving at a second reader, and a *correctness* fix rather than a batching one. The two events set one flag and `Update` acts on it, which is where the fills are read anyway and which runs at a `timeScale` of 0, so the row is right before the level-up screen has even closed → **[AR §18.1](../Architecture.md#181-ordering)**. Second: **rule 9 gives this row four kind tints and rule 5 makes three of them unreachable**, because membership admits Actives and nothing else — so a live cell is always `_active`, the lookup is total anyway for `TreeNodeView.Frame`'s reason, and the row that proves all four differ has to reach the private lookup. Third: **driving a `Tick(float)` seam by hand proves the split, never the clock** — EditMode advances neither `deltaTime` nor `unscaledDeltaTime` — so `Overflow_ToastSurvivesAPause` walks the IL and asserts `OverflowToast` reads the unscaled one and never the scaled one.

**Follow-ups:** **Ledger rows 1, 4, 6 and 9 all move and none closes.** Row **1** gains the only thing that ever tells the player half of stage 30's answer happened — ≈ +28 % damage and +28 % max HP over 29 Overflow levels — and it still does not close, because nothing here raises a number; what changed is that the Overflow half will be *visible* at M3-12 rather than silently correct. Row **4** gains **two** more device rows and they are the smallest numbers in the build: whether a 4 dp strip on the top edge is visible at all beside a notch, and whether a 24 dp radial fill reads as a cooldown or as a dot — sharper than it sounds, because the cell carries no icon and no text, so the sweep is the only thing telling one cell from another. Row **6** gains its **third real reader**, with all four tints agreeing with `OfferCard`'s value for value so M3-13a inherits one answer across three files; nine predicted becomes **six predicted and three real**. Row **9** gains the **seventh reader, the one nobody can close** — a 24 dp cell holds neither a key nor a word, so M3-14a's table cannot fix it and only art can, which is **not** M3-10a's 60 dp button where a word plausibly fits and the open question is device legibility — plus an eighth that is trivially harmless, the toast, where *"Overflow ×14"* is mostly the number. Next is **M3-11a**.

### 2026-09-17 · M3-11a-i · The granted shield pool: a third pool, spent first, taken back per source

**Built:** `GrantedShieldPool` (internal, file-scoped, two parallel arrays and a count — `SkillRunner`'s shape) plus the small edits: `Health` gains **one field and zero new branches**, `RunState.PlayerGrantedShield` is the twelfth scalar read with the `internal` seal unmoved (AR §18.2), and `DebugOverlay` gains one `Append`. `ApplyDamage` now spends granted shield, then the Aegis, then HP; the Aegis's own numbers and its ring mean exactly what they meant before. **Wired to nothing** — nothing in this build can put a grant on a live player until M3-11a-ii's handler.

**Split:** **M3-11a was split before continuing, under its own pre-declared tripwire**, into `M3-11a-i` (this) and [`M3-11a-ii`](tasks/M3-11a-ii-bulwark-and-timed-effects.md). The proof is in M3-11a's own Tests table, not in any code: `Health_TwoSourcesStack` puts two sources in the pool, and rule 8 removes *what remains of that source* — so A=35, B=20, spend 20 leaves a total of 35 either way, but A's expiry removes **15** or **35** depending on who paid. One float cannot tell those apart. **Both shapes of the whole task breach a stated limit** — the table inside `Health` trips the one-field-one-branch tripwire (~479 → ~640 lines), the table in its own file is the sixth counted file against protocol rule 6's five — so the split is forced rather than chosen.

**Deviations:** three, all in the *As built*. `Restore` clears the pool as well as `Reset` (rule 9 has to be true of a resumed run, and `Restore` is the door one comes through). `State_ExposesTheGrantedShield` ships as its **reachable half** — a live run reads 0, correctly empty and saying so — because `RunState.Combat` is sealed and only M3-11a-ii's handler will ever grant; it lands in `SkillRunnerTests` because `HealthTests` has no `RunSession`. And `DebugOverlay.cs` is a sixth touched file the parent table did not carry, added under the owner's ruling so M3-11a's manual step 1 is runnable at all.

**Learned:** **the i-frame branch was the finding, and it would have shipped silently.** It read `toShield + toHp`, so a hit swallowed whole by a grant would have started **no i-frames** *and* left `_lastDamageAt` unmoved — the Aegis refilling straight through a fight, which is a buff to the class's signature mechanic that Bulwark was never meant to give. It reads `toGranted + toShield + toHp`. **And `toGranted` is deliberately absent from `DamageResult`:** `ToShield` means *the Aegis*, because CH §3.1's Martyr keystone sums that field, so folding a Bulwark's points in would quietly make Martyr scale with a skill it has nothing to do with. The cost is named rather than hidden — a fully absorbed hit publishes `PlayerDamaged(0, 0, unchanged, unchanged, blocked: false)` — and **M3-13b** is the task that has to tell that apart from a hit that did nothing.

**Four corrections to M3-11a, recorded in its *As built* so they travel with the children.** **`ShieldGranted` does not exist in code**, whatever [M3-00d's *Verified* row](#current-state) says: `grep` over `Assets/_Project` returns zero hits for it, `ShieldGrantExpired`, `GrantedShield` and `GrantShield`, and `CombatEvents.cs` holds eight events of which the only shield-shaped one is `PlayerShieldChanged`. M3-00d read the **spec set** — M3-11a's own *Public API* block — and recorded *"a spec declares it"* as *"code holds it"*; `EnemyDamaged.HpFraction`, the row's other half, is real. M3-11a-ii creates both events. **`IClock` cannot carry the deadline** (one member, `DateTimeOffset UtcNow`), so the handler takes the simulated `now`. **`Health_RefreshDoesNotRestoreSpentPoints` contradicted its own number** — 35 *is* the spent 20 handed back — and ships renamed as `Health_RefreshSetsTheSourceRatherThanStacking`, whose claim is that a refresh does not stack to 50. **Manual step 1 was unrunnable**, which is what the `DebugOverlay` line answers.

**Follow-ups:** **[Ledger row 2](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) checked and honoured by writing nothing for the sixth consecutive task, with a reason of its own rather than a copy of the fifth**: a cooldown was derivable from `TakenNodeIds` (M3-07b's ruling), a grant is derivable from **nothing on disk**, because the cast that made it is gone. `RunSnapshot.CurrentVersion` stays 3, no migration step is owed, and a resumed run comes back with no shield — a decision, written into `Restore`. **Row 4** gains an eight-entry per-frame walk beside the unmet `GC.Alloc` line. **Row 1 moves nothing**, plainly: Bulwark deals no damage and this half deals even less, so M3-12's TTK test must not count it. **Rows 6 and 9 are unmoved** — no `Color` and no `LocKey` reader, the first task this milestone to add neither. Next is **M3-11a-ii**.

### 2026-09-17 · M3-11a-ii · Bulwark, and the clock that takes a cast effect back

**Built:** **`TimedEffects`** — one table of sixteen, sorted by deadline, holding `(effect, source, expiresAt)` triples and calling `EffectRegistry.Remove` when a second comes round — plus **`GrantShield`** and its handler, **`GrantShieldDefinition`**, the two events `ShieldGranted` and `ShieldGrantExpired` **created rather than found**, and three lines in `RunSession`: the clock beside `State.Time += Dt`, the tick one line after the runner, and a `Clear` at the run's end. **The first production caller of `EffectRegistry.Remove` in a live run** (M3-05 rule 10's other half), and the first effect in the game that has to be *undone*. Nothing casts it: `Bulwark.asset` is M3-12c's, so every `Bulwark_*` row builds its own `SkillSpec` and this is the **sixth consecutive task whose feature needs M3-12**.

**Deviations:** four, all in the spec's *As built*. The one that changes a decision: **the handler takes a clock after all, and the spec's own two signatures could not both be true.** `IEffectHandler<T>.Apply` is `(TEffect, object)` — no time — while `TimedEffects.Hold` takes an *absolute* deadline, so something had to compute `now + Duration` and nothing in `Apply` could. Widening `Apply` changes every handler, both halves of the registry's dispatch and `ModifyStatHandler`, which wants no clock at all; letting `TimedEffects` remember `_now` from its own `Tick` — the house pattern — makes **every grant one frame stale**, because this class ticks *after* the caster rather than before it. So a one-field run-scoped **`SimulatedClock`** holds the number, written on the line beside `State.Time += Dt` and read by the one handler that needs it. `Hold` keeps the absolute deadline the spec declared; **rule 3's tick site is still one line**, and the clock line at `:705` is not part of it.

**Learned:** **the row the spec wrote could not be written, and the code says the opposite of what it says.** `Bulwark_CastsOnAnInboundBolt` asked for *"`SkillCast` then the grant"* — but `SkillRunner.Fire` applies the cast effects, starts the cooldown and **then** announces the cast (its rule 9), so what a tick actually holds is `ShieldGranted` → `SkillCast` → `PlayerDamaged`, and the row asserts that order. Its *"before `PlayerDamaged`"* half survived only because the fixture's class has **no Aegis** and a Spitter bolt is 70 against a 35-point grant: a hit an absorbing grant swallows whole publishes **no event at all** (M3-11a-i rule 8), so a row ordered against `PlayerDamaged` needs a bolt that spills — filed in that row's own comment.

**A second rule the spec did not state, and a recast depends on it:** **`Hold` refreshes a pair rather than queuing a second note.** `GrantedShieldPool.Grant` *sets* a source's contribution instead of adding to it, so a second note against the same `(effect, source)` would come due on the **first** cast's clock and cut the refreshed shield short — a Bulwark recast that left the player *less* protected than not recasting. One row, `Timed_HoldRefreshesRatherThanStacking`.

**The frame order was proved the way M3-06 proved its two, and that is why it is two rows rather than the spec's one.** A single row spanning both halves cannot be GREEN under either swap. Split, each is **RED under its own swap and GREEN under the other's** — measured, both times: the tick moved above `SkillRunner.Tick` fails `Timed_TicksAfterTheRunner` alone (12 passed, 1 failed), and moved below `ProjectileSystem.Tick` fails `Timed_TicksBeforeTheProjectileStep` alone (12 passed, 1 failed). Each row measures its own timings in a first pass and then arranges the coincidence it needs with **half a frame of margin**, because a deadline computed to land *on* an accumulated float is a coin toss — filed as **AR §18.1**'s new row.

**One fixture cost a cycle and it was not the one the spec warned about.** The `EnemySense.HasLineOfSight` trap was paid for in advance by copying M3-06's `SnapshotBuilder`-playing fixture, and a bolt landed on the first attempt. What went red twice was `Bulwark_RefiresAfterItsCooldown` with *"no run is running"*: **the arena is not one Spitter for long.** The director keeps composing waves, every body is reported back in firing position, and at CC §7's 140 hit points the run **ends on the third bolt** — at 900 it ends at about six seconds, still inside an eight-second cooldown. The fixture's class carries 100 000 hit points and says why; nothing in it reads a fraction.

**Follow-ups:** none. [Ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) moves **nothing** and the row says so out loud — Bulwark deals no damage, and M3-12's TTK test must not count it; [row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) gains **a sixteen-entry per-frame walk** beside the unmet `GC.Alloc` line, plus the feel question deferred **twice**, to M3-11c and to a phone; **rows 6 and 9 move neither**, which is the second task this milestone to move neither and the reason is the same one: no view, no `LocKey`, no colour.

### 2026-09-17 · M3-11b · Consecrate: ground you have to stand on

**Built:** **`ZoneSystem`** — eight places on the floor, parallel arrays and a count, each with a position, a radius, an absolute pulse schedule and an end — plus **`SpawnHealZone`** and its handler, **`SpawnHealZoneDefinition`**, the three events `ZoneSpawned` / `ZoneHealed` / `ZoneExpired`, and the small edits: `CombatBlackboard.PlayerPosition` written in `UpdateBlackboard`, `RunState.ActiveZoneCount` and `ZoneAt` behind an `internal Zones`, and three lines in `RunSession`. **It is the promise `TimedEffects` was built to keep, called in one task later**: a new primitive is one file and one `Register` line, and this one needs *less* than a grant, because a zone owns its own life and the handler's `Remove` does nothing. Nothing casts it — `Consecrate.asset` is M3-12c's — which makes this the **seventh consecutive task whose feature needs M3-12**.

**Four corrections to the spec, all ruled before a line was written, all in the *As built*.** **`IClock` is wrong for the second time in two tasks** and the handler takes M3-11a-ii's `SimulatedClock`; a `_now` remembered from this class's own `Tick` would be last frame's, because it ticks after the caster. **Rule 2's stated reason is false** — `RunState.PlayerPosition` is public and assigned before the skills step, so a position is not "read, used and forgotten" — and the blackboard ships anyway on the argument that is true: **`Spawn` happens inside `Apply`, which is not inside any `Tick` of `ZoneSystem`'s**, so `ProjectileSystem`'s parameter precedent cannot serve the moment that needs a position and route (b) drops no file. **The quarter bar is 106 and the order is now a rule**: pulses first, then the retirement, so a zone is alive up to and including its last instant — twelve pulses, not eleven. And **the events carry an `Id` rather than the spec's `Index`**: a retirement shifts the entries after it, so a view keyed by index would retire the wrong decal the first time two zones overlapped.

**Two things the spec did not ask for, and one row that ships as its reachable half.** `ZoneSystem.MaxPulses` (10 000) bounds the catch-up loop **at the door** — `Tick` lands every pulse a zone was alive for, so a six-second zone at a one-microsecond interval is a hang rather than a fast zone (AR §18.3) — and it is a real door rather than a formality: `Zone_AllocatesNothing` walked into it on the first run and had its million-second zones cut to four thousand. `Tick` also **refuses** a non-finite `now`, which is `ProjectileSystem`'s answer rather than `SkillRunner`'s. And `Zone_BoundaryLeavesItRunning` drives every call `StageFlow.Advance` makes to the player — including the wider `PlayerCombat.Reset` that method deliberately refuses — rather than a real boundary, because clearing a stage needs a kill, a kill needs a cone report from an adapter `Soulvail.Tests.Core` has no player for, and that fixture would be larger than the system under it.

**Learned: the fixture answer from M3-11a-ii is exactly wrong here, and the door is the save file.** That task sandbagged its class at 100 000 hit points because a Spitter is in the arena for the length of an eight-second cooldown; every row here reads HP as a *fraction* of 140, so a sandbag would destroy all three `Consecrate_*` rows. There is no public damage door — `RunState.Combat` is `internal` — so the live fixture ships **CC §7's 140, an empty roster and nothing that can hurt the player**, and starts each row at the hit points it needs through the **resumed snapshot**. A run resumed at 83 of 140 is the cleanest statement of "below 60 %" available, and 84 is the control.

**Both orderings were proved by swapping them, and the two swaps redden different things.** The tick line moved above `TimedEffects.Tick` fails **`Zone_TicksAfterTheTimedEffects` alone** (10 passed, 1 failed) — an EditMode row in `Tests.Core`, deliberately not in `FrameOrderTests`, so PlayMode stays at 16. The retirement moved above the pulse loop fails **four** rows and nothing else: the ruling's own `Zone_PulsesOnTheTickItExpires`, plus `Zone_ExpiresAfterItsDuration`, `Zone_PulsesAreAbsolute` and `Consecrate_HealsAQuarterBarIfYouStand` — which is the deduction from 36 hit points to 33 showing up in every row that counts a pulse.

**Follow-ups:** **[Ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) moves nothing, plainly, for the second task running** — a heal is survivability, Consecrate deals no damage, and M3-12's TTK test must exclude it exactly as it excludes Bulwark. **[Row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)** gains an eight-entry containment walk per frame beside the unmet `GC.Alloc` line, and **the feel question deferred twice — to M3-11c and to a phone — is a sharper one than Bulwark's**: not *does it read*, but **is standing still for six seconds survivable at stage 15 at all?** If it is not, the skill is a trap and the answer is a bigger radius rather than a bigger heal. **Rows 6 and 9 move neither, for the third consecutive task**, and the reason is the same one: no view, no `LocKey`, no `Color`. **One Traps row was written rather than spent**: §4 now carries the `RunCommand` wrapper shape, which cost four probes at M3-11a-ii. **And one inherited claim was corrected in six places rather than propagated a third time**: `Bulwark.asset` and `Consecrate.asset` are **M3-12c's**, not M3-12b's — M3-12c's own Tests table names both (`Bulwark_CarriesItsAuthoredNumbers`, `Consecrate_CarriesItsAuthoredNumbers`) where M3-12b is the two primitives — so M3-11a-ii's entry, its Current State row, and the two comments in `GrantShieldDefinition` and `GrantShieldTests` were fixed with this task's own. **Two of those six files are outside this task's table and the edit is one word each**, named here rather than left as a false pointer beside a true one. Next is **M3-11c**.

### 2026-09-17 · M3-11c · What a cast looks like, and the untested view that has been waiting for it

**Built:** **`ZoneView`** — `TelegraphRingView`'s body with a longer life and no fill, placed and sized from the event, flashing only when a pulse restored something — **`ZoneViews`**, the pool and the three subscriptions, **keyed by `Dictionary<int, ZoneView>` rather than by a list**, and **`BulwarkView`**, a shell on the player raised by `ShieldGranted` and lowered only when `ShieldGrantExpired.Total` reaches zero. Plus `PlayerAnimatorView`'s `Cast` trigger, `RunScope`'s `_zonePrefab` / `_bulwark`, `_zoneViews.Step(_snapshot.Dt)` beside the rings, **`VFX_ConsecrateZone.prefab`** and **`VFX_Bulwark.prefab`**, `Player.prefab`'s shell child and two references in `Run.unity`. **Ledger row 5 closes here**, and the two suites are the whole of why.

**Four corrections to the spec, all ruled before a line was written, and a fifth found by running.** **The zone events carry an id from 1, not an index** (M3-11b correction 4), so the census is a dictionary and `Zone_TwoZonesAreIndependent` retires the older of two overlapping zones — keyed by index that row draws the survivor back into the pool. **`Animator_DoesNotFlinchOnAKillingHit` tested the wrong thing**: `PlayerDamaged.ToHp` is damage *dealt to* HP, so the shipped `ToHp <= 0` branch means *nothing reached HP* — M3-11a-i's fully-absorbed grant — and it is renamed `Animator_DoesNotFlinchWhenNothingReachedHp`. **Whether a killing hit flinches was decided from the publish order rather than assumed: it does.** `PlayerCombat.ApplyDamage` publishes `PlayerDamaged` and only then `PlayerDied`, and `_dead` is set by nothing but the second — so the killing blow reaches a component that does not yet know it is over. Recorded as shipped behaviour in a row of its own, not corrected (rule 8). **An EditMode fixture can see a trigger fire** — probed before the suite was written: a controller built in the fixture gives `isInitialized`, and `SetTrigger`/`GetBool`, `SetFloat`, `SetBool` and `ResetTrigger` all round-trip in edit mode. **So all thirteen `Animator_*` rows are EditMode and PlayMode stays at 16.**

**The fifth correction cost a red run and is the one worth keeping.** `Animator_AttackSpeedIsDerivedFromTheInterval` cannot go green as written, and the reason is *not* that two synchronous publishes share an instant — it is that **`Time.time` is 0 inside an EditMode run**. `OnAttacked` writes the multiplier only when the previous swing was at a positive `Time.time` and this one is later, so with the clock at zero **no assignment of `_lastAttackTime` satisfies both and the write is unreachable from EditMode entirely** — the division, the ceiling of 6, and every value the parameter can take. A first draft primed the previous swing from `Time.time − 0.001` on the strength of a `RunCommand` probe that read `Time.time` as **0.02**; it came back **inconclusive** on its own `Assume`, which is how the real number was found. The ruling is a narrowed EditMode claim rather than a PlayMode file (a sixth counted file, and a split): **`Animator_AttackSpeedIsUnreachableFromAnEditorClock` asserts the clock**, so the day it advances the row goes red and tells the next reader the ceiling has become testable.

**Three deviations outside the Files table, two of them unpredicted.** `RunTicker`'s constructor gaining `ZoneViews` rippled into **`FrameOrderTests.cs`** as expected — and into **`ResumeFlowTests.cs`** and **`SkillBarPresenterTests.cs`**, two more sites in `Soulvail.Tests.Game` nobody had named. All three take an empty census, prewarm 0, for the rings' reason. None adds a row. `ZoneViews`' constructor is `TelegraphRings`' `(IObjectResolver, prefab, Transform, DomainEventHub, prewarm)` rather than the spec's `(DomainEventHub, ViewPool<ZoneView>)`, which nothing registers, and `IPoolable` is `OnSpawn`/`OnDespawn` rather than the spec's `OnReturnedToPool`. `BulwarkView` owns an `Update` calling a public `Step(dt)` — the fade is 0.15 s of cosmetics core never timed, so the wall clock is the honest clock for it, and `ZoneView` keeps the no-`Update` rule the ticker's step depends on.

**Learned: the colour initialisers are white on purpose, and that is Traps §7 obeyed rather than a placeholder left behind.** Both classes initialise `_colour` to `Color.white` and both prefabs author `#22D3EE`, so `Views_UseNoDangerColour` asserts the authored value as well as the absence of `#FF4A1F` — a row that only ruled out the danger colour would pass whether or not the serialized field ever bound, which is precisely the failure it exists to catch. **Ledger row 6 now has four of its readers as real code rather than three**, and the count it should be sized against is unchanged.

**Follow-ups:** **[Ledger row 5](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) closes here, and this task is its last owner** — the row named M3-11c and nothing else, `PlayerAnimatorView` now has fourteen rows, and `BulwarkView` is the second Animator-adjacent driver arriving *with* tests, which is rule 9's whole point. **[Row 6](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)** takes its two predicted readers into real code (eight across five files, unchanged in size), and rule 10's hard rule is now a red test rather than a sentence. **[Row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)** gains two more pooled additive floor-and-body effects on a phone's fill rate — and **the two feel questions deferred twice are, from today, askable**: whether a shield that appears before the bolt reads as the skill working, and whether six seconds of standing still is survivable at stage 15. Only a phone can answer either, and both need M3-12c's tree first.

### 2026-09-17 · M3-12a · Five numbers a node can reach: the stats M3-05 deferred

**Built:** the enum is **eleven members**, not six. `Weapon.Range` and `Weapon.ConeAngleDeg` stopped being forwarded floats, `ChargeSkill.Damage` was promoted off the spec at the line `ResolveChargeHits` has flagged since M1-15, `Health.ShieldRechargeDelay` became the one reachable Aegis number, and **`PlayerCombat.HealPerKill` was added outright at base 0** — paid by `EnemySystem.PendingKills` / `DrainKills()`, `DrainXp`'s shape exactly, drained one line below it in `RunSession.Tick`. **It is the first task in nine whose feature the owner can actually see**: `ModifyStatDefinition` already serializes `PlayerStat` as an enum, so `WeaponConeAngle +50 %` is authorable from the Inspector the moment this compiles, and manual step 2 is runnable today with three hand-authored assets and no code.

**Three rulings the spec did not make, all made before a line was written.** **File count: 4** — `Weapon`, `PlayerCombat` and the two test files — which is size M's floor and agrees with the spec's own header; `Health`, `EnemySystem`, `ChargeSkill`, `PlayerStat` and `RunSession` are all additive and none counts. `PlayerCombat` was counted although every one of its five sites is additive or a one-expression swap, because under-counting to 3 would have contradicted the spec's declared size. **No split.** **`Cone_ClampsAnAbsurdAngle` asked for two answers to two invalid values and NaN got the more permissive one; it now gets the less.** 400 → 360, −10 → **0**, and a non-finite angle → 0, because answering NaN with 360 draws the widest wedge in the game for a number nobody can read and quietly turns the Censer omnidirectional. Rule 7's `(0, 360]` becomes `[0, 360]`. **The refusal is a zero-angle intent, not a suppressed one** — the swing has already been announced by then, and suppressing the intent would strand `PendingConeRequestId` waiting on a report that can never arrive.

**The third ruling is a finding the spec does not have, and it is now measured rather than argued.** `Stat` computes `(Base + ΣFlat) × (1 + ΣPercentAdd) × Π(1 + PercentMult)` — checked in `Recompute`, not assumed — so **`HealPerKill`'s base of 0 makes every percentage kind multiply into zero, and `ModifierKind.Flat` is the only kind that can ever move it.** A probe read +500 % on a base-0 stat as **0** and +2 Flat as **2**. **M3-12c's Retribution must be authored Flat or it heals nothing, silently, with no error anywhere.** The base stays 0 — a non-zero one would make every class in the game heal on kill, which is a balance change no document asks for — so the constraint is written in `HealPerKill`'s own remarks where M3-12c's author reads it, in `PlayerStat.HealPerKill`'s, **and pinned by two rows** rather than left as prose.

**Two corrections found by running rather than by reading.** `Cone_ClampsAnAbsurdAngle`'s NaN third asserted the wrong shape and cost one red run: **`Stat.Base` and `Modifier` both *throw* on a non-finite input** rather than swallowing it, so a NaN cannot reach the swing through either ordinary door and the clamp is a backstop against arithmetic overflow inside the stack. The row now asserts both refusals, and `Cone_ANegativeProductHitsNothingRatherThanBecomingACircle` reaches the same branch the only way a shipped modifier can — a `PercentMult` below −1, which ADR-0008 explicitly permits. And **the spec's `Cone_ClampsANegativeRange` as written is unobservable**: the in-range test and the intent read the same clamped reach, so a negative range stops the swing rather than throwing one that reaches nothing. Shipped as two rows — the intent's 0 is reached by driving the reach negative *mid-swing*, which is `Weapon`'s documented "the damage frame belongs to the swing, not to the target" — and that is strictly better than the row asked for.

**Learned: there was no sixth reader, and the ripple stopped at one assembly pair.** `Weapon.Range` and `ConeAngleDeg` had exactly the three readers the owner named — `PlayerCombat` lines 942, 943 and 980 — and `ConeOverlapQuery` reads `ConeHitIntent.Range`/`.AngleDeg`, which are floats on the intent and did not change. **Fourteen files across two assemblies, `Soulvail.Core` and `Soulvail.Tests.Core`**, with no third. **No prefab and no scene moved.** The compile confirmation was built from `Stat.Base`, `Value` and `ModifierCount` rather than from a swing, because every class this task touches is core-only and a `RunCommand` can name no `System.Numerics` vector (Traps §4).

**Follow-ups:** **[Ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) gains the thing it has been waiting for and does not close, and the distinction matters.** This is the first task that makes the damage-touching half of the tree **reachable at all** — four of the five are what that half is made of — but **it moves no number in any run this build plays**: every base is exactly what the assets say, every new stat ships with zero modifiers, and nothing authors a node. **M3-12c still owes the TTK test at stage 1 / 15 / 30**, and a task that adds reach is easy to mistake for a task that closes the row. **[Row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)** gains **five `Stat.Value` reads a frame where five float reads used to be**, all on the swing path, beside the `GC.Alloc` line unmet since M1-21. **No `AllocationAssert` row was added, deliberately:** a `Stat.Value` read on a clean cache is a field read and `Handler_AllocatesNothingAfterWarmUp` already pins the modifier path, so a new row would assert what four existing rows assert and still not be the Profiler line on a phone. Rows 5, 6 and 9 move nothing — no view, no `Color`, no `LocKey`. Next is **M3-12b**.

### 2026-09-17 · M3-12b · Two primitives that are not a stat: a skill's own cooldown, and a swing that shoves

**Built:** **the two things v1's twelve nodes want that a `PlayerStat` cannot express.** `ModifySkillCooldown` names a *skill* rather than a number and resolves through `SkillRunner.TryIndexOf` → `CooldownOf` onto the runner's own `Stat` — M3-06 rule 12's promised pair, arriving as a file rather than a refactor — and `KnockbackOnSwing` lifts a new base-0 `PlayerCombat.SwingKnockback` off zero, after which `ResolveConeHits` emits an `EnemyKnockbackIntent` per enemy the swing reached. Both `Definition`s ship with **block namespaces** (Traps §5). Five new files and three additive production edits; `RunSession.Start` registers both beside `ModifyStatHandler`.

**Three rulings before a line was written, because the file count started at the *ceiling*.** **Five is the Files table's own count of new code files**, so the question was never *"is this one task"* but *"does any edit make it six"* — `PlayerCombat`, `SkillRunner` and `RunSession` were each ruled additive out loud, and the pre-named split into **M3-12b-i / M3-12b-ii was not taken**. **Rule 3 has no mechanism today and one was built:** `SkillRunner.Add` publishes nothing, so it gains `internal WatchArrivals(Action<ContentId, Stat>)`, invoked **last**; the flush-at-the-seam alternative drags `LevelUpFlow` in as a fourth production edit and a domain event is banned outright. **And the `Definition_*` rows cannot live where the spec puts them** — Tests.Core cannot see a `ScriptableObject`, so they went to `SkillAuthoringTests` and were renamed, because `Definition_Invalid_NamesTheAsset` is already taken there by `SpawnHealZone`.

**The finding the spec does not have: rule 3's pending path is not an edge case — it is the path every resumed run takes.** `RunSession.Start` replays the whole tree through `SkillTree.Restore` and only *then* pushes the resumed run's Actives into the runner, so a cooldown node is applied to an **empty** runner on every `Continue`, whatever order it was taken in. The Passive-carrying-a-cooldown-node case rule 3 argues from is the *rarer* one. `Session_RegistersBothHandlers` drives it end to end through a real session and reads **12 s → 9 s**, which is manual step 1's own number.

**The ordering claim is measured rather than asserted.** Moved above `_cooldowns[_count] = new Stat(...)`, the hook reddens **exactly four** rows — `Cooldown_LandsWhenTheSkillArrives`, `Cooldown_PendingIsSpentOnce`, `Cooldown_RemoveLeavesAnotherSourceAlone`, `Session_RegistersBothHandlers` — and leaves the other **sixteen** in that fixture green, which is what makes those four the rows the line belongs to.

**Ruled: the shove is the swing's facing, and rule 7 contradicts its own test row.** The prose says *"the direction the swing faced, which is what `ResolveChargeHits` already does"* — one direction for everything one sweep catches — while `Swing_ShovesAwayFromThePlayer` stands a Husk to each side and asks for the **radial** answer the same rule rejects two sentences later. Shipped as the facing, **renamed `Swing_ShovesTheWayTheSwingFaced`**, and made discriminating: as written it would have gone green against either implementation. `ResolveConeHits` had no facing in scope, and it is a **field written on the damage frame beside `PendingConeRequestId`** rather than a parameter — a parameter would make the body remember core's geometry across a round trip and `RunSession`, `IRunCommands` and `RunTicker` all carry it back.

**`SwingKnockback` is a second base-0 `Stat` and it carries M3-12a's warning verbatim — and is pinned rather than left to the argument.** Rule 6's *"wrong authoring is impossible"* holds for the doors that exist (one number, no kind, the handler picks `Flat`, and `Charge_KnockbackIsNotAddressable` already bars the enum), but the property is `public` and the arithmetic will not have changed the day a second door reaches it. `Swing_TheHandlerPicksTheOnlyKindThatCanWork` asserts **1.5 off one modifier on a base of zero**, which only `Flat` can produce — behavioural proof, and the row that reddens if anyone "tidies" the handler into taking an authored kind.

**Learned: a probe can implement `IIntentSink` whole, and Traps §4 is narrower than filed.** *"No `System.Numerics` reference, so no command can call a core method taking a `Vector2`"* is true of `Vector2` itself and **not** of a struct that merely holds one — `ConeHitIntent`, `ChargeIntent` and `EnemyKnockbackIntent` all compiled inside a `RunCommand`, because the signatures name only Soulvail types. That is what let the knockback half be probed against a real `PlayerCombat` → **Traps §4**, one bullet narrowed. Two other rows held: the rewriter hoisting a nested class cost one probe, and `ActiveSpec` **refuses an empty `onCast`** (*"an active must do something when it fires"*), found by running rather than by reading, so every fixture Active carries a `ModifyStat`.

**Follow-ups:** **[ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) *moves* rather than only gaining reach, and it gains a condition on how M3-12c may close it.** Both primitives raise **effective DPS without raising damage per hit**, so **M3-12c's TTK test must state the path it measures and exclude these two in its own text** — a test that counted them would **pass while the tree did nothing**. Nothing else is outstanding from M3-12a or M3-12b, so the row closes with M3-12c **provided its test states its exclusions**, and does not close if it merely makes them. **Row 4** gains one `Stat.Value` read and one comparison per swing, plus one delegate call per `Add`. Rows 2, 5, 6, 8 and 9 checked row by row and unmoved: nothing written to disk and no version bumped, no Animator, nothing drawn, nothing paused, and what this hands out is `ContentId`s rather than `LocKey`s. **1 802 EditMode / 0 / 0 twice on the final code, PlayMode 16/16, four assemblies, no prefab and no scene moved.** Next is **M3-12c**.

### 2026-09-18 · M3-12c · The Oathbound tree v1: twelve nodes, and the number ledger row 1 has been waiting for

**Built:** **the first content a player can be offered.** Twenty-six assets — twelve `SkillDefinition` nodes in three branches of two tiers of two, thirteen `EffectDefinition`s behind them (thirteen, because Unbowed carries two), and `OathboundTree.asset` — plus `BootScope.prefab`'s `_skills` and `_trees` arrays, **empty since M3-02b and filled here**, which makes `TryGetTreeFor(character.oathbound)` answer **true for the first time in the project's life**. Two test files: `TimeToKillTests` (Tests.Core, 8 rows) and `OathboundTreeTests` (Tests.Game, 23 rows).

**Deviations:** **one, and it is the count assertion in the wrong file.** The Files table named `InstallerTests` *"if a count is asserted"*; the count is asserted in `SkillAuthoringTests.Boot_ScopeCarriesTheTwoLists`, which has read `arraySize == 0` since M3-02b. **Inverted rather than deleted** — rule 7's claim is that the arrays stop being empty here, so a row that stopped asking would stop saying so. `InstallerTests` was not touched: it passes `Array.Empty<>` explicitly and never reads the prefab. **Two more outside the table, both forced by the pre-commit hook rather than chosen:** `CLAUDE.md` and `Docs/Traps.md` each asserted there is no .NET SDK on this machine, which stopped being true before this task — corrected in place, two sentences each. Full account, plus the four rulings this task owed before it could author anything, in the spec's *As built*.

**Learned:** **`ContentId` refuses camelCase and seven of the spec's own twelve ids could not be constructed** — M3-02b's recorded finding, ruled before authoring: all seven ship kebab-case, and CLAUDE.md's *"a file name matches the last segment of its id"* becomes a **mapping** (`TemperedVow.asset` ↔ `skill.oathbound.tempered-vow`), which is what M3-14b's sweep must implement — **[ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3)**. **`LocKey` forbids only whitespace, so the twenty-four keys were legal either way and were deliberately not touched** — the asymmetry is the trap — spent. **`AssetDatabase.CreateAsset` works from a `RunCommand` and so does the plural `DeleteAssets`**, probed on one throwaway before twenty-six were authored — **[Traps §4](../Traps.md)**. **A wrong enum ordinal in an authored asset is invisible to every check the project has:** Bulwark shipped with `EnemiesWithin8m` where `IncomingProjectiles` was meant — it compiles, converts, and is a legal Active that fires at the wrong moment for ever. Caught by **printing the converted spec from a probe**, not by a green suite — spent, and pinned by a test. **`RunSession.Start` refuses a catalog missing any enemy the mode's roster names**, which reddened three rows on the first run — that check working — spent. **The .NET SDK is installed now (10.0.401) and the pre-commit format check has stopped self-skipping** — this is the first task it ever refused, over single-line `case X: y++; break;` arms. CLAUDE.md and [Traps §5](../Traps.md) both said there was no SDK and both are corrected; the whole of `Assets/_Project` passes, so a future failure is that task's own — **[Traps §5](../Traps.md)**.

**Follow-ups:** **[ledger row 1](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) CLOSES on the test, gated on the playtest.** 3 / 4 / 5 hits at stages 1 / 15 / 30 against the **shipped** curve — which corrects this row's own table from 66 / 99 to **66.24 / 98.64** — with 8 for the unlevelled control. **The condition M3-12b attached is met in the test's own text**: `Ttk_ExcludesSurvivabilityAndReach` names all six exclusions in code and proves none can address `WeaponDamage`. **It does not invert.** **The warning is recorded**: it holds at stage 30 because of **Overflow, not the tree**, so M7-04 owes a re-measure. M3-15 still owes the played counts; **M3-14b still owes the starve check**. **[Row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) becomes concrete and does not close** — twenty-four node keys plus three branch keys ship, the tree view now draws twenty-seven strings on one screen, and M3-14a owes English for all of them. **Row 8** gains a sentence: this is the task that makes its *"M3 changes the thing it measures"* true in the build. Next is **M3-13a**.

### 2026-09-18 · M3-13a · `Palette`: GD §16.4 in one file, and the nine readers that have been waiting for it

**Built:** **the colour language, in the one place the rule can be enforced from.** `Palette` is fifteen `static readonly Color`s — GD §16.4's five reserved meanings at the hex the document names, CH §4's four kind tints, M3-09d's three node states, and the four derived readouts — plus `IsDanger`, which is how *"for nothing else, ever"* stops being a sentence. Nine readers each lose their copy: `ThreatArrows` loses both statics **with no forwarder**, `TelegraphRings` stops reaching across the `Views`/`Presentation` seam, and seven views lose **seventeen serialized `Color` fields** between them.

**Ruled before writing: it does not split, and the criterion is the ROADMAP's own.** Sixteen files, but **two new code files and zero substantial rewrites** — every production edit is one field deleted and one expression repointed, with no line of behaviour changed. The objection that a *subtractive* edit is not the *additive* one the rule's examples name cuts the other way: a class ending with fewer members is a smaller edit, not a larger one. **What decided it is that every seam is disqualifying** — any cut leaves `Palette` shipping eleven of fifteen members with no reader and leaves `Views_CarryNoSerializedColour` unwritable until the second half, so ledger row 6 would close on the *second* PR whichever way the files fell, and a task whose point is *"the number exists once"* would ship a state in which it exists twice.

**The finding the spec does not have, and it is the drift row 6 exists to catch, arrived at before the palette: there are two player cyans in the build.** `ReticleView`, `FocusGlowView` and `PlayerView._swingColour` each carry a serialized cyan nothing had counted, so the honest reader count is **twelve files, not nine** — and theirs is **`#4CE6FF`** against the palette's `#22D3EE`, measured off `Player.prefab` and `Reticle.prefab` rather than off the initialisers. **Ruled out of scope**: moving them recolours the reticle, the focus glow and the swing arc on every frame, which manual step 2 forbids. `Views_CarryNoSerializedColour` is scoped to the nine **by name** and asserts the three are not in the list; `Palette_IsNotTheOnlyCyanInTheBuild` pins all three. Now a [parking-lot](ROADMAP.md#parking-lot) line, because no task owns which cyan a world-space view should be.

**Ruled: `HeldFocus` is `Player` byte for byte, and the spec's *"dimmer"* was about the wrong thing.** It and `HpBarView._fillColour` were already identical, and `HeldFocus_IsDimmerThanDanger` passed on 0.933 against 1.0 — green for a reason unrelated to its name. **What reads dimmer is `ThreatArrows.Place` ramping alpha by distance**, and an out-of-range focus is far away by definition, so the comment was corrected and the value was not. **No *"every member is distinct"* row was written**: `Player`, `Heal` and `HeldFocus` are all `#22D3EE` on purpose.

**Ruled: `EnemyLook.BoneGrey` stays content and `Neutral` takes its value.** That field is `M_BoneGrey`'s own number written down twice on purpose, pinned by `EnemyLookTests` against the shipped material; folding it in would mean retuning the palette silently recoloured every unauthored archetype. `EnemyLook.cs` was not edited and `Neutral_AgreesWithTheEnemyDefault` pins the agreement. **Ruled: the AR row is §7, not §18.3** — §18.3 is *"Numbers and identity"* and this is a rule about what may be **written**, so it sits beside the ban it is the exception to.

**Three predictions were wrong on the facts and were measured rather than repeated.** **The ripple is nothing**: `Run.unity` holds both components only as **stripped** references carrying no field data, and Unity 6.3 **keeps** a removed field's YAML line — `ForceReserializeAssets` on `VFX_ConsecrateZone.prefab` produced a **zero-byte diff** with `_colour:` still on line 107. So no prefab and no scene moved, the stale lines are dead text, and **manual step 1 still passes**: a `SerializedObject` walk of all seven components reports no colour property, so the Inspector shows no swatch. **Two more test files break than anyone counted** — `ThreatArrowsTests` and `TelegraphRingsTests` both read `ThreatArrows.Danger`. **And the rewritten rows are five, not four**: `Tree_TintsByKind` compared `TreeNodeView`'s tints against `OfferCard`'s field by field and could not survive the fields going.

**The white initialisers were read from the YAML, which is what rule 5's argument depends on.** Both were `Color.white` in C# and both prefabs were dressed `#22D3EE`; `Palette.Heal` and the Bulwark shell are that, not white. **Four values moved and nothing else did**, each by less than half of one 255th and each landing on the byte it already had: the five reserved colours carry GD §16.4's exact hex while the derived sets keep the exact floats the build shipped, because they answer to no authored hex. `IsDanger` compares at eight bits for that reason and answers **true for both spellings of `#FF4A1F`** — probed.

**Follow-ups:** **[ledger row 6](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) CLOSES, at nine readers — and the row says so, because a row that closes on a count it got wrong twice closes on nothing.** What closes it is a rule rather than a count: `Danger_IsUsedByNothingElse` reflects over every member and asserts exactly one is `#FF4A1F`, where M3-11c could only test two prefabs. **The tenth colour now costs one field and one row** — `Veilrot` and `Essence` ship with no reader at all and `Palette_HasTheColoursNobodyReadsYet` asserts it — and **M3-13b's damage tint is the first caller that will test that door**, by design the hard case: GD §16.2 wants a dying enemy to shift *"toward red"* and this row is what refuses `#FF4A1F` for it.

### 2026-09-18 · M3-13b · Health that reads at a glance: the damage tint, the Elite's bar, and the shield you were granted

**Built:** **all four rows of GD §16.2's table.** A basic enemy darkens toward `Palette.EnemyDying` `#3B2422` as its hit points fall — composed inside `EnemyHitFeedback`, which already owns the body's colour — and earns a 28 × 3 dp bar on each hit that fades 2.25 s later; an Elite's bar is up from the rental and never leaves; and the player's HP bar finally draws the shield Bulwark grants, as a `Palette.PlayerBlocked` segment ahead of the fill, in absorption order, driven by `ShieldGranted` and `ShieldGrantExpired`. Elite-ness rides `EnemySpawned` as a one-field core change, because `EnemyViews` holds a look book and no catalog — and because M7-02's Elites are made at spawn rather than authored.

**Ruled before writing, all five.** **Size is five** — `EnemyHealthBar`, three new test fixtures and one substantial rewrite — which is size M at its ceiling and does not split; the spec's Files table was short by two test files, because `EnemyHitFeedbackTests` never existed and the nine `Player_` rows had no named home. **The tripwire did not fire**: the tint is a third term, not a second writer of `_baseColor`. **`IsElite` is defaulted**, so the ripple is one file and the PR is three assemblies rather than four, with PlayMode out of the blast radius entirely.

**The 0.15 band means *not all three channels*, and the other reading is red on shipped content.** The Bloater ships 0.051 from `Danger`'s green and 0.098 from its blue **before any tint**, so *"no channel within 0.15"* fails on an untouched body at full health. **Red is the whole margin** — worst case 0.361 over three archetypes × 21 steps, 2.4× the band — because the Bloater's green crosses `Danger`'s exactly on the way down.

**The ordering was proved by swapping it, and the spec over-counted.** The rejected arrangement reddens **exactly one** row, `Tint_ResetForgetsIt`. `Tint_ArchetypeLookRebasesIt` is green under both, because the rental assigns `_liveColour` outright either way — it pins the rental being absolute, not the choice between shapes.

**Rule 9's *"oriented once"* rests on a false premise.** `EnemyView.Face` yaws the **root** every tick, so a bar parented to the body cannot hold a rotation written at `Start`. Kept in intent: aligned in `LateUpdate`, only while shown and only when the body actually turned. A standing body still costs one quaternion for its whole life.

**A red row found a real design bug and the rule changed, not the row.** Clamping the shield segment collapses it to nothing at full health — exactly when Bulwark grants one — so the width is preserved and the *start* gives way instead.

**Deviations:** four, in the spec's *As built*. The one that changes a decision: **`Tests/Game/Presentation/GrantedShieldTests.cs` is a third new test file the table does not name**, because no fixture for `HpBarView` or `HudPresenter` has ever existed.

**Learned:** a `MaterialPropertyBlock` round-trips a channel at ~1 ULP, so `Color.Equals` is false on a value nothing changed while `operator ==` is true — and NUnit uses the former; four rows went red on it — Traps §7. A command cannot construct `EnemySpawned`: its ctor names `System.Numerics.Vector3` — Traps §4, already filed.

**Follow-ups:** **[ledger row 6](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) stays closed and the price held — one field, one row.** `EnemyHealthBar` is deliberately *not* added to `Views_CarryNoSerializedColour`'s historical nine and carries its own guard instead; the day a third view is written, someone has to know to copy it. **[Row 4](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) gains four**: a 3 dp bar at six inches, peripheral legibility of the tint (GD §16.2's entire claim for it), twenty-eight world-space canvases at the concurrency cap, and the finding that a full-health Husk's body is `Palette.Neutral` — the bar's own fill colour, bit for bit. The GD §16.2 ↔ §16.4 contradiction is **flagged for the owner, not edited**; M3-15 rule 9 resolves it.

### 2026-09-18 · M3-14a · `ILocalizer`, `TableLocalizer`, and one English table

**Built:** the AR §6 port that had never existed as a file, a `LocalizationTable` `ScriptableObject`, the adapter over it, and `English.asset` at **63 rows** — registered eagerly in `BootScope` as `ILocalizer` only. Six readers stop drawing keys: the offer card, the Skills row *and its trigger line*, the tree view's twelve cells and three branch headings, the first-active hint, the Overflow toast and the 60 dp slot button. Five raw English strings leave `Menu.unity` and `Hud.prefab`. **Every screen in the game is legible for the first time.**

**The spec's own key spelling is wrong and the failure is silent by design.** Rule 7 says `.desc`; the assets say `.description`, kebab-case. A table typed to the spec ships **24 dead rows and a green suite**, because rule 1 makes a miss return the key — so rule 1 is precisely what makes rule 7 untestable here. The rows were **generated by reading `_nameKey`/`_descriptionKey` off the twelve assets**, never typed, and probed: 24 of 24 resolve, while `…consecrate.desc` answers `Has=false`. **Coverage stays unasserted on purpose — M3-14b owns it**, and `Has` is the door it will call.

**Rule 8 counts four raw strings and there are five:** `Continue` arrived with M3-07b's resume flow and neither rule 8 nor the ROADMAP's parking-lot line counts it. It is a key too, because AR §11.5 is unenforceable if it is exempt; **the ROADMAP's count is the owner's to correct.**

**GD §13.1's one-line rule is unsatisfiable at the shipped card, and that is about the card.** Measured: the description label is **176 dp at 18 pt — about twenty characters a line**, so **10 of 12 wrap to two lines and one to three**. Three rows were shortened here first and the count did not move. The label's box is 176 × 150 dp, room for ~8 lines, so two lines is not a layout failure — what survives is reading time, and the two worst were both Actives, which must state a condition *and* an outcome.

**Deviations:** several, in the spec's *As built*. The one that changes a decision: **five code files against a ruling of four.** The fifth is a shared `DictionaryLocalizer` fake, forced by **ten** broken fixtures rather than the six predicted. Five is size M's ceiling so the pre-declared split did not fire, but the ruling was four.

**Learned:** a command cannot build a VContainer container, so boot wiring stays the suite's — Traps §4, already filed. Unity **will not reload the domain while any assembly has a compile error**, so a new type is invisible to a probe until the *test* assemblies compile too — Traps §4. A `Does.Not.Contain` over a whole scene file matches **GameObject names**, not just drawn text — spent (stays here).

**Follow-ups:** **[ledger row 9](ROADMAP.md#carry-forward-into-m3) closes for six readers by name and stays open for the seventh** — `AutoCastRow`'s 24 dp cells, where the gap is an icon and M7's, pinned by `AutoCastRow_IsStillIconless`. **[Row 2] does not fire**: a table is content read at boot, so `PlayerProfile` stays at v2. **[Row 4] gains two**: whether a description reads in under two seconds on a 6-inch screen mid-fight, and whether twelve tree cells still fit a landscape safe area now they hold English. **One finding with no owner: eleven static labels on four prefabs draw unresolved keys and nine still do** — the tree view now shows twelve English nodes under a Close button reading `ui.tree.close`. All eleven have rows, so wiring each is one line.
